Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

5

How Astrology Amplifies Our Understanding of the Energy Interaction Between People

Love cannot be forced, love cannot be coaxed and teased. It comes out of heaven, unasked and unsought.

—Pearl S. Buck

The energy interactions between people are such an immediately perceptible part of life that most of us take such forceful interpersonal reactions and the strength of their impact for granted, without stopping to inquire whether those experiences can be better understood. And even more rarely do we ask whether such powerful interchanges can be clearly represented through some system, science, or language. Throughout the world, comprehensive and profound systems based on the “elements” have been used in healing practices, ranging from Traditional Chinese Medicine to Indian Ayurvedic medicine to the Polarity Therapy system developed by a Chicago osteopathic physician, Dr. Randolph Stone. Therefore, becoming familiar with the four elements (Air, Fire, Water, and Earth) is valuable not only from a relationship and compatibility standpoint, but also for the purpose
of understanding and improving our physical health. When powerful forces have such an impact on our lives, it is a wonder that more people do not seek to identify and understand those energies. And since astrology is the very language of energy that we need for such clarity, it would seem foolhardy to ignore the insight and potential for a better life that it can provide.

The Persian mystic Rumi, whose works have recently become widely known in the Western world for their inspirational beauty and profound spiritual insights, has written: “Love is the astrolabe of God’s mysteries.” I think most of us have had enough of a glimpse into an elevated, intoxicated, or transcendent state of mind and emotions through an intense relationship—even if short-lived—that we can resonate with this quotation. To me it means that, through the experience of love, you can sense or intuit more of God’s mysteries, more of cosmic reality, or more of the universal dance of life that Rumi’s poems constantly express. Through love, Rumi says, you can
experience
 some part of these ultimate realities, but they are not accessible through thinking or analysis! As so many of the greatest writers have shown, you may now and then portray different types of love, but you cannot
explain
 it.

Astrology cannot explain love either, but it can describe some of the energy interactions that are often intense and sometimes profound and seemingly magical. Admittedly, astrology is better at explaining “getting along” with someone, in quite a specific way, than at analyzing the imponderable experience we call “love.” But, not to sell short astrology’s scope of application, in almost all cases I have seen, if two people have experienced an especially strong mutual love, the astrological factors will reflect the intensity and attraction in that relationship and often will show whether it is deep or superficial, and whether it is likely to be enduring. That complex level of analysis is beyond the scope of this book, which is focused on introducing people to the most easily accessible and quickly usable parts of astrology for
understanding relationships. If the methods and ideas I present in this volume prove insightful and accurate, then naturally one might choose to learn even more of this fascinating field.

Before going deeper into how astrology expands our understanding of the energies of life and relationships, I would like to explore a bit further the transcendent aspect of love discussed earlier by referring to some observations made by the well-known Jungian psychiatrist, Eleanor Bertine, MD. She noticed over the years in her practice that many important experiences of love between man and woman have not only a transformative power and profound mystery, but also a powerful
energy
 component that perhaps provides the “fuel” for the intensity of their experience and their possible transformation.

I remember the immense impression made upon me by manmade lightning, shown at Steinmetz Hall during the 1938 World’s Fair in New York. This consisted of millions of volts roaring and crackling across the gap between one electric pole and another.… As I watched in awe, it became the image of the overwhelming emotions which may be constellated between a man and a woman. It pictured for me the incalculable energy of the instincts and the archetypes in violent interaction. This is something quite beyond the small sphere controlled by the human will. It belongs to the drama of the gods or to cosmic forces.… Yet it is just that fate which has compelled many persons to make the climb from psychological childhood to maturity. The energy of intense desire either turns destructive or it heats the alchemical retort for the
magnum opus
. I have seen many a person who once had a spoiled and self-indulgent, indeed quite infantile, attitude toward life develop through such an experience into a decent adult, able to take whatever gambles were required by the relationship and pay the stakes, if necessary. Indeed, one does not expect to command the lightning, but one may reasonably aspire to carry oneself appropriately in a thunderstorm.

It offers them a chance to be lifted out of their banal and limited selves and to participate in the fateful experience of life’s august supra-personal powers—powers which have always been worshipped or placated as gods. (JCT, pp. 154–155)

To translate briefly a few of Dr. Bertine’s terms to the astrological language: the “gods” are the archetypal powers within human life, often represented in many cultures by the planets. And the powerful energy she refers to consists of life forces that manifest in four ways in human life: Air, Fire, Water, and Earth. The poles she refers to not only are male and female, in the sense of two types of bodies, but can also be described as the positive/negative, creative/receptive, expressive/retentive polarities found in life and in astrology. Two of the most significant polarities in regard to relationships are the Sun-Moon and Venus-Mars combinations, as we will explore in some detail.

Energy Interactions: An Experience, Not a Metaphor

The four elements of modern astrology—Air, Earth, Fire, and Water—can of course be considered “mere” symbols, but all languages are composed of symbols. All languages seek to represent meaning and perception, and to enable people to communicate common experiences. Therefore, in the rest of this book I will simply refer to the elements as four definite energies of life, energies that we can experience, interact with, and use in practical ways. I will define each of them systematically in
Chapter 6
 but will introduce the elements from a variety of angles in this chapter.

Let me give some examples of these four elements and their manifestations and interactions, to make these terms come alive for you. Once you become accustomed to identifying these energies and the astrological factors associated with them, you will probably wonder why everyone does not recognize such vital and useful terminology.

The interaction of energy fields between people is so real that you can feel it viscerally. In fact, the complications of these interactions have enabled entire professions and industries to flourish, under the promise of helping people cope with conflicts, poor communication, and sour group dynamics or unpleasant atmosphere: psychological couples counseling, corporate personnel and group communication consulting, business teamwork methods coaching, and others. In some people’s presence, your breathing becomes restricted, your voice weakens, or your eyes hurt. With some people in close proximity, you feel comfortable, elated, or stimulated creatively. With others, you may feel irritated or angry, or you may develop a stiff neck, headache, or just an exhaustion that might be felt as aching in the solar plexus. With some people, you feel hostility far out of proportion to the matters at hand, whereas with others you feel accepted and appreciated, no matter how difficult and stressful some task or problem appears. As one therapist told me as we discussed such interpersonal energetic experiences, “With some people, you just get within ten feet of them, and you feel like you’ve just lost a pint of blood.” In essence, another person’s energy field interacts with yours, and that person’s energies may impact yours in such a way that the functioning and integrity of your own energy field is impeded. In those cases, you can feel not only an effect on your physical energy and health, but also often a sensation of mental and/or emotional confusion.

Particularly if you are a sensitive, receptive sort of person, you will notice that almost anyone will affect you significantly if you are in that person’s presence for very long. One way of portraying this phenomenon is to say that, positively, we feed or encourage each other on an energy level, or, negatively, we restrict or conflict with the other person’s energies. In reality, most relationships are a mixture of such energy interactions, which is what makes them so difficult to understand in many specific cases. For, no matter how much you may “love” someone else, you may not be able to tolerate him or her in some ways.
Or you may find it necessary to get completely away from the other person physically in order to allow your own “batteries” to recharge. I have written in detail in other books about couples who found that they had to sleep in separate bedrooms in order to maintain optimum health. In most cases, they found themselves too drained of energy if they stayed in close physical proximity for so many hours. In a few other cases, however, the mutual energy stimulation proved to be so exciting that sleep was virtually impossible.

I have a friend, a nurse for more than thirty years, who uses astrology to understand coworkers and patients in order to maximize the healing effects of their interactions. One day she commented to me: “You can really see and experience the elements through the body—not only in the patient’s energy but also in the ability or willingness of people to
care for
 others in need. Those with lots of Water are the natural care-givers and healers. They are so sympathetic and soothing. Many with a lack of Water hate to get emotionally involved and are useless in care-giving. One person who was all Earth and Air came to the hospital to see an ill friend, and insensitively accused him, ‘You’re such a lousy patient. You get so angry when you’re sick.’ ”

Visualizing the Elements and Their Interactions

There are a number of ways of visualizing the elements and imagining how they interact with each other. But first, if you do not know the element of at least your Sun sign and preferably also your Moon sign, Venus sign, and Mars sign, please consult the information in
Chapter 6
 and the tables in Appendices C and D to determine which elements you are especially attuned to. If your knowledge of astrology is advanced enough that you also know your rising sign, or Ascendant (always based on the precise time of birth), that sign’s element is also one to which you are dynamically attuned.

If you can take a few minutes to imagine how the four elements
combine (or don’t combine!), it will give you an immediate visual sense of how the elemental
energies
 interact with each other. All the elements are needed to some extent in everyday life, in order to work effectively in all areas of human activity and experience, but they do not always combine well in interpersonal relationships. Traditionally, Air and Fire combine well, since Fire needs Air to give it oxygen and stay alive, and Air gets rather stale without the heat of Fire to get it moving. Likewise, Water and Earth are traditionally considered compatible, primarily because they rest easily in each other, the Water finding containment in the Earth, and the Earth becoming either mud (if the Water is excessive) or fertile soil that can produce abundantly.

Signs of the
same
 element are the most compatible without any effort, since they automatically blend and flow into one another. However, in order to experience other realms of life and other types of reality and consciousness, one must also interact with elements to which one might not be especially attuned. Almost every human being’s birth chart contains a mixture of zodiac sign emphases (based on the location of the planets) that includes at least two elements, and most people are attuned somewhat to three. Some people are attuned to all four elements in a fairly balanced way, but this is relatively rare. However, many people have
no
 attunement to one, or even two, elements, which almost invariably creates certain problems in everyday life, sometimes with a major psychological impact.

In many ways, the most stimulating energy interactions are between Air and Fire, and also between Earth and Water, which are portrayed graphically in the zodiac by the Air signs always being opposite Fire signs, and the Earth signs always being opposite Water signs. In human relationships, two people who have major factors in their birth charts in opposite signs are experiencing one of the polarities of astrology that can be particularly enlivening and stimulating. The opposite signs (Aries/Libra, Taurus/Scorpio, Gemini/Sagittarius, Cancer/Capricorn, Leo/Aquarius, and Virgo/Pisces) are not actually completely
opposite

in temperament or in overall qualities, but rather are more often
complementary
. Some opposite signs may clash to some extent (mainly the “fixed signs” [Taurus/Scorpio, Leo/Aquarius], because of their stubbornness), but generally opposite signs are mutually stimulating and responsive at an energetic level, as well as often mutually attractive.

One is also frequently attracted to a person who has an attunement that one lacks. For example, if I have no planets in Earth signs, I may particularly admire or find intriguing those with Capricorn, Taurus, or Virgo in their charts. That does not mean that I will be
compatible
 with that personality or energy type in a close relationship, but it is a complementary energy that I can learn a lot from and that may indeed
ground
 me in a way that makes for a rewarding relationship on any number of levels. Or, for another example, if you have no planets in Water signs, you may find a Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces especially interesting since that person would not only embody but also easily pour out the sympathy, sensitivity, and ease with emotional expression that you find so difficult or awkward. Especially in close, living-together situations, one should be cautious about a commitment with a person who has no significant attunement to an element that is dominant in one’s own chart. In that case, what is
vital
 for one person may be completely uninteresting or irrelevant to the other. The two people indeed may be complementary, but going on the assumption that one is beneficially supplementing the qualities or resources that the other lacks is quite dangerous and may wind up proving to be an illusion. For, in trying to maintain such a relationship—as many counselors and therapists can confirm—such an innate gap between two people is not so easily bridged once the energy of physical attraction diminishes over time. In fact, the feeling of distance and even alienation may grow.

My other books offer many examples of how the elements and their interactions can be visualized, but here I would like to quote another person’s viewpoint as well, since she makes the elements come alive
in quite an informal but concrete way. The following was written by Jeanetta Era and published in the journal
Mercury Hour:

You can’t hold onto fire, it hurts you if you try. You can’t put it in a container and keep it, for it will either burn up the container or it will suffocate and die. Fire needs air in order to burn, but if it gets too much air, it burns itself out rapidly. Earth gives fuel so fire may continue to burn, but earth itself can smother fire. Water controls fire and keeps it under control, but too much water will drown it.

You can’t hold onto air. Put it in a box, if you want, and keep it there. But remember that when air doesn’t circulate it gets stale and finally becomes unbreathable. Air needs earth’s gravity to hold it down, or it spreads itself too thin and becomes part of the void. Occasionally air needs fire to quicken it and make it rise to greater heights, and water to precipitate out the impurities, keep it fresh, cool it off, and bring it back down closer to earth. But too much gravity holds air down too much, makes it heavy and sluggish, and too much fire consumes air, while too much water makes clouds and obscures the view.

You can hold water, you can use it to enrich your existence, to sustain you, but eventually water will flow on. Water needs the earth to support it, for it is too heavy to roam around free like air. Without earth it would flow continuously. Even so, water will change its course occasionally, or become more than it was and overflow the banks, flooding the earth. Sometimes it is blown onto the land by a strong air current. Water must be aerated to be at its best. Without air it gets heavy and very limited in what kind of life it will promote. Then only algae seems to thrive. But if air becomes too strong, it will ruffle the surface of the water, agitate it and even force it out of its course. Fire makes water boil and purifies it, which water needs occasionally, but if kept in close proximity to fire for too long, water will boil away rapidly, or boil over and put out the fire.

You can hold the earth, but while you hold it, it produces very little. If you put it in a box it eventually becomes sterile and produces nothing. Earth needs air, as soil must be aerated. It needs water to keep it from becoming too hard. Yet too much air can dry out the earth, too strong an air current will blow away the top soil, and too much water makes the earth swampy. However, an occasional fire can be quite beneficial to an overgrown earth. It serves to clear out the underbrush and fertilize the land in preparation for new growth. Mustn’t get too much, though, or it will bake the earth and make it barren.

So the way we try to hold onto each other, in the name of love, is not beneficial to either, sometimes even dangerous. If we choose to share life with another, and they choose to share with us, we can both benefit. By sharing our natures with each other we both grow. The four elements need interaction with each other for the maximum growth and well being of each, though some are needed by others only in small doses, or for short periods of time.

On the other hand, two fires can quickly burn up the space between them and burn as one. Two bodies of water can wash away whatever lies between them and become one, and it is almost always beneficial to mix soils; sand lightens clay, alluvial soil enriches most any other soil, etc., and air currents can float around blissfully together.

Of course even in your own element, you run the risk of a backfire, or clay being mixed with clay, or the thunder and lightning resulting from a cold air current meeting a hot air current, or two huge waves moving in opposite directions meeting in the middle. (January 1980 issue)

It can be accurately said that an individual’s elemental attunement reveals what is
real
 to that person, where that person’s consciousness is focused, and what motivates them. For example, Air signs consider
concepts
 more real than anything else, and this motivates
much of their behavior, especially their need to find other people of like mind. Fire signs live on
inspirations
 and are motivated by
aspirations
, which they feel are more real than anything else, because everyday reality bores them to death. Water signs find
emotions
 and
yearnings
 the most real and motivating factors in life, and they have scant respect for those who live only in their heads or in the material world’s obsession with “objective facts” and outer appearances. And Earth signs find the world of nature and its material
forms
 endlessly fascinating and undeniably
real
, and they are most motivated by practical
needs
 and by what they see as useful.

Another way of describing these profoundly different categories of human personality and energy attunement is to say that the element of any particular sign shows the specific type of consciousness and method of most immediate perception to which the individual is attuned. Air signs are correlated with the mind’s perception and expression, and also with sharing their ideas with others. Fire signs express the warming, radiating, energizing life principle that can manifest as enthusiasm and love, or just as ego. Water signs symbolize the cooling, healing, soothing principle of sensitivity and emotional responsiveness. Earth signs reveal an attunement with the world of physical forms and a practical ability to utilize the material world.

Facing the Facts and the Limits of Therapy

I would be the last person to advocate the view that all psychological therapy and counseling for relationship problems are a waste of time. On the contrary, for many relationships, especially those that have considerable harmony and thus a good chance of working out if some effort is applied, the perspective gained by such a dialogue or group discussion can be invaluable. If such a session also used the insights available through astrology, it could be even more valuable and provide something of a cosmic perspective—a sense of how the different personality types fit into the comprehensive scheme of life
represented, throughout millennia and in hundreds of cultures, by the signs of the zodiac.

However, therapeutic approaches and counseling methods do have their limits, and they cannot achieve the impossible. If two people have such intense incompatibility that the conflicts, hostility, irritation, mistrust, or sense of oppression is perennially overwhelming, often the best thing a counselor can do is to help the people accept the facts of their lives and the reality of their own natures. Such acceptance can sometimes at least overcome some of the bitterness or the tendency to blame the other for the problems. If the counselor also knows astrology, the people can explore an even greater sense of acceptance of certain cosmic realities, thus coming to terms more quickly with their life situation and beginning to heal. And for those relationships that can continue on and develop further, the understanding of each other’s unique nature that astrology can provide encourages
trust
 since we can then see into people more deeply and accept how they really are. Hence, we then do not have to continue harboring fears or imaginary notions about their motives or behavior that are so often obstacles to a relationship’s future growth.

Among the many “cosmic realities” that astrology can clearly reveal are the “trade-offs” that are implicit in relationships when one commits to a certain person. By this I mean that each person’s nature is an integrated whole, which must be accepted and dealt with in its entirety. In other words, all of a sign’s qualities and attributes come as a package! If you want the neatness of Virgo, you will also have to put up with their pickiness and often their criticism. If you want the passion of Scorpio, you will also have to deal with their jealousy and paranoia. If you want the initiative and fearlessness you admire in Aries, you will also have to cope with their selfishness and lack of consideration. One could go through all the signs of the zodiac in this way, but I am pointing this out simply to acknowledge that this is another cosmic fact of life that must be faced in order to have a healthy relationship. It is a sign of maturity about close relationships when we
begin to drop the illusion of expecting, demanding, or longing for a relationship in which all factors are idealistically perfect to suit one’s own personality, psychological quirks, and moods. In short, one of the most valuable things that astrology contributes to our lives by illuminating “cosmic realities” is to define what is right and natural for each individual person. You might say that it is a sort of
cosmic validation of one’s authentic self
, and it thereby helps overcome excessive self-doubt or self-criticism.

Although counselors and therapists rightly emphasize “communication” in relationship counseling, they unfortunately have no way of realizing and understanding how radically different people are in their communication methods and energy attunements. My overall observation is that, if the basic attunement is not there (of empathy, energy harmony, caring, and mutual understanding of each other’s ideas), good communication becomes an uphill battle. Trading words or agreeing to an “insight” that a therapist poses as a solution is not communication. But acknowledging that the other person has a valid but completely different way of thinking and communicating is certainly a worthwhile outcome of any therapy or counseling session—a realization that can perhaps become more clear and certainly occur more rapidly through the use of the astrological language.

There are indeed many levels of communication between people. But each person is capable of receiving and appreciating only certain types of communication with real clarity and without “static.” In fact, a given individual may love you very intensely in her or his own way, but you may be unable to accept or appreciate it—or even to be aware of it. Or you may feel “loved” just because certain kinds of behavior provide the messages that you are readily attuned to and appreciate. But that does not necessarily mean that the other person really has whole-hearted affection for you.

In intimate relationships, as well as in everyday life, when you are really attuned to someone harmoniously, there is no problem with the pace, rhythm, or intensity of your energy flow in relation to the
other person’s. It all flows together naturally, and you’re not secretly criticizing each other, because you sense each other without much “static” or conflict and merge together easily. However, in intimate relationships, the more different you are from your partner, the less attuned to him or her you are, the more you’ll need to reduce the distance between you through creating some special mood, spell, trance, or atmosphere.

For example, everyone knows that in a classic romantic situation, there is usually an attempt to create a special mood or spell. It’s an ideal associated with the planet Neptune that people carefully try to create; and if you successfully create that atmosphere, then you find yourself in a magical place, your own little world, in which your tension and your real, obvious differences are reduced. The less compatible you really are, the more you
need
 to do this, the more you need to cultivate this archetypal illusory state of trance, because it helps the two of you melt together. This Neptunian mood or spell, however—often created by Neptunian substances (drugs or alcohol) or candles or music—is very fragile if it is not based on anything real, which is often the case. If it is not based on real energy flow and authentic communication, then it is self-created out of imagination, desire, and visualization, using images and illusions. This unreal mood can have its fragile structure shattered amazingly fast. All one has to do is say the “wrong thing” or make the “wrong move” for the other person to notice that your words or actions don’t fit the ideal image. The bubble gets punctured; the fragile bowl won’t hold water.

True personal intimacy, close emotional sharing, and comfortable, meaningful sex are for most people very special and longed-for experiences. Therefore, people try to
coax
 these experiences into being by setting up a Neptunian mood, a special atmosphere, but the resulting experience can disappoint if the energy flow is not there or is not especially dynamic. Instantaneous communication through the electromagnetic waves and energy fields that enliven us
can
 spontaneously
occur. We cannot make it happen. So many of people’s problems, including sexual problems, derive from their trying to
force
 a certain energy flow into being. They pressure their partners and themselves into pretending all sorts of things. But such pretense never lasts. Over time in any relationship, the images and illusions eventually shatter, and you start seeing what’s really there, a vision of reality that was shown in the birth charts all the time, but which we were not ready to see.

What Is a Chart Comparison? Assessing Interpersonal Energy Dynamics

Comparing birth charts (or doing a “chart comparison”) is an extremely valuable method of analyzing the flow of energy between two people. To me, it is the ultimate tool available that enables us to open unimagined doors to deep insights into other people and into
all
 types of relationships and interpersonal interactions. In comparing any two charts, one must consider the relative harmony of the planetary positions according to the elements and signs, as well as the specific angular relationships between the two people’s planets. The specifics of those geometric relationships are explained in more detail in
Chapter 17
, but I should mention now that the more
exact
 the angles between two charts’ planets are to 0, 30, 60, 90, 120, 150, and 180 degrees, the more dynamic the energy interactions will be between the two people.

Gaining a comprehensive understanding of chart comparisons in all their intricacies takes a number of years, and there are many books devoted to the subject, such as my own
Relationships & Life Cycles
, which includes step-by-step guidelines and sample forms to use in the analysis. (Some of the other best books on the subject are included in the bibliography in
Appendix F
.) However, just by focusing on some easy-to-determine basic factors, one can begin to explore the potential
of this extremely useful technique, and those fundamental factors—easily accessible to beginners—are what I will emphasize in this book. A chart comparison can be most accurately seen as a graphic portrayal of the interaction of two energy fields, a diagram of where and to what extent the people encourage, challenge, stimulate, block, deplete, or harmonize with each other.

When a blockage exists, there is an experience of either frustration or severe energy depletion, or sometimes both. With a mutual harmonious interchange of energy, there is a feeling of satisfaction, vitalization, and completeness. Naturally, in most relationships, both types of energy exchange will occur; but an overall emphasis will usually predominate. The harmonious exchanges between two individuals will provide resources upon which both people may draw in order to furnish the necessary energy required to deal with other areas of the relationship that are more problematical. If, however, there is a predominance of discordant energy exchange between two people, it is only a matter of time until one or both desire to end the relationship—out of sheer exhaustion if nothing else.

The most compatible energies are considered to be those of the same element but of different signs. The incompatible elements, such as Water with Fire, can cause a depletion of the individuals’ magnetic fields at different levels. The most neutral energy exchanges are considered to be between individuals of the same Sun sign who don’t have enough compatible or stimulating energies of other attunements to create dynamism. They ordinarily do not refuel each other effectively, and they tend to blend and “act as one element.” Hence, those with the Sun in the same sign who don’t have other compatible, energizing attunements often find that a slow starvation of the nervous system can result. It is not necessarily that they are in conflict with each other, but simply that neither person is receiving a slightly different and thus enlivening vibration. Our natural attunement to such energy interactions can be seen in the case of a child who automatically gravitates to
the parent who feeds his or her magnetic field. If both parents’ energies are discordant with the child’s energy pattern, the result is often a child who is delicate and high-strung, and the child’s nature remains a mystery to the parents.

Although the Sun’s element is the primary energy fuel of the individual, the elements of the Moon and Ascendant are also inevitably energized in any person and must be taken into consideration in any chart comparison. Everyone is attuned to some extent to all the elements, but the most dynamic and conscious attunement is indicated by the elements of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant in the birth chart. Particularly the elements of the Sun-Moon polarity are highly energized, and the individual therefore will be most harmonious and responsive with another person who shares some of that same attunement. Although there are many cases of a marriage, for example, wherein the man’s Sun and Moon elements are incompatible with the woman’s Sun and Moon elements, the vast majority of those marriages either don’t last long or endure in spite of one or both partners’ constant frustration, whether or not consciously acknowledged.

The relative importance of the presence or absence of such compatibility factors in comparing two charts depends on the type of relationship being analyzed, the degree of intimacy involved, the closeness of the association, and the amount of time the two spend in each other’s company. A distant business relationship or an occasional friendship would not test the energy compatibility so deeply as a relationship involving living-together lovers, marriage partners, or parent and child. The harmony between Suns and Moons is indicative of a mutual understanding and identification with the other, an immediate awareness of the two people’s oneness. There is in such cases a blending of identity, a mutual vitalization, and a natural encouragement of each person’s essential self-expression. This type of rapport can be indicative of one of the highest forms of love, a type of mutual acceptance and responsiveness that is far more lasting and
more deeply satisfying than relationships based primarily upon the traditional indicators of “love,” such as Venus-Venus, Venus-Mars, Venus-Sun, or Venus-Moon interactions. These traditionally positive or “harmonious” factors in a comparison are always good and quite pleasant and go a long way to smoothing the rough edges of everyday life. But one should avoid a narrow focus when doing chart comparisons if one wants to understand the broader range of compatibility and the deeper types of love.

If one is not attuned strongly to an element, it is virtually impossible to participate fully in that realm of being and that quality of consciousness. In a relationship wherein one person has no attunement to an element that is dominant for the other person, such people often find themselves increasingly distant from each other as they grow, evolve, and individualize in their different ways. The more they grow in awareness of their essential individuality, the more they will become resigned to the fact that they will never be able to share at the level of depth that they might want. The degree of dissatisfaction depends upon each person’s expectations in the relationship, how dependent on the relationship each is for self-fulfillment, and what degree of personal
intensity
 is sought through the involvement. A very impersonal Capricorn, for example, may be satisfied with a marriage (simply because its basic structure is secure and comfortable) that would be ultimately frustrating for a more personally oriented Libra.

As I mentioned earlier, people
are
 often attracted to others who are attuned to an element that they themselves lack. A number of writers on the subject of chart comparisons have stated that such a situation is ideal because the people “complement” one another, each providing something that the other needs. This is one of the often-heard astrological statements that sounds great on paper but often doesn’t work in practice. It is a fact that we are often intrigued by and attracted to others who exemplify qualities or abilities that we aren’t endowed with. However, my experience indicates that it is only rarely that we
can deal with such a person
intimately
 over a long period of time with any degree of contentment. This type of relationship is more likely to be satisfactory if it is seen as a fairly short-term growth-oriented arrangement or a practical business relationship where both diverse personalities may be alternately useful in different ways for a common goal. Some writers have even gone so far as to assert that the ideal relationship exemplifies a perfect balance of the elements between the two people, with one providing, for example, the Fire and Earth, and the other contributing the Water and Air. Again, this
sounds
 better than it works out in experience, for two such people will often be so different that the gap between their particular types of consciousness and experience will grow wider with every year. They usually will find that they are unable to participate fully in or understand each other’s worlds.

Some chart comparison methods proposed in various articles and books are quite radical oversimplifications of this highly complex art, and the astrologer who relies upon such halfway methods cannot hope to understand relationships in any depth. I am not emphasizing the elements’ importance to the exclusion of the more traditional methods of analyzing interchart aspects, but I am simply trying to elucidate the deeper foundations of what is happening in any relationship on the energy level. For instance, although mathematically exact aspects (or energy interchanges) in a chart comparison will always indicate a specific type of energy flow or blockage, two people who are harmoniously attuned according to the elements have a much better chance of constructively meeting the challenges of their more difficult or stressful aspects.

Some Key Factors in Chart Comparison

Let’s now summarize some of the key factors to look at in any comparison of birth charts, beyond the
general
 energy attunement symbolized
by which of the four elements are emphasized in the charts. In other words, what dimensions of life should one especially examine, as portrayed by each person’s major birth chart factors?

Sun and Moon:
 The all-important Sun-Moon polarity was discussed previously. Regarding these dominant factors in any birth chart (called the “lights,” in astrological tradition), I only want to add that, in an intimate relationship, if neither the Sun nor the Moon of one person harmonizes with one of the lights of the other individual, then it will probably be required
for long-term compatibility
 that either the Sun or Moon be harmoniously attuned to the other’s Ascendant.

A short example should clarify what I mean. If you have, for example, a Gemini Sun and an Aries Moon (Air and Fire) and you are in a relationship with someone who has a Taurus Sun and a Pisces Moon (Earth and Water), the odds of the relationship working well long term will be far better if
you
 have an Earth or Water Ascendant or if he or she has an Air or Fire Ascendant.

Moon Harmonies:
 This important area of compatibility analysis is discussed at length in
Chapter 7
. Suffice it to say here that having compatible Moons is of incomparable help for domestic harmony and feeling comfortable with each other in the constant interactions of daily living. A test one can use to reflect on how comfortable you are with someone in a lunar way is to ask yourself the following question: Would you feel at ease and relaxed with the person on a multiday cross-country drive? Or would you feel awkward and strain to find things in common?

The Ascendants:
 It is imperative to determine the accurate Ascendants of both persons for a full chart comparison, as well as to see how the Ascendant of each blends with the planets of the other person’s chart. The Ascendant has much to do with what the older psychology
books called “the temperament,” and two people with temperaments and approaches to life that are particularly clashing will invariably have some conflicts to work out.

Mercury Harmonies:
 Much can be learned by a comparison of two people’s Mercury signs, which reveals how easily they develop mental rapport and share common interests. Contrasting two Mercury placements that are in different elements can help explain different viewpoints and ways of thinking and thus can help us appreciate the other’s mental or intellectual viewpoint. The Mercuries need not be in harmonious elements, so long as some of the other planets blend well with the partner’s Mercury. Some of the most stimulating relationships involve people who have sharply contrasting Mercury attunements. Such different ways of thinking do not have to be a major problem so long as there is much else in common and sufficient mutual respect and goodwill between the people.

Venus and Mars Polarity:
 This vast subject forms the core of this book, so here I will just note that understanding the Venus attunements will reveal much about the people’s tastes, what feels pleasurable in daily life, and individual propensities for emotional giving and receiving. Mars reveals much about the physical energy in general, including sexual energy, and more broadly, one’s
modus operandi
 in all of life—that is, how one goes about trying to get what one wants.

Jupiter and Saturn Polarity:
 Jupiter’s harmonious contacts are particularly encouraging and growth-promoting, indicating broad acceptance and a positive rapport. Jupiter factors can also show to some extent whether there are significant religious or philosophical differences or compatible aspirations in those areas of life. Saturn’s harmonious contacts are stabilizing and can indicate an enduring commitment and a sense of responsibility. Its more stressful interactions
can indicate the same, but with less pleasure, a stronger feeling of obligation, and sometimes even a weighty sense of oppression.

Uranus, Neptune, and Pluto:
 The “outer planets” are beyond the scope of this book but are principally significant insofar as they powerfully transform any of the other astrological factors with which they strongly interact.

Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

6

The Four Elements, the Twelve Signs, and the Rising Sign

Earthly things must be known to be loved; Divine things must be loved to be known.

—Pascal

Since so many other books cover the basic factors and symbolism of astrology, and because so many readers will already know many of the fundamentals, I will present my own synthesized version of astrological basics very concisely in this chapter. However, because the Ascendant (or rising sign) is so rarely explained well, and since the descriptions given in so many sources focus on superficial features and the supposed physical appearance of people who have certain Ascendants, I will elaborate somewhat on each of the rising signs in a key section later in this chapter.

One might say that the four elements are the
energy substance of experience
. Each of the elements manifests in human nature through three modalities (called
cardinal, fixed
, and
mutable
), resulting in the twelve possible energy patterns that we call the signs of the zodiac. The signs indicate specific
qualities of experience
 and give us great understanding of the different types of consciousness and perception that we human beings express as well as an appreciation for the varied types of motivation that we experience.

The four elements in a birth chart reveal the ability to participate in certain realms of being and to tune in to specific fields of life experience. These elements have nothing to do with the elements of chemistry and in fact transcend them completely. The astrological birth chart is drawn for the exact moment of the first breath, that instant when we immediately establish an attunement and a rhythm with the cosmic energy sources. The birth chart therefore reveals our individual energy pattern, our attunement to the four elements.

The four elements—Fire, Earth, Air, and Water—each represent a basic kind of energy and consciousness that operates within everyone. Each person is consciously more aware of, and more sensitive to, some types of energy than others. The element of any sign that is
emphasized
 in a chart (by significant planetary placement in that sign) shows a specific type of consciousness and method of perception to which the individual is strongly attuned.

Fire signs
 express the warming, radiating, energizing life principle that can manifest as enthusiasm, faith, encouragement, and the drive to express self.

Earth signs
 reveal an attunement with the world of physical forms and a practical ability to utilize and improve the material world.

Air signs
 are associated with the mind’s perception and expression, especially in relation to personal interaction and to concepts and abstract ideas.

Water signs
 symbolize the healing principle of sensitivity, feeling response, empathy, and emotional merging with others.

The elements have traditionally been divided into two groups, with
Fire
 and
Air
 being considered active and
self-expressive
, and
Water
 and
Earth
 considered passive, receptive, and
self-containing
. This differentiation is of great importance in a holistic approach to birth charts. These terms refer to
the mode of operation of these energies
 and to the individual’s method of self-expression. For example, the Water and Earth signs are more
self-contained
 than the Fire and Air signs
in that they live more within themselves and don’t allow themselves to project their essential energy outwardly without a good deal of caution and forethought. However, this enables them to build a solid foundation for action. The Fire and Air signs are more
self-expressive
 because they are always “getting it out,” pouring forth their energies and life-substance unreservedly (at times ignoring limits completely): the Fire signs by direct action and the Air signs by social interaction and verbal expression.

This classification of elements, and the fact that the signs of the same element (for example, Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius—all Fire) and the elements in the same group (for example, Taurus and Pisces—Earth and Water) are considered to be generally “compatible,” is of the greatest importance not only in the interpretation of
individual
 charts but also in the art of
chart comparison
, which is used to analyze person-to-person compatibility.

The Fire Signs: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius

The Fire signs express a universal radiant energy, an excitable, enthusiastic energy, which through its light brings color to the world. The Fire signs exemplify high spirits, great faith in themselves, unending strength, and direct honesty.

K
EY
 C
ONCEPT:

Radiant energy, confidence, and initiative

C
HARACTERISTICS AND
 K
EYWORDS:

fearless impulsiveness

high spirits and enthusiasm

motivation to make an impact on life

strength of purpose

direct honesty, even bluntness

outgoing orientation

freedom of expression on many levels

directed willpower and leadership

demonstrative, energetic style

impatient demeanor

intuitive perception into future trends

The Earth Signs: Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn

The Earth signs rely heavily on their senses and on practical reason. Their innate understanding of how the material world functions gives the Earth signs more patience and self-discipline than other signs. The Earth element tends to be cautious, premeditative, rather conventional, and usually dependable. Knowing their niche in the world is especially important to the Earth signs, for security remains a constant goal for them throughout their lives.

The Air Signs: Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius

The Air signs express the life energy that has been associated with breath and the world of archetypal ideas beyond the physical world; in the Air element, the cosmic energy is actualized into specific patterns of thought. The Air signs have the inner need to
detach themselves
 from the immediate experiences of daily life, and thus to gain objectivity, perspective, and a rational approach to everything they do.

The Water Signs: Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces

The Water signs are in touch with their feelings, and in tune with the nuances and subtleties that many others don’t even notice. The Water element represents the realm of
deep
 emotion and feeling responses, ranging from compulsive passions to overwhelming fears to an all-encompassing acceptance and love of creation. The Water signs know
instinctively that, to realize their soul’s deepest yearnings, they must protect themselves from outside influences in order to assure themselves the inner calm necessary for deep reflection, subtlety of perception, and inner tranquility.

Keywords and Ruling Planets for the Sun Signs

Opposite is a list of the signs of the zodiac, with concise keywords that express much about how people of each Sun sign express their core consciousness and where they find their identity, whether consciously or unconsciously. The dates indicate the approximate times that the Sun is in that zodiacal sign
.

Zodiacal Signs and Their Key Concepts

The following grouping of signs is one that I formulated to express not only some key concepts, but especially the psychological qualities of the signs. It includes some precise phrases that can be helpful again and again when trying to understand all the planets’ placements in the signs
.

The Ascendant, or Rising Sign

The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the exact moment of birth is an essential factor in any individual’s complete birth chart. However, in order to determine that sign (otherwise called the “Ascendant” because it is the sign
ascending
 on the horizon at the moment of birth), one must have correct and reliable information about the birth time and place (such as daylight savings time, time zone, and so forth,). In addition, the calculation method must be precise. For this reason, I strongly urge that readers use a reliable, professional calculation service to ascertain their complete birth chart. Using fly-by-night services, fortune-teller pseudo-astrologers, some websites, and most quick-reference tables in books results in perhaps 50 percent of people thinking they have an Ascendant that is not in fact accurate.

Because of the complexity of the calculation, the most we can do in this book is to make the best estimate possible using the tables in
Appendix E
. Be sure to subtract one hour from your birth certificate birth time if you know you were born during daylight savings time. If using those tables gives an Ascendant at the very beginning or end of a sign, it is especially important that you compare the two possible signs’ qualities by using the comments outlined below. In many cases, the stark contrast between the two signs will enable one to make a reasonable judgment about which rising sign is more likely.

I group the rising signs by element, because all the rising signs of one element share a common energy quality and attunement, as well as a somewhat similar approach to life. The element of the Ascendant denotes a spontaneous energy that the person expresses, although it may often be unconscious, especially in youth. That element is a symbol of an attitude toward life that pervades the entire being and personality. (One might accurately say that it is one of the ultimate biases in the person’s point of view on life!) It is often said that the Ascendant is the “image of the personality,” a description that is usually quite
accurate. However, it is an image that is not
intentionally
 projected, but one that is spontaneous and automatic, and that others often see in an immediate and obvious way. The dynamism of the Ascendant is such that I have to express my impressions of the different Ascendants in the following descriptions in an immediate, informal way, based on my experience and not hindered by trying to make each a perfect picture. I am more interested in accuracy than in literary grace.

The Fire and Air signs rising encourage active
self-expression
 (sometimes self-depletion through unchecked outpouring of one’s energies) in the outer world and dynamic expenditure of energy. The Earth and Water signs rising tend to conserve energy and resist self-disclosure, and hence they indicate
self-containment
 (sometimes self-repression) and the tendency to live within oneself.

I emphasize that the Ascendant and its element are crucially important in analyzing close relationships, as will become more apparent later in this book. Hence, for the purpose of understanding person-to-person astrology, it is quite necessary that one eventually determine the Ascendant as precisely as possible.

T
HE
 F
IRE
 S
IGNS
 R
ISING
 (A
RIES
, L
EO, AND
 S
AGITTARIUS
)

Great vitality, physically energetic, beams energy outward into the world. Marked by a positive, optimistic outlook on life and a confident, bluntly honest demeanor. Active, wants to make a mark on life and see the results of one’s efforts manifested in the world. Action-orientation can lead to wasteful excesses and to less awareness of the subtler needs of oneself and others.

Aries:
 Physically restless! Has to keep new projects going at all times, with emphasis on the NEW! Easily bored. Daring, a pioneer in many fields who especially likes to be first to do or to discover something. Easily and quickly grasps essentials of any issue or situation, perhaps more directly and quickly than any other sign, and thus naturally decisive.
An active, dynamic attitude, although very impatient and often abrupt. Everything they are involved in during the day always seems to them to be urgent! Social skills are not only sometimes lacking but are often not particularly valued.

Leo:
 Proud but usually tries to be their best self at all times, thus earning the respect they crave. Dignified, usually with a loyalty and sense of integrity that are becoming rare in modern times. Weakness is rarely admitted, and a sense of lordly authority often comes naturally (usually without the authoritarianism that Capricorn, for example, displays). Hence, more an administrator than a dynamic leader. An open, honest manner with others elicits loyal and affectionate responses.

Sagittarius:
 Perhaps the most “upbeat” personality of them all. Astoundingly buoyant attitude toward life, even displaying persistent optimism ten minutes after experiencing a trauma, disappointment, or major setback. The ultimate positive thinker, with an innate philosophical and/or religious attitude, even if not in a conventional way. Often a grateful person with generous instincts. The overflowing positive energies frequently manifest as wide-ranging inspirational, original, and/or promotional ability.

T
HE
 E
ARTH
 S
IGNS
 R
ISING
 (T
AURUS
, V
IRGO, AND
 C
APRICORN
)

A matter-of-fact outlook. Focus on the material world and conservative attitudes can inhibit imagination, thereby limiting the person’s options and/or restraining spontaneous self-expression. Steadiness and reliability are often well developed and highly valued by self and others. Practicality and inborn patience give more tolerance for routine than other Ascendants. A systematic approach, usually along established channels, is the most common method of expressing oneself.

Taurus:
 Always
slow
 but never to be underestimated because of their determination. They often have an artistic streak, and their infinite patience with the rhythm of life also enables them to be good with people. Imperturbable in a crisis, or a surprising, alarming event, when others often abandon ship under pressure. Absolutely insists on his or her own pace of doing
everything
, often infuriating others. The even temperament is noticeable to all, but occasionally gives way to anger when they cannot have their way or when people insist that they accelerate their work.

Virgo:
 The best detail worker of all, but hates assuming authority to make decisions because of their innate humility and tendency to worry. Chronically high-strung, with a very sensitive nervous system, which can manifest as a fussiness about food and their environment. They really love work, perhaps as the only way they feel they can prove themselves to be valuable. A rare group of people that is actually humble and needs to serve others, without facade or pretense.

Capricorn:
 Old before their years, the impression they convey of solidity, reliability, and respectability often gives them early responsibility, which in fact they often handle quite well,
unless
 they give in to the authoritarian streak in their personalities that instinctively makes them want to dominate others. In spite of the image of total confidence they seek to convey, they worry much more than is apparent. Their sense of
time
 is a key part of their personality, since they usually are either patiently attuned to timing and the unfolding of events, or, more often, very slow, foot-dragging, and overly cautious, upsetting others’ expectations of timely action.

HE
 A
IR
 S
IGNS
 R
ISING
 (G
EMINI
, L
IBRA, AND
 A
QUARIUS
)

Mentally quick and active; inquisitive, social, friendly, verbal. Often clever, with rapid perceptions. Can be overly intellectual to the point of
chronically debating everything without taking action. Wants to
understand
 everything; lives mostly in the world of concepts. Has a natural facility for communication and perceiving others’ viewpoints.

Gemini:
 Immediately friendly and always curious. Communicative, with no limit on the range of interests they will listen to or pursue. Very talkative and/or intellectual. Words, concepts, and new skills endlessly intrigue them. Quick-witted but have trouble controlling their own mind. One part of their brain doesn’t acknowledge what the other part is thinking or doing, leading to contradictory actions that confuse or frustrate others. Almost always clever, but needs direction and discipline to guide daily life.

Libra:
 Sensitive to others’ opinions and to the social and artistic atmosphere and current tastes. Instinctively want to please others. Can be creative, but usually most productive in a partnership or working group, because of their reliance on others for inspiration and confidence. Thinks constantly, weighing all the options, with perhaps too much analysis and not enough action. Very conscious of personal appearance and usually has a refined artistic sense. Kindly and sympathetic in most personal contacts, except when feeling competitive or treated unfairly. Excellent judgment when advising others, but rarely able to think so clearly and decisively about their own situations, especially when alone.

Aquarius:
 Seems socially adept and friendly, but often extremely detached. Very sharp intellectually and “with it” in cultural, social, and professional trends. Comfortable in groups and relatively humanitarian, but often clueless in intimate, emotional situations. Often extreme in attitudes or behavior—sometimes completely spaced-out in their abstract mind and “out of it,” sometimes brilliant, and sometimes surprisingly rigid and conservative, trapped within their own tight conceptual categories.

HE
 W

 S

 R

 (C
ANCER
, S
CORPIO, AND
 P
ISCES
)

Most easily influenced by the environment and by other people. Sensitive, moody, wary because of a strong feeling of vulnerability and the likelihood of being hurt. Protective of self but also of others they care about. Sympathetic, feels others’ emotions immediately and forcefully. Very private, lives deep within oneself. Relies on hunches and intuitions and possesses the greatest healing powers of all rising signs.

Cancer:
 A very sympathetic, but private person who often lives in his or her own world. Easily hurt and never forgetting it, they are always self-protective and cautious in all activities. Some of the most fundamentally decent people on earth, they appear humble, but deep down they demand respect and are tenacious about their objectives, which they rarely divulge—even to themselves! Although you may never get to know them, unless accepted into their small inner “family,” a good mutual rapport can be developed nonverbally through emotional undercurrents.

Scorpio:
 The rising sign with one of the worst reputations but one of the most abundant reservoirs of talent. Very private and intense, this is the sign of emotional extremes, the scope of which the people themselves do not consciously understand, except slowly through in-depth experience and bluntly honest self-analysis. They have a natural inclination toward discovering the hidden, the denied, and the repressed in others, but have trouble dealing with their own suppressed or neglected aspects of self. Good at detective work, research, healing, and dangerous, challenging tasks. If you want to know the most
thorough
 information about something, contact a person with Scorpio attunement!

Pisces:
 Naturally the most empathetic, responsive, intuitive group toward people, animals, the sick, or the spiritually needy. Often artistic or poetic with marked creative imagination, the material world is far too harsh for them. They therefore have to dedicate themselves to an ideal that is more subtle and inspiring than the social or materialistic norm. Often have an otherworldly charisma, although they rarely know it themselves. Even when appearing oversensitive or weak, they can surprise by the inner spiritual strength they express when things are difficult.

A F
EW
 G
UIDELINES FOR
 U
NDERSTANDING THE
 A
SCENDANT

Although the Ascendant is of pervasive importance for each individual, there is no denying that it must be related to the rest of the chart, and especially to the Sun sign, in order to understand it thoroughly for a particular person. The Sun is the
core identity
, the very center of the consciousness, the way we assimilate much of our experience.
Whereas the Ascendant shows the approach to life, the Sun shows consciousness and life itself!

The Ascendant modifies the expression of the solar energy
. An entire book could be written studying the interplay of all the 144 combinations of Sun and Ascendant, but just to give an example, a
Gemini
 rising will always give a more socially lively and intellectually curious approach to life to
any
 Sun sign’s energy expression. It will even speed up a slow Taurus Sun, make a Scorpio Sun more social and less secretive, help a Capricorn Sun be less defensive and more communicative, and encourage a Cancer Sun to be less shy! And yet, in all cases, no matter how similar all these Gemini rising people may seem in approach and observable personality, the central nature symbolized by the Sun remains defined by the Sun’s sign position.

Another useful tool for understanding how a person’s Ascendant and Sun sign interact is to compare the elements of the two factors. For example, a Cancer Sun sign person with a Fire sign rising is usually
more extroverted, forcefully expressive, and confident than a Cancer Sun individual with a more conservative, self-protective Earth or Water sign rising. For another example, an Air Sun sign person with a Water sign rising may appear much more emotional than he or she really is, whereas a Water Sun sign person with an Air sign rising may appear far more detached and less emotional than he or she is on the deepest level of being. Going through these various combinations, either in one’s imagination, by analyzing them systematically on paper, or in discussions or brainstorming sessions with other students of astrology, can be an extremely useful and insightful learning exercise.

Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

7

The Moon: The Moods We Can Live With

We have lost the cosmos. The sun strengthens us no more, neither does the moon.… Now we have to get back to the cosmos, and it can’t be done by a trick. The great range of responses that have fallen dead in us have to come to life again.
It has taken two thousand years to kill them
. Who knows how long it will take to bring them to life? … What we lack is cosmic life, the sun and the moon in us.…

—D. H. Lawrence (
Apocalypse
)

There are thousands of books that focus on the Sun signs, but very few that intelligently and with some depth explain the meaning of the equally important Moon signs. (A significant exception is Donna Cunningham’s
Moon Signs
, which has quite a lot of psychological insight and subtlety.) One reason for this discrepancy is that the Moon symbolizes an aspect of the personality that is more subjective, more unconscious, one so instinctive and automatic that people may have more difficulty identifying with it. Another reason is the more practical one that, whereas it is usually easy to know one’s Sun sign just from the birthdate (see Figure 6.1), it is necessary to have a precise chart calculated to know one’s Moon sign for certain. Until that is possible, one can only estimate the location of the Moon, using a table such as
that included in
Appendix C
, and—if the Moon’s sign is not clear and obvious—attempt to determine which of two signs is the more likely location of the Moon based on the signs’ inherent qualities. The proliferation of “Sun sign” columns in newspapers and magazines (and now on the Internet as well) has also contributed to the overemphasis on the Sun sign and the neglect of the Moon sign, but there are also cultural reasons, which I will discuss in this chapter.

The title of this chapter refers to one of the essential meanings of the Moon—the ever-changing emotional fluctuations that all people go through, some much more than others. These changing moods are similar to the Moon’s light constantly waxing and waning each month. In astrology, among myriad symbolic meanings, the Moon and its sign reveal one’s attunement to constant fluctuations in the environment and one’s capacity for adjustment to inner and outer changes. And one of the essential lessons of self-knowledge that knowing our own Moon attunement can teach us is this: not only must we learn to
accept
 our own natural moods and spontaneous emotional reactions, but we also have to
adapt
 to life (with the help of and sometimes in spite of our own moods) in order to live with ourselves in a state of relative inner tranquility.

In addition, and especially important for the subject of this book, studying the Moon sign of oneself and any person one is closely relating to is tremendously revealing about both individuals’ moods, reactions, and natural emotional tendencies. The compatibility or lack of it that can be determined by comparing the two people’s Moons reliably depicts much about whether the two people are sufficiently emotionally compatible that they can probably live together successfully. The Moon sign also describes the instinctual energies and natural attitudes toward
mating and making a home
 (one might say the domestic instinct), and thus it has a broad impact on how one feels physical and emotional comfort. How one expresses and feels nurturing and caring is partially revealed by one’s Moon and, in that
sense, has a powerful emotional impact on one’s capacity for intimacy and—especially for women—a strong influence on their sexual needs and approach. Comparing two people’s Moon signs therefore shows in a striking and revealing way how well one’s security needs are being met with another person, and whether one feels a sense of familiarity and support.

A comparison of Moon to Moon is by no means the only method of doing such an assessment, but it is undoubtedly one of the most important astrological factors in determining if we can harmonize with another person’s moods in daily life. (A certain amount of emotional harmony is also especially indicated by one person’s Moon being compatible with the other’s Sun, Venus, Jupiter, or Ascendant, for example, even if the Moon signs themselves are not in principle particularly compatible.)

Because the essential meanings of the Moon are outlined in
Chapter 5
 and
Appendix A
, I will not repeat that here. But, before we look at each of the Moon signs, I should point out a couple of key lunar characteristics that should not be overlooked. First of all, the Moon has much to do with non-intellectual, non-rational mental functioning—influencing our thinking far more than many would like to admit. When we say, “I feel that …,” we are speaking from our Moon’s attunement and acknowledging that, although what we are saying is not necessarily provable or logical, we are
comfortable
 with that viewpoint. The Moon likewise reveals much about how at home one is with oneself—how inwardly secure one is. The Moon’s location and element are intimately related to what one needs in order to
feel right about oneself
. In addition, the Moon especially refers to the feelings or instinctive behavior we are most comfortable expressing
in a social situation
.

Secondly, if you are only going to remember one concise key phrase for interpreting the Moon’s sign, it should be
instinctive reaction
, for the Moon sign symbolizes the way we immediately and automatically
respond to many aspects of life and experience. One might therefore elaborate on this idea, for example, by thinking through such questions as: Which Moon signs react actively? (Aries, Sagittarius, Pisces) Which react with immediate analysis? (the Air signs and Virgo) Which are especially deliberative? (Capricorn, Taurus, Scorpio) Which try to hide their emotional reactions? (Cancer, Scorpio, Capricorn) … and so forth using any question one can pose. This is a good exercise to learn quickly about the varied tendencies of each Moon sign, and one that the reader might want to experiment with after reading this chapter.

The Moon, the Feminine, Passivity, and Dependency

Another reason there is lack of good material on the Moon in astrological writings is the cultural bias toward activity and ego-development in the Western world, qualities associated with the Sun. In astrological tradition over endless centuries, the Moon was regarded as the feminine archetype (even as a goddess) in the Sun-Moon polarity. It ruled over family, home, the womb, childbirth, and all expressions of nurturing and healing. Typically, in the modern world, women still more easily identify with their Moon signs than do men, and it may be the case that the majority of men in the Western world cannot readily identify with their Moon sign qualities. In fact, it seems unnatural to many men to be asked to express passivity, dependence, or needs that may be interpreted as weakness (such as asking for assistance). The Sun, after all, simply shines of its own innate power and grandeur. It is the Moon that
reflects
 the solar light, rhythmically distributing greater or lesser amounts of light toward the earth in its predictable monthly cycle. These are powerful symbols, which can reveal a great deal, but one observation we can draw without controversy is that the Moon’s distributed light is much gentler, more soothing and cooling, and more emotionally inspiring than the “pitiless gaze” of the Sun that the poet Yeats described. The Sun’s light and power, without modulation
or periodic diminished intensity, simply burns up all vegetation, evaporates the water, and makes the earth a desert.

We certainly cannot explore extensively here the issues related to the alienation of so many men from their feminine side and the social and psychological conflicts it creates. After all, entire libraries could be filled with the books and articles related to those issues! I will therefore restrict myself to a few key points, many of which are clearly described in Herb Goldberg’s book,
The Hazards of Being Male
. He points out that the inability to tolerate passivity destroys the naturally alternating life rhythm of activity followed by passivity (the essence of the Moon!). And he says,

This is undoubtedly one major cause of many men being burnt out or being afflicted by chronic diseases at an early age. The repression of the passive dimension prevents the regularly needed time for rest and recovery. (HBM, p. 47)

This explains in part why the Moon is connected with healing and gives us an insight into how understanding the Moon in our own birth chart can aid our developing a healthy lifestyle and a respect for the body’s periodic need to rest and recuperate. The typical male reticence to ask for help, for example, derives from the male resistance to any show of dependency. The more we understand the Moon’s role, the more we can gain insight into how the lunar side of one’s nature can tremendously support one’s physical and emotional health. In fact, since nowadays so many women are in the workforce and often emulating the worst habits and unbalanced lifestyles of traditional Western males, many of the above comments now apply to them as well, including the “burnout” syndrome and other stress disorders.

Many men project most of their lunar qualities onto their female partner, seemingly assuming that all aspects of the archetypal feminine are then taken care of permanently. But, as Goldberg explains, the results of this can be not only unhealthy but also disastrous:

Many adult men, once they have established a primary relationship with a woman, begin to abandon almost all of their other relationships. The dependency becomes increasingly intense and the crisis, if and when she does leave him, is often life-shattering.… With all their eggs in one emotional basket some men have no wells of nourishment from which to draw.… The male has isolated and alienated himself from other men supposedly to bring his woman the spoils of competitive victory. Now his woman also is proclaiming herself his competitor, even potentially his enemy as she harangues him with epithets of “male chauvinist pig.” Not only has he lost his earth mother, but in his fear and confusion he is scrambling to be liberated, not necessarily for his own benefit, but rather to please
her
. (HBM, pp. 13 and 16)

What is often not mentioned in the literature on this male dilemma is that many women
encourage
 such dependency by either subtly or overtly influencing the man to cut off his old friendships—not just with women friends but with male friends also. A number of therapist colleagues have confirmed what I have observed many times: this is an extremely destructive behavior on the part of the women, which slowly erodes the man’s independence and self-awareness. Often the motivation seems to be the desire on the part of the woman to dominate the man’s psychological and emotional life so completely that he could not even entertain the fantasy of ever doing without her; therefore, he is increasingly likely to agree to anything she wants. And yet, if she succeeds, one wonders whether she will still respect him in the same way she did before she influenced him to become so submissive. (In all fairness, it should be noted that men often try to isolate their woman partner also, but perhaps from different motives and with different implications.) So, from the man’s point of view, getting in touch with the Moon energy and qualities will actually make him
stronger in the long run, and also much less vulnerable to various types of emotional domination or manipulation.

When one becomes familiar with such lunar terms as the following, one may rightly assume that the Moon also has far more impact on erotic, romantic, and sexual attunement for women than for men:
intimacy, tenderness, closeness, responsiveness, emotional receptivity
, and
caring attention
. However, a man with a strong lunar nature (especially one with a prominent Moon or other significant placements in the Moon’s sign Cancer) will have many of these feeling-needs in close relationships and in sexual interactions as well. This is also true for many males who have a major emphasis in the other two Water signs, Pisces and Scorpio.

Please note that in the remainder of this chapter I will quote now and then from interviews and questionnaires I did over a number of years. These will be coded with AI (Author Interview) or AQ (Author Questionnaire), and I trust the reader will find such vital and personal insights sufficiently engaging that he or she will not mind the informality of my inserting them with complete freedom whenever they add something unique to the subject at hand. Any quotations from other sources will be identified briefly or by the abbreviations listed in the bibliography in
Appendix F
. I keep long citations of sources to a minimum because this is not an academic or professional scientific work that needs such cumbersome references. I also use concise impressions wherever possible to convey the essential qualities of each Moon sign so that readers will immediately recognize those qualities in themselves and others—and also so they will be easy to remember. I therefore take the liberty to intersperse any descriptions, phrase, or keywords that seem to be particularly striking and accurate.

The Moon in the Fire Signs

When the Moon is in Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius, individuals tend to react to experiences with enthusiasm, and usually a positive approach and direct action. They in fact may tend to leap before they look, and they are not known for a lack of decisiveness. They feel especially at home when they can initiate or create something new and/or stimulate others toward some definite action or belief. They can be very effective at promoting ideas, ideals, products, or services since they can automatically inspire confidence in others. This is a reason why they need to adhere closely to ethical standards in all their undertakings, so that their natural credibility is not misused.

Although the Fire Moon signs are generally some of the most positive lunar placements, and although their direct and highly energized attitude can motivate and inspire others, the apparent display of confidence should not deceive one into underestimating the despondent, self-doubting moods that they may privately endure. (This is especially true for Sagittarius and Leo Moons, who, in my experience, need to be noticed and validated by others, unlike the loner Aries, who does not need to put on a show of bravado since they would rather just be left alone and rarely attribute any setbacks to their own deficiencies.) The encouraging, inspiring qualities that these three signs so often express can, however, sometimes degenerate into a self-serving and habitual feeling of superiority, which—needless to say—can eventually elicit hostility or resentment from other people.

Aries Moon:
 Probably the simplest, most bluntly honest and emotionally direct of all the Moon signs, they tend to be single-minded when involved in their latest enthusiasm, not liking to be bothered (least of all by “emotional” distractions or someone else’s “personal needs”). Most of them will have thousands of such interests in their lives, each
of which takes on a feeling of pressing importance and tremendous urgency … until they lose interest when the source of excitement is no longer new. Very action-oriented and thus often a bit pushy, they frequently react impatiently, even when they are not nearly so annoyed or irritated as others may assume from their explosive behavior.
