Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

8

Mars and Venus: Not All Men Are from Mars and Not All Women Are from Venus

The feminine spirit inclines towards completeness but not perfection, and the masculine spirit inclines towards perfection but not completeness.

—Peter Redgrove, Poet/Writer

In traditional astrology, Venus and Mars are usually treated as a pair and together symbolize the broad range and variety of romantic feelings, erotic desires, aesthetic tastes, and sexual attraction that constitute such an important part of our intimate relationships. In fact, there have been dozens of books that focus
only
 on Venus and Mars as the keys to understanding individuals’ widely varied love, sexual, and romantic needs and modes of expression. Some of these books are quite good in their limited way, but their narrow approach (neglecting such dominant influences on personality and energy attunement as the Sun, Moon, and other important factors) should be recognized as a significant oversimplification of any attempt to understand close relationships and their complex interpersonal dynamics.

Venus and Mars are widely recognized throughout Western culture as archetypal symbols of male and female, for reasons that will be explored in this chapter. The tremendous popularity of the book
Men Are from Mars, Women Are from Venus
 and of the many later books in that format by psychologist John Gray, together with the enthusiastic reception of similar concepts borrowed, copied, or alluded to in hundreds of other books, courses, and media of different types, shows that a majority of people today, whether or not they “believe” in astrology, still instinctively and emotionally identify with the traditional roles of Venus (affectionate and receptive) or Mars (competitive and assertive), even if they intellectually might prefer to deny it.

I should emphasize, however, that meaningful understanding of people’s dynamic energy and emotional attunements is not limited only to intimate or romantic relationships. A comparison of the Venus and Mars positions in two people’s charts can also be tremendously useful in revealing how they get along in almost any type of close proximity or continued interaction. For example, business partners will do especially well if their Mars positions are in harmony; an artist will have a far better relationship with an agent or gallery owner if their Venus positions harmonize; and one’s financial dealings are more likely to be characterized by trust and prosperity if the two people’s Venus positions work well together. In this book, however, I will primarily focus on more intimate relationships, since that is a central concern of most people who see astrological consultants or who look for clarification in books on the astrology of compatibility. I will concern myself with exploring the archetypal energies of Venus and Mars and their most common modes of expression as I have observed them and experienced them with clients, and as most counselors of all types regularly confront them. Social roles are in such flux today in the Western world that trying to generalize about erotic or love relationships is more difficult now than ever, but I shall attempt to explore the deeper, essential, ubiquitous human needs that are encountered
broadly in one’s personal life and also in many forms of counseling or healing work. I certainly cannot cover the entire spectrum of human relationship or erotic behavior, nor will I refer to the endless types of aberrations or extreme expressions of these fundamental human energies.

Too often human beings tend to assume that other people are really like they are, deep down, if they would only reveal their true feelings. A study of even a dozen people you know, seen through the lens of astrology, will quickly disillusion anyone who believes this. Over the years I personally have adopted a guiding philosophical motto, which I repeat so often that most people who know me well are familiar with my favorite saying in Latin:
De gustibus non est disputandem
. This literally means: Of tastes, there is no disputing, or “There’s no accounting for taste.” More than any other factor in astrology, Venus reveals one’s
tastes
—be they aesthetic, romantic, or sensual—as well as the resulting modes of sharing one’s finer feelings and affections. Mars, I should add, also symbolizes a component of one’s erotic tastes, specifically one’s sexual tastes and the quality of energy that motivates sexual desire. Together, Venus and Mars represent the energy attunement of one’s erotic nature and each individual’s more intense personal and passionate feelings. Whereas Venus shows most specifically the relationship needs and mode of sharing (giving
and
 receiving) with others, Mars reveals the energy level of the sex drive and the
modus operandi
 of going after what or whom one desires, as well as how the sexual energy is most naturally expressed.

Attractiveness (and particularly an especially strong magnetic attraction) between people on the erotic and intimate level, however, usually involves both Venus and Mars factors. A man will usually want to find a woman pretty, pleasing, and with some interpersonal charm (Venus) as well as sexy, energizing, and stimulating (Mars). A survey of women who placed ads looking for new relationships likewise
found that both Venus and Mars factors were desired. Mars qualities such as physical appearance, athleticism, and—above all—financial success were mentioned at least as much as Venus characteristics such as kindness, empathy, liking music or dance, and so on. However, I cannot emphasize too strongly that the type of relationship and/or compatibility one wants is entirely dependent on one’s own unique birth chart. Some people are almost entirely “Martian” and lack significant Venus qualities. Others may be extremely nice, sensitive to others, artistic, and aesthetically refined (a strong Venus attunement) while perhaps completely lacking self-assertion, a strong desire for sex, or interest in vigorous activity of any kind (Mars). And then other folks, for example, may be so Mercurian (with major emphasis on the Mercury signs Gemini and Virgo and on the Air element) that, while they may require a variety of friends to talk to, they may have little need for an erotic life or a physically intimate partnership. So, when applying the descriptions in this book, one needs to keep in mind the essential nature and needs of the people involved, without making any assumptions or judgments in advance.

Considered together, Venus and Mars ideally link your personal emotional needs and ideals about love (Venus) with your sexuality (Mars); sexual expression then combines impersonal instinct with interpersonal sharing, social bonding, and intimate commitment. Being able to express both Venus and Mars in our lives in a relatively spontaneous, honest, nonstressful way is an ideal, however, and the details of the birth chart (as well as the volatile experiences of our personal lives) clearly show that for most of us, attaining that ideal does not always come easily or naturally. As Stanley Keleman wrote regarding the importance of erotic activity as a way of expressing our personal history and emotions, and more fully owning our identity,

But if you are unmotivated by your gender, or if you lack the ability to share your private experience, to mingle your emotions and
to be affected by your passions, if you are unable to transmit caring [Venus] and passion [Mars], tenderness [Venus] and intensity [Mars], you are less than you can be. (IDH, p. 17) [Bracketed planets inserted by SA.]

Some of the potential inhibitions that Keleman mentions above may be expressions of the placement of our Venus and/or Mars, and the birth chart will quickly show that and sometimes also provide some guidance in how to work with it. Astrology is not in itself a therapy, but by providing an accurate picture of our true nature and needs, it does lend a potentially healing power to our lives and even our loves.

Another way of clarifying the psychological and emotional functions of Venus and Mars is to acknowledge that, although both relate in some of their modes of expression to
desire
 in interpersonal relationships, each planet operates in a different and complementary way. Whereas Venus attracts others through beauty, kindness, charm, and pleasant social skills (that is, the archetypal feminine approach in most cultures), Mars goes after what it wants more directly and aggressively, or—even if somewhat indirectly—through demonstrations of power, strength, or effectiveness (the archetypal masculine approach). Of course, every man and every woman has both Venus and Mars in his or her chart, and—although their ease with expressing both energies varies tremendously among individuals and cultures—we might set out as an ideal that people are most emotionally content with life if they have accommodated the expression of both energies and needs. This brings us to the issue of the relationship of Venus and Mars, a phenomenon that can be examined both within a single individual and between two persons.

The harmony of Venus and Mars on an
individual
 level might be called
personal emotional and erotic integration
, and this can be analyzed in quite remarkable detail in that person’s birth chart. In contrast,
the harmony (or lack of it) between one’s own Venus and Mars energies and the Venus and Mars attunements of
another person
 might be described as
interpersonal emotional/erotic energy interchange
, and this can be analyzed in detail by doing a comparison of the two people’s birth charts. The relative harmony between Venus and Mars on an individual level depends upon many factors, including the harmony of their placements in the elements, as well as their angular relationships to each other and to other planets (a more advanced type of analysis introduced in
Chapter 17
 on the “aspects”). Guidelines for understanding how Venus and Mars energies in one’s own chart interact with those of another person are found throughout this book, since that is of course a key theme of this entire work. And because this book is especially designed for use by relative (or complete) beginners in astrology, I have tried to provide enough information for an accurate and useful interpretation without bogging the reader down in overly technical methods or complicated theories. In any case, if one can get a feeling for the energy reality of the
basic
 factors in astrology, all the other details assume an appropriate secondary level of importance.

Psychotherapist Stanley Keleman, without any reference to astrology, came up with a phrase that, on the intimate and erotic level, nicely includes both Mars and Venus modes of expression; he calls them “patterns of assertion and pleasure.” He writes: “Adult maturity is the ability to engage in emotional and instinctual behavior with high degrees of personal intimacy. Adult roles demand mutuality [Venus] and a recognition of each person’s patterns of assertion [Mars] and pleasure [Venus]” (IDH, p. 64). [Bracketed planets inserted by SA.]

How does that theory and terminology translate into understanding one’s own personal experience in relationships? In a potentially close relationship where the two people are experimenting and reacting to the give-and-take of their energy interaction, incompatible Venus and Mars factors between them may manifest as an inability to recognize, accept, and enjoy the other person’s needs, identity,
erotic energy, style of self-assertion, and mode of self-revelation. In an extreme case of incompatibility, one person will find it difficult to validate or even appreciate the other person’s emotional risk-taking or expression of affection; and thus no easy, pleasant, healing merging of energies will take place. A somewhat humorous example of this is a case where a woman with Mars in Libra (a Venus sign; she thus asserts herself very romantically) was going out with a man who had almost no Venus attunement and Venus in Aries (a Mars sign, showing that his natural tendency when expressing affection was to be rather blunt and unromantic). When telling me about this experience, he said that she was frustrated with him because she felt that he didn’t “ask her enough romantic questions.” He looked at me with a confounded expression and said with Aries bluntness, “I didn’t know what the fuck she was talking about.”

On the other hand, compatible Venus and Mars factors between two people will usually be felt quite quickly, even viscerally if they are very strongly aligned (that is, attuned to each other). In these types of interactions, at best there can be an instant mutual recognition of each other’s emotional nature and orientation, and a spontaneous (even if unspoken) acknowledgment and validation of what the other person is feeling or expressing. There is an implicit acceptance and—with an especially strong harmonious Venus attunement—an enjoyable expression of appreciation for the other person’s demonstrated identity, tastes, and attitudes, as well as a natural encouragement of the other’s self-revelation due to their relaxed mood of mutual acceptance. In cases where the “vibes” are even mildly harmonious, there can be instantaneous friendliness and liking for the other person. In especially powerful experiences, there can be a feeling of identification, merging on an emotional and energy level, and sometimes a powerful erotic attraction since there is, after all, already a mingling of energies beyond personal control, a merging happening spontaneously on some subtle levels.

At this point, let us summarize some of the basic meanings of Venus and Mars:

Venus:
 Its position (especially its sign) reveals much about one’s tastes and values, and how one shares with others—one’s mode of giving and receiving energy and affection, both socially and also intimately. Venus’ placement shows a great deal about one’s need to feel close to another person, and how one feels emotional comfort and harmony and togetherness. The details of its placement are remarkably revealing of how capable one is of relating on personal, intimate levels and, in fact, how social one is in general, as well as how much one really needs close personal relationships. (For example, Venus in Scorpio may be so antisocial that opportunities to express their passionate feelings are limited; Venus in Aquarius may be social but so impersonal that intimacy and emotional expression are difficult; Venus in Gemini and Pisces may be indiscriminately social with almost everyone, and so on.)

Venus shows by its sign position the energy quality and mode (or style) of interpersonal emotional expression, as well as one’s attitude toward love, close relationships, and emotional openness and sharing. It shows what is especially pleasurable to you; consequently, it reveals much about what is
fun
 for you in life. (For example, Scorpio Venus may have fun only if the experience is intense; Aries Venus only if it is new or a challenge; for Aquarius Venus, big groups and political or social change movements and revolutionary ideas are especially fun; and so forth.)

Mars:
 Its position reveals much about one’s physical energy, sexual drive, and will toward action and achievement. It clarifies how much initiative and decisiveness one has, and its placement illuminates how one expresses one’s aggressive and self-assertive urge (one’s self-assertion
style
, one might say). One’s Mars indicates how you go after what you want—your methods of operation in the material world (or
modus operandi
). In short, it reveals a great deal about one’s capacity for effective action and taking charge of one’s life direction. One’s needs for energetic physical activity of all kinds and sexual excitement in particular are also symbolized by the Mars placement.

Mars shows by its sign position the energy quality and mode of sexual expression, and one’s reservoir and focus of passion in life. After all, rarely does one accomplish much in life without
passion
, and this is by no means to be understood only sexually. Mars’ placement, especially its sign, also symbolizes with remarkable accuracy one’s
attitude toward sex
. And it shows the individual’s innate vitality (along with the Sun) and self-healing capacity, as well as one’s energy reserves needed to meet all varieties of challenges or to succeed in the most demanding struggles in life.

Additional dimensions of how Venus and Mars are expressed will come alive in the following sections as I expand the scope of their descriptions and provide detailed examples.

Venus and Charm, Mars and Anger

I mentioned above how Venus is related to social activity and one’s need for interpersonal interaction. In traditional astrology Venus has always been associated with beauty, pleasing relations with others, and also money. (Some studies have clearly shown a correlation between good looks and higher income!) And along with this trio of cosmic gifts is personal charm that makes other people like you and find you attractive. When one is attuned to Venus harmoniously, one automatically elicits more positive reactions from other people. To put it concisely, Venus in a birth chart reveals the social instinct and symbolizes how people use or persuade others, make themselves likable, and charm others to attract benefits. Let’s briefly characterize the twelve types of Venus charm according to the sign positions.

Aries Venus:
 Uses quick wit and directness to charm. Likes to stir things up through impulsive, outrageous actions or humor. Can lack depth of affection or sustained interest in one relationship.

Taurus Venus:
 Charms through easy laughter and natural social instinct, being practically supportive and making life easier for others. Sometimes considers people as objects to own and show off.

Gemini Venus:
 Charms through clever, witty, vivacious friendliness. Quick to establish connections with others and enjoys superficial banter, but can lack depth of affection. Curious about anyone, but for a limited time.

Cancer Venus:
 Charms others by sensitivity and sentimental caring, which is often sincere but sometimes not. Often treats others as “family,” but then expects total devotion. Empathy is usually instinctive, but they can play on others’ sympathies to get what they want. Can be very cheap.

Leo Venus:
 Charms through gregarious, smiling, extroverted social nature. Can encourage others through affection or saying how proud they are of them. Others are captured by their uplifting, enthusiastic expression of noble aims.

Virgo Venus:
 Although not always particularly social due to modesty and self-consciousness, Venus in Virgo charms through humble, reliable helpfulness. Wants to be liked by demonstrating reasonableness, and easily allows others to shine.

Libra Venus:
 Charms through their politeness, consideration, and expressions of fair-mindedness. They give others lots of space and are great listeners. Accommodating with a strong urge to please, they function best in one-to-one situations.

Scorpio Venus:
 Charms and shows affection through loyalty, thoroughness, self-sacrifice, or an unwavering dedication to a mission. Intrigues others through eroticism or charisma, but prefers private exchanges since they are quite asocial, if not antisocial. Can be very ungiving socially and financially.

Sagittarius Venus:
 Charms through humor, playfulness, or expounding their “truth” or ideals. Easily bored in social situations unless actively talking, inspiring others, or being adventurous.

Capricorn Venus:
 Charms through dry humor and demonstrating reliability and respect for others. Social exchange and expression of feeling are not spontaneous, since their reputation and self-control are always dominant. Can use others for security and furthering their ambitions.

Aquarius Venus:
 Charms by being a good friend—reliable, trustworthy, and supportive while giving the other person lots of freedom. Can be very loyal while also being aloof, which can hide the affections. Unconventional and rebellious social instincts.

Pisces Venus:
 Charms by giving away everything anyone needs. Wants to be liked for selfless generosity, caring, and helpfulness. Quite social but yet very private.

An important point to make about the Venus mode of giving and receiving with others, however, is that not all kinds of apparent “giving” are truly an expression of sincere understanding of and interest in the other person. A very common problematic expression of Venus is insisting on trying to please another person, but only on one’s own terms, and expecting to be appreciated for it. Objectively understanding and adapting to the other person’s tastes and preferences are far more difficult but also result in greater mutual harmony.

With the association of Venus to beauty, making oneself attractive, and expressing consideration and kindness to others, it is obvious why Venus is especially associated with women. Naturally, some men are quite attuned to Venus and feel comfortable expressing Venus qualities, and Western society has become somewhat more encouraging of this broader self-expression by men in recent years. However, the charming, appealing ways of Venus can be only a superficial display without much true emotional depth. And in particular, in many men (especially in the Western world), the Venus function atrophies to the point where genuine loving affection on an intimate level is never seen. As Jung pointed out decades ago, this function in men often degenerates into mere sentimentality.

On the other hand, Mars is traditionally associated with the male principle, and the majority of men identify far more with such Mars qualities as strength, bravery, conquest of obstacles, winning over competitors, and proving oneself than with Venus qualities. To understand Mars, one really has to acknowledge that the ancient God of War does in part represent raw, brute power and impersonal aggressive force. If Mars is disciplined and channeled, it can accomplish great things, but if undisciplined it can manifest as random violence, an abrasive approach to other people and to sex, and uncontrolled anger. It is important to strike a balance in one’s attempt to control the Mars energy, and this is of course true for women as well as for men. With no control, one can unleash tremendous destruction and harbor selfish resentment and even hatred. But too much suppression of the aggressive instincts, or of the personal aims or ambitions, results in frustrated desire, chronic anger, sexual inhibitions, and all sorts of emotional blockages.

It is revealing to watch how the Mars energy expression in Western women has changed in recent decades, as women have been increasingly encouraged to assert themselves, be more competitive, get in touch with their sexuality, and express themselves more honestly—but also more selfishly with less restraint and sensitivity to others. We cannot conclude that a balanced expression has been achieved by
many women at this time, for when the lid was taken off and the Mars energy allowed to come out, not only creative, athletic, professional, and leadership talents became apparent, but also a large torrent of rage, resentment, and destructive emotions of all kinds. Let’s now examine how anger might be expressed, by both men and women, according to the position of Mars.

An especially intriguing group research project a few years ago investigated Mars’ association with different ways of expressing anger, with the people grouped according to the four elements of their Mars sign. Those with
Mars in Air signs
 rationalized their anger or philosophized about it. Generally, they felt that they were not quick to anger, but when they did express anger or anything remotely violent, they often felt very depressed about it and often simply “hated” the person who caused them to display such uncivilized behavior. In general, there was an avoidance of incendiary activity, as well as a relatively low level of interest in athletics (this was, however, a small group, and many pro athletes do have Mars in Air signs). But all in all, they were very much “head people,” who preferred to think out rather than act out their problems. I personally have found that an assault on their intelligence, more than anything else, can provoke quick anger, a competitive attempt to prove their mental power, and, at times, considerable resentment.

Mars in Gemini:
 Anger in these folks can manifest as argumentativeness, pickiness, or nasty slights; but in general this is not a sign known for displays of hostility or aggressive intensity. They would rather forget the irritation and put their energies into their next new interest, perhaps thinking that the one who irritated them simply doesn’t know how to communicate.

Mars in Libra:
 In the ultimate pacifist sign Mars rarely demonstrates real uncontrolled emotional destructiveness, at least in the presence of anyone other than their best partner. They often hold strong opinions
and may resent others forcing them to argue about them, but—all in all—these folks would rather avoid people, situations, or environments that make them feel resentful or angry.

Mars in Aquarius:
 Attacks on their political, social, or idealistic concepts are most apt to provoke an angry reaction, although these folks will try to maintain control as long as possible, especially because they pride themselves on their rational and objective approach to life. Basically, challenging them in an area in which they feel that
they know
 for sure may bring about some ego-centered expression of irritation.

Those with
Mars in Earth signs
 found that anger made them tired. They did not enjoy expressing anger whatsoever, and suppressing it evidently took a lot of energy. They had a tendency to hold onto anger and grudges and to cut off people who provoked them to such “negative energy.” In general, they preferred not to be disturbed by others’ behavior that provoked anger. Most did better under pressure and deadlines as far as energy efficiency and channeling anger were concerned. In other words, the group was evaluated to be a “veritable bunch of potential workaholics” who quite spontaneously sublimated anger into productivity.

Mars in Taurus:
 Since I am writing this in Spain, I cannot help but use the image of the slow-to-anger bull in the traditional blood sport of Spain. He meanders about, would prefer to be left in peace, but eventually—if continually prodded and tormented—he paws the ground gathering his strength and determination, and then charges with tremendous power. Because Taurus is a Venus sign, those with this placement will generally prefer tranquility to fighting, but they are nevertheless stubbornly fixed on their desires.

Mars in Virgo:
 Anger upsets them physically, especially the nerves and intestines, and they often direct irritation upon themselves since
they almost instinctively assume that they are so imperfect as to be likely to be at fault. But if the anger is directed toward another person, it can be quite vindictive and harshly critical of the other person.

Mars in Capricorn:
 Controlled anger is the keynote here, and rarely does Capricorn Mars really lose control of hostile feelings, at least not in public! But these people are very self-directed and goal oriented, and they will patiently evaluate what may be a productive channel for that emotion, sometimes leading to a power play or authoritarian leveling of an enemy or competitor.

The group with
Mars in Water signs
 generally had some difficulty expressing their anger, especially since this group was composed of idealistic and mediator types who were called by the group leader “aggressive pacifists.” Some tended toward tears when frustrated, and all felt guilt after letting anger explode outwardly. Overall, I have found that this group harbors undercurrents of anger that are allowed to build up over time, sometimes to the point where the anger is affecting judgment or behavior without the person consciously being aware of its intensity.

Mars in Cancer:
 They will hide the anger as long as possible, but it seeps out into the atmosphere, and they will try to avoid you if you provoked it in them. Their digestion reacts horribly to anger, and they may not be able to eat at all.

Mars in Scorpio:
 When angry, they completely clam up, brood forebodingly, and seethe until steam is visibly coming out the ears! Then in some people and situations, a huge explosion ensues wherein the rage expressed can be very destructive and completely irrational, and hard to forgive. These folks take decades to forgive a betrayal or unjust personal attack.

Mars in Pisces:
 Not usually as quick to anger as most signs, perhaps because they have less ego and also because they often don’t notice an offense or take it so personally as others would. But when they do feel anger, it is very difficult for them to express it because they simultaneously sympathize with the one who caused the offense.

Those with
Mars in Fire signs
 seem to be the only group that sometimes actually enjoys expressing anger. There is almost always a certain impatience and often a tendency toward quick temper. Those in this group all tended toward explosive behavior at times and judged themselves to have very high expectations of themselves and others, which of course can lead to irritation if these are not met. All in this group were specialists in some creative field, but each had individual ways of dealing with anger: for example, heavy drinking to drown it (a woman); breaking inanimate objects at one point in life (a man); and problematic explosive aggressiveness (a man).

Mars in Aries:
 They seem to be angrier than they are, because they are simply always impatient and have to blow off steam, which others take too seriously and which they themselves forget two minutes later.

Mars in Leo:
 Anger can stem from hurt pride, so don’t ever question their loyalty or integrity, or fail to pay enough attention to their demonstrations of generosity. They often demand attention, full credit for their actions, and/or substantial respect and can be extremely irritated if it’s not forthcoming.

Mars in Sagittarius:
 In angry responses, they often project an aura of self-righteousness and/or righteous indignation. They may never communicate with you again if you demean their high ideals, offend their exalted opinion of themselves and their beliefs, or point out their failures to live up to them themselves.

The Contrasts Between Men’s and Women’s Expression of Venus and Mars

Although it is more difficult than ever to describe “typical” male and female expressions of psychological factors and emotional/physical energies, due to social behavior and roles being in great flux today, one can never arrive at any overall principles without some amount of generalization. I will no doubt offend the politically correct by venturing some guidelines for understanding the differences between how men and women often express and describe their intimate life, not only as I have observed it and learned about it from the psychological literature, but also as derived from hundreds of interviews and questionnaires.

It should first be emphasized that Venus and the Moon are also closely interwoven with Mars in a woman’s pattern of sexual expression—far more so than in most men, and it cannot be denied that female sexuality is generally more complex than that of males. For example, a woman with Moon in Scorpio will have strong sexual needs that manifest quite powerfully emotionally and physically, even if Venus and Mars don’t reflect that attunement (for instance, if Venus is in Pisces and Mars in Virgo). Although many women with emphasis on Mars, Aries, and Scorpio can and do express their assertive energy and aggressive sexuality quickly, directly, and spontaneously, the Mars energy in the majority of women remains somewhat inaccessible or at least restrained until the Venus affections are enlivened. The sexual energy and its potentially free expression can thus come forcefully to the forefront when she is relaxed and feeling loved and appreciated (all of which are Venus kinds of experience). In other words, the impersonal Mars energy in the majority of women can flow outwardly and passionately (and orgasmically) most easily after the more personal feelings of Venus are engaged. Many sensitive men will be able to
identify with this description as well, but the majority of men find it relatively easy to express the sexual and orgasmic energy of Mars in a wide variety of relationships, ranging from the deeply personal to the almost completely impersonal.

One very useful shorthand way that I have developed for encapsulating the meaning of Mars for men is that it represents the
male ego
. Likewise, Venus for women symbolizes the
female ego
. I use these terms to mean a self-conscious part of the person as it relates to their physical identity and gender, and to their pride in certain qualities related to it.

Mars for a man shows
how he gives of himself, powerfully and assertively, including sexually
. Even though Mars is not talked about in other astrology books in terms of “giving of oneself,” I think it is an appropriate phrase. For example, using his strength to provide something for another is an archetypal Mars expression that a man may be proud of—hence, the “male ego” concept. In other words, a man needs to feel that he has the qualities of his Mars sign in order to feel masculine. It will in fact be very difficult for him to play the male role in the world if he does not feel confident in his ability to express those qualities. And, on a relationship and energy level, if a man’s Mars self-assertion and energy are not received, accepted, and absorbed by a woman with whom he is developing a close relationship, the man will undoubtedly be frustrated and disappointed and may well get angry, irritated, and devitalized because he is not getting sufficiently polarized on an energy level to experience a sense of completion and energy satisfaction.

Venus for a woman represents the “female ego” in the sense that it symbolizes how she receives affection and gives of herself in love and sex. A woman typically needs (often unconsciously) to feel that she expresses the ideal of her Venus sign to some extent; she adjusts her appearance to fit this image, and this reinforces her confidence and sense of being attractive (female ego). A woman needs to feel that she
has the qualities of her Venus sign in order to feel feminine, and many women will elicit—overtly or subtly—behavior or gestures from others that support this confidence in her Venus qualities. If such needs are not fulfilled in a close relationship she is involved in, she may feel unloved, unneeded, and unfeminine. She may then become irritable, despondent, withdrawn, or negative in her attitudes because she is experiencing so little of the needed energy input and polarization that nourishes and sustains her emotionally and aesthetically.

I will give many examples of how the male ego and female ego are expressed in specific signs in the following eight chapters. But here I also want to elaborate somewhat on what, in addition to the general meanings common to all people, Mars means for most women and what Venus signifies for most men. In most women, Mars’ position has to do not only with how she is integrated with her own assertive, masculine qualities, but it also shows an idealized image of male qualities that she may project onto prospective partners. In any case, that image of excitement, and her own Mars energy, would be energized by a man whose chart activated her Mars through any number of astrological contacts. Whether or not she acts on that stimulating interaction of energies is another matter entirely, and whether she will be
broadly
 compatible with the man in question depends upon the entirety of their compatibility on many levels. But at least she will find someone who energizes that part of her physically attractive and often be romantically intrigued by him.

In most men, Venus’ position has to do with the idealized image of beauty and romance that magnetically and often erotically attracts him. Whether or not he will act on this energetic stimulation, and whether he would be compatible with such a person in a broad way, depends on many factors. But almost invariably, a man whose Venus is highly energized by a woman will find her unusually attractive, to say the least, in some cases even if there is a large age difference. In other words, his own Venus energy and awareness of beauty and
erotic attractiveness would be awakened, and in fact might occupy his fantasies for some time, even if the encounter is transitory.

Earlier in this chapter I mentioned the issue of how well integrated Venus and Mars are
within each individual
, and it is worth noting here a few more details that have an impact on this important question. The next section of this chapter, on Venus and Mars in the four elements, will give you some guidelines for evaluating the relative harmony between the two planets and their energy attunements within the individual. An important fact to keep in mind is that what appears on the surface of an individual’s personality is not necessarily aligned with or an accurate reflection of the deeper energies or tendencies the person keeps private.

For example, the Venus sign of a woman may give a public message that is starkly different from her Mars qualities. One example would be a woman who has Venus in a rather modest sign (Virgo or Libra perhaps) or in a rather aloof sign (Aquarius or Capricorn), but whose Mars is in Leo or Scorpio (very interested in energetic sex). Another example would be the opposite: if Venus is in Aries, like in the charts of Elizabeth Taylor and Marilyn Monroe, the message of glamour and aggressive sexuality is transmitted powerfully through their appearance and charm (Venus), but if Mars is in a mental sign, such as Gemini or Virgo, the woman’s actual attunement to intense sexual energy and its physical expression may be far less than advertised. In other words, in cases like this, the woman may inadvertently give messages that men constantly misinterpret. And the woman therefore may find herself quite bewildered when a variety of men get angry at her for “promising more than she delivers” or for misleading them. This is why having Venus and Mars in at least a mildly compatible relationship to each other is so helpful in close relationships: not only is it easier for the person to express the emotional and sexual energy simultaneously with another person, but also what’s on the inside may be more accurately reflected on the outside.

The same principle holds true for men’s Venus and Mars interaction. If, for example, a man has Mars in Libra and thus expresses himself in a very refined and gentlemanly manner, but his Venus is in perhaps Capricorn or Aries (both of which can at times be rather crude and impersonal), a woman encountering such a man may be in for a rude awakening. On the other hand, if a man has Mars in an overtly erotic sign like Scorpio or Taurus, but has Venus perhaps in Gemini or Aquarius (flirtatious but very detached, mental signs), once again he will give a public impression of himself that is only a partial revelation of his total erotic and romantic nature.

Venus and Mars in the Elements

Understanding the elemental energy attunement of a person’s Venus and Mars is at least as important as knowing what their sign positions symbolize. In other words, will one’s emotional, affectionate, and sexual energies be expressed in a fiery exuberant way, an earthy sensual way, an airy intellectual way, or a watery feeling way? Let’s discuss what that means in some detail and examine how such fundamental human energies are expressed in relationships.

“F
EELINGS
” C
ONTRASTED WITH
 “E
MOTIONS
”

First, however, I should make one important distinction—differentiating between
feelings
 and
emotions
. Although most astrology and psychology books do not distinguish between these experiences, it is important to define how I use these terms, especially in relation to the four elements.

The unfortunate confusion of feelings with emotions leads to all sorts of misunderstandings of people’s individual psychological nature and needs. Even in most psychology books, both popular and academic, the terms are used very imprecisely and thus are often quite misleading. In fact, many therapists and counselors believe with religious
fervor that many people’s problems can be solved merely by freely expressing their “feelings,” as if such an uninhibited outpouring cannot also lead to destructive rage, revengeful violence, and permanent damage to another person or to the relationship itself. Others mistake obvious expressions of feelings or sentiment for love, as if many feeling types of people do not also have major relationship problems (for example, if their feelings are too unfocused or too impersonal), or as if people who do not overtly express a lot of sensitivity are unable to express or experience love. Some therapists have in effect created a cult that upholds the view that “expressing feelings is communication.” The problem with this narrow view of human relationships and
eros
 is that, like so many therapeutic and personality theories over the last century, it seeks to impose a narrow straitjacket of concepts on the infinite variety and complexity of human personality.

I think it is most accurate to associate
feelings
 primarily with the
Water
 element, a receiving, private sensitivity to others, to the environment, and to subtleties of all kinds.
Emotions
 should be associated more with the
Fire
 element, as an outgoing, strongly dynamic and demonstrable form of energetic expression. A number of recent discoveries in neurobiology have also made a similar distinction, such as in the book by Antonio Damasio,
Looking for Spinoza: Joy, Sorrow, and the Feeling Brain
. In short, that book makes the point that “feeling” is mainly the experience of perceiving our
internal
 world and is essentially private, whereas “emotions” are more
extroverted
 and more overtly, excitedly physical and are often expressed with raised heartbeat, gritted teeth, and tears, anger, or visible enthusiasm. Indeed, traditionally,
Water
 in astrology is associated with private, sensitive responsiveness, whereas
Fire
 is known for great physical energy, enthusiasm, strong opinions and reactions, and a sustained focus on making an impact on the outside world.

Nevertheless, since I cannot correct the lack of precise vocabulary available today or that which we habitually use to describe human
experience, in a number of places in this book I have used the term “emotions” in a more traditional sense. For example, the fact that watery people are “emotional” is obvious to anyone who views life through the astrological lens. I merely want to make the point clear that feelings and emotions are quite different, and we would do well to define our terms better when analyzing people through any psychological system.

V
ENUS AND
 M
ARS IN THE
 W
ATER
 E
LEMENT
 (C
ANCER
, S
CORPIO, AND
 P
ISCES
)

Because water itself is formless, those with Venus or Mars in Water signs often don’t really know what they feel or what they want without experimenting. It’s only when they experiment that their feelings are drawn out and thus experienced more consciously. Remember, Water is
formless
, and so watery people’s feelings need to be given form by being with and relating to another person or situation. Water is a passive element; it
reflects
. Anyone with Venus or Mars in Water signs reflects other people’s desires; and so,
they want to be wanted
. If another person really wants them, it turns them on! The Water signs don’t want to make themselves too vulnerable, which is another reason that they will often wait for the other person to show desire or feeling first. The Water signs tend to be very passive, especially Pisces and Cancer, reacting to the other person’s intentions and desires before they themselves discover where they’re at. This is somewhat true of Scorpio also, but Scorpio is a more desirous sign with more initiative, and—even though Scorpio doesn’t like to be vulnerable either—those with strong Scorpio attunement often get so desperate for sex and/or emotional intensity that they take the risk.

Mars and Venus in Water signs are extremely sensitive, and hence they experience pleasure and emotional intimacy with even the subtlest of stimuli. Sex for them is closely associated with emotional security and emotional release, as well as with a sense of emotional
belonging
 and tranquility. The sensitivity of Mars and Venus in Water signs is not limited to their own feelings; they are also extraordinarily sensitive to where the other person is at and how he or she is responding emotionally and sexually. They
reflect
 the other person’s state of feeling and energy. They
merge
 into the other person’s feelings and so adapt themselves to the other person’s emotions. They get a lot of pleasure out of seeing the
other person’s
 subtler feelings and needs being satisfied.

Those with Venus and/or Mars in Water signs get a lot of feeding
themselves
 from feeding the other person. Cancer and Pisces are traditionally known for being giving and nurturing signs; but I think Scorpio is often much more giving and nurturing than they’re given credit for, although admittedly they can seem to
consume
 others just as often as they feed others. They’re simply so private that their better qualities are not often noticed! Yes, they are often extremely cheap with emotions as well as money; but if they decide to give, they can give tremendously! All of the Water signs can be cheap and can be takers, as well as givers. Cancer is often extremely cheap with money and self-protective of their feelings, sometimes to the point of becoming almost totally identified with the rigid mask they often wear. And Pisces, while not known for being cheap, is often a taker in the sense that they can endlessly leach energy or money from others without contributing any energy themselves. This is an example of the important point that there are always two sides to any astrological factor. We shouldn’t heap all the negative qualities of a certain element onto just one sign of that element.

One last note about a Water sign Venus or Mars: they get personal satisfaction from feeding another person emotionally and by being fed as well, with all of this happening in a mysterious sort of way that is beyond concepts. Because Water is a silent element, slowly working its will without much flamboyance, those with a Water sign Venus or Mars don’t like talking very much in romantic or sexual situations.
They tend to prefer a silent sort of merging and blending, a quiet harmony that is rarely experienced and that is to be savored in depth.

Venus in the Water Signs Keynotes:
 Affection and appreciation are expressed sympathetically and with empathy, through identifying with the other person’s feelings. The person feels love and closeness with another through an interchange of sensitivity and feelings on a subtle level, leading at times to an experience of deep merging.

Mars in the Water Signs Keynotes:
 The individual asserts self through emotional subtlety and persistence, and by appealing to the deeper feelings and needs of others. The method of operation uses intuition, shrewdness, and slyness to attain goals and desires. Physical energy is stimulated by deep yearnings, feeling needed by others, subtle intuitions, and intensity of emotional experience.

, L

 S

Venus and Mars in the Fire element are direct but rather impersonal. Love, relationships, romance, and sex are tied in with the release of abundant energy and a confirmation of their identity. They tend to be quite self-centered, in relationships and in sex; they tend to be very much on a narrow track. It’s hard for them to come down to other people’s level, to relate to others as a mere, ordinary human being. In other words, those with these placements can have relationship problems because they are too impersonal; they have difficulty adapting themselves to others and meeting others on their own level—on a simple person-to-person level. This is especially true of Venus in the Fire signs.

The Fire element has much to do with style and fantasy. Those with Venus and/or Mars in Fire want the lover or spouse to fit whatever dynamic image intrigues and excites them; they want to pull the person
into their own personal fantasy world and dramatic vision of life. Fire is always bigger than life; it’s never quite enough for them just to
be
 contentedly. Aries always has to go on to something new; Sagittarius has to expand continually; and Leo has to express themselves so broadly and to dramatize themselves so exaggeratedly that they sometimes find that their audience has given up on them and left!

Another way of saying this is that those with Venus and/or Mars in Fire want to pull their lover into the flaming dance of unreflective energy that they consider to be life. There is a dance of life that fiery people feel within themselves, and that is what they want everyone else to feel and share as well. It is as if they were saying to you, “Come on! Live! Come along with me, join my fantasy, merge with my enthusiasm, and be part of something big! Join my big dream. You’re never going to have me unless you join in with my grand vision of life.” And, if you are not so inclined, if you prefer to maintain your own identity and your own private grounding in reality, you may not blend with them very well; you may in fact exclaim, “What an obnoxious, selfish person!” and leave. You really won’t have much choice: merge into their dance or forget it. You can’t fight them and ever hope to win. The fiery people don’t extend themselves to you or moderate or limit themselves to make real contact with you; you have to adapt to them. But if you have Fire in your chart and/or admire it in others, and share this dynamic vision of life and love, then joining in that dance of cosmic Fire and vital celebration can be magical and exhilarating.

The Fire element is a strong element and brings with it a strong ego. The image of self that they project to others is very important to them; their style is very important to them. And thus, romantic and sexual encounters for them involve a required confirmation of their own sense of identity. (I might add that Fire by nature
projects light;
 those with strong Fire in their charts cannot help but project some kind of image. At best, it is light and bright, positive and inspiring. At worst, the person thinks he or she is the brightest light on God’s earth.)
Here’s a quote from one interview with a man who was describing a woman with Venus in Fire:

With fiery women, you have to become part of their dreamworld “up there” in their imagination, or else nothing can happen in the relationship. It is as if they are always waiting for the dream man to come along and fulfill their magic image. You have to fulfill or to pretend to fulfill
their
 dreams.
Your own dreams
 don’t really seem to matter to them. But as long as you can pretend, those with fiery Venus are rather easily fooled. They are not the most realistic people in the world. (AI)

One thing about Mars in Fire that appears significant is that, in men, Mars in Fire usually manifests as definite action in their own lives. In women, the Mars energy is often projected onto their partner and also powerfully activates their imagination. It is as if the Mars energy flows into the imagination and activates all sorts of projections, images, and fantasies. This is also true for men with Mars in a Fire sign, but more often a good percentage of their Mars energy also flows into physical activity and dynamic, overt action. This is of course not a sharp differentiation; you will find your local sports stadiums filled with women who have Mars in Fire signs, both as athletes and enthused spectators. But it is notable how much of a tendency there is, particularly in Fire Mars women, toward a highly charged imagination. Both sexes share a powerful tendency toward dynamic projections into the future!

For those with Venus and Mars in Fire, there is a connection between sex and high-spirited laughter, joyous exuberance, and feeling appreciated through being shown a good time. One woman interviewed who had a Fire sign Mars said, “Laughing a lot and having a good time makes me more seducible.” The Fire sign placements manifest as a genuine enthusiasm for sex, but—for many—only when it is morally permissible and when they are getting sufficient respect
from the other person. Remember, their self-image has to remain intact and needs to be polished by the other person’s attention! But the Fire element also shows sexual impatience and a tendency toward ordering others around that quite often elicits considerable resentment and sometimes rejection from their partners.

Venus in the Fire Signs Keynotes:
 Affection and appreciation are expressed energetically, directly, and grandly. The person feels love and closeness with another through sharing of vigorous activities as well as through mutual aspirations and enthusiasms, such as the sense of their own superior ideals.

Mars in the Fire Signs Keynotes:
 The individual asserts self through direct physical action, initiative, and outgoing demonstrative energy. The personal method of operation is direct expression of will, authority, and power. Physical energy is stimulated by constant movement, confident enthusiasm, and dynamic action.

With Venus and Mars in Earth, bringing an innate attunement to the rhythms of nature, it should come as no surprise that
time
 is a major consideration and determining factor in their relationships … time and patience. They take time to express their feelings and desires; sometimes they take what seems to others to be an interminable amount of time! Strong as the physical instincts are in the Earth element, there is a tremendous caution, self-protectiveness, and practicality that dominate the instincts, overruling the need for romance and excitement. Self-control is a theme you find throughout all the activities and motivations of all three Earth signs. And, in fact, not just self-control, but also a desire to control everything and everyone else! The Earth signs seem to imagine that through control they can achieve security.

However, once those with this Earth emphasis finally say yes to someone and commit themselves to a relationship, they tend to do so very deeply and with considerable steadiness of commitment. You then know they are
there
 with you, not spaced-out in their heads or anxiously awaiting a more exciting opportunity. Then they try to make the relationship
work!
 One problem that arises in relating to those with a dominant Earth element is that you never quite know if they are involved with you because they really
care
 about you personally or because they have some practical reason for being with you. For instance, are they just buying security? Do you happen to be convenient for them as a stepping-stone to their ambitions?

Those with Mars or Venus in the Earth signs tend to be dutiful and efficient, although the laziness of Taurus sometimes takes precedence over duty and efficiency. They also tend to be proud of sexual
technique:
 they often work on technique, trying to become accomplished at it, and attempt to stay in control even of passion. Mars and Venus in Earth signs are very basic and down-to-earth. Even Virgo is quite a sensual sign. It’s not particularly sexual, but it is sensual and very physically oriented. So many people with strong Virgo get into the healing arts, nutritional studies, nursing, physical therapy, and massage. The Earth signs want to take care of human needs and instincts efficiently and impersonally. Sex, love, and intimate relationships are tied in with basic needs and duties. This approach leads to their being rather mechanical at times and sometimes downright boring. Spontaneity and imagination are not their greatest strengths.

Those with Venus in Earth signs seek shelter, structure, and emotional security in relationships, and this can lead to excessive traditionalism, conservative attitudes, and rigidity, which can lead, in turn, to deep loneliness. Instead of producing a solid, grounded emotional base, the Earth element sometimes takes over and makes the person totally inflexible. The physical presence of their beloved is felt to indicate that everything in the relationship is OK. The earthy person tends
to believe in forms as an ultimate reality, even if they are in essence false, hypocritical, or deceptive. If you want to make an impression on a partner who has a lot of Earth emphasis, whether Venus, Mars, Sun, or Moon, you may well find that what really gets through to him or her and elicits a prompt reaction is your physically leaving for a while. That is the only thing that is real to many of them and accomplishes much more than months of discussion trying to convince them that a problem exists.

Here is a quote from a woman with Mars in Taurus, taken from an interview: in explaining how she likes to be treated by men, she said, “Be substantial with me. Be physical with me. Want my body, not just me!” This is a sharp contrast to Mars or Venus in Air, for example. The Air person would never say this. The Air person would say, “Get to know me personally, inquire about what I think.… my body can come later!” Physicality, form, appearance, and dress are all extremely important to the earthy people. In other words, exterior appearances matter a great deal, especially when Venus is in Earth.

Because of their extraordinary conservatism in relationships, those with Earth emphasis (especially those with the relationship planet Venus in Earth signs) deny themselves many possibilities in human relationships. They are so traditional and people have to fit their categories so specifically that many possibilities of new experience with people are neglected or dismissed as impossible or impractical. This is of course true for many people, not just those with Venus in Earth; but I feel it is more common and restrictive for those of the Earth attunement. The Earth types tend to be quite formal, and it is beyond their imagination that people with different attunements might have less rigid approaches to life and relationships. Nor can they imagine that there would be much satisfaction from any kind of relationship other than a somewhat traditional arrangement of well-defined duties, rights, and lifestyle.

Venus in the Earth Signs Keynotes:
 Affection and appreciation are expressed tangibly, dependably, and physically. The person feels love and closeness with another through commitment and building a life together, as well as through sensual pleasure and sharing responsibilities.

Mars in the Earth Signs Keynotes:
 The individual asserts self through concrete achievement requiring patience and persistence. The personal method of operation is careful, cautious, and often focused on efficiency. Physical energy is stimulated by hard work, self-discipline, challenge, and satisfaction at having done one’s duty or obligations well.

 M
ARS IN THE
 A

, L

 A
