In contrast to the Water element’s propensity toward silence, the Air element placements of Venus and Mars are inclined toward talk—verbal communication and verbal expressions of curiosity, feeling, interest, and desire. They can even be rather noisy at times! And, as mentioned earlier, Venus and Mars in Air do not tend to be particularly sensual, which is to say that they do not need to feel
physically
 close to another person in the same way that those with Water and Earth emphasis do. However, if the person has an Earth Sun sign, for example, this alone can add a strong tone of sensuality and physicality to the personality, even if Venus and Mars are both in Air. So, I reiterate that the entire chart must always be considered in any evaluation. This is why an in-person dialogue is so much better than any kind of written or computerized “chart interpretation”; it’s far easier to blend all the factors in person, especially with the help of feedback, to achieve an accurate and realistic impression of the whole person.

Mars in the Air element does not indicate a particularly strong
sexual energy, although they may get the prize for flirtatious remarks! Sex for those with Mars in Air is tied in with
communication
 and also with fun, mental stimulation, and enlivened mental images. This is the kind of person who may go out on the town with someone and actually enjoy the entire evening even though there is no emotional or physical sharing of an intense sort, so long as the conversation and communication are interesting. This sort of approach will bewilder those with a more erotic and physical orientation—it staggers their imagination that anyone can be so pleased and satisfied with talk alone.

Those with an Air emphasis are stimulated by mental forms and images. They have a very
personal
 orientation. Libra is the most personal of all the signs, hopelessly personal in fact. As Dane Rudhyar wrote in
The Pulse of Life
, Libra takes all social and personal activities with the utmost seriousness; and that often makes life difficult for them because they assume that everyone else is as sincere as they are! Gemini is probably the second most personal sign, although they have an impersonal streak that surprises those who were entertained and stimulated by their charm. And Aquarius is the least personal of the Air signs. Aquarius can be personal, but only for certain periods of time. Their impersonality will eventually surface, so you have to get used to being periodically ignored … which may not be hard for you if you also have some Aquarius (or Aries) in your chart.

In fact, if you’ve ever been jilted by someone with Venus or Mars in Aquarius, you know what I mean by the impersonal quality. (This is also found in those who have a strong Uranus in their chart.) With Aquarius, at a certain point as a relationship with them is being terminated, you may suddenly realize that he or she was
never
 really relating to you personally. Evidently, you were merely a category of person who temporarily was needed to fill a particular slot in the Aquarian’s mental-reality model. So, Aquarius can act very personal, and they can be personal in their own way, in an authentic way; but when the impersonality comes through, it’s almost frighteningly aloof and detached.

I have had counseling sessions with so many women who have Venus in Aquarius who come to the office and immediately say, “I have a big problem with relationships. None of the men I know understand me. I’m completely frustrated. I need them but I also need independence from them!” All of these women were so independent that it was obvious to the men that these women didn’t need them as much as the men would have liked them to. And yet, they enjoy these men’s intelligence and they enjoy flirting and socializing; they tend to be quite vivacious. But their independence and detachment are very disconcerting for most men, especially possessive or traditional types of men. Traditional female social roles are felt by these women to be far too restrictive in all aspects of intimate relationship.

Another comment about Mars in Air signs: with Mars in Air, the mind rules the sexuality and sexual energy. Everything first goes through their heads! The
idea
 of a particular relationship, its
form
, must appeal to them to get them motivated. In fact, they will often reject feelings if the intellect cannot easily categorize what is happening. Even if the feelings are pleasant, the person might repress or reject them unless the mind can somehow process the feelings into familiar categories. This is a good example of the rarely acknowledged
conservatism
 of the Air signs. It demonstrates one reason why the Earth and the Air signs have the same ancient ruling planets (Venus, Mercury, and Saturn). Both groups of signs have a tendency to become fixated on form, categories, or rigidity of one sort or another. This is why an old Libra often resembles a Taurus, why an old Aquarius often seems similar to a rigid Capricorn, and why an aging Gemini often gets as cranky and irritable as a picky Virgo!

Venus in the Air Signs Keynotes:
 Affection and appreciation are expressed through intense intellectual communication and a sense of companionship. The person feels love and closeness with another through verbal sharing, a meeting of minds, and mutual pleasant socializing.

Mars in the Air Signs Keynotes:
 The individual asserts self through expression of ideas, active communication, and energetic imagination. The personal method of operation relies on persuasion and an appeal to reason or being personally likable. Physical energy is stimulated by mental challenges, social activism, relationships, and new ideas.

Compatibility on the Elements’ Energy Level

So, how does one usually
experience
 the energy compatibility I am referring to, on the Fire, Water, Earth, or Air levels? Let’s discuss a few recognizable characteristics of such experiences, in all of which one’s sense of personal isolation is happily reduced. In fact, in many cases, one temporarily no longer feels so
lonely
 in life! This is a rare and extremely pleasing sensation, one that is often remembered for years afterward—a sense of
belonging
 in life, a feeling of security in the cosmos, an experience of unity with another person and sometimes even with humanity as a whole.

On the
Water
 level, one loses one’s sense of time and space, and the feeling of going beyond self can even reach the point of feeling totally merged with another person. This level of harmony is also quite profound and thus usually accompanied by a sense of awe rather than verbal descriptions. With compatibility on the
Air
 levels, there is instantaneous mental understanding of (even if not complete agreement with) the other person’s way of thinking and communicating. One’s own mental boundaries expand, and there is a genuine appreciation of the other person and his or her intelligence. There is also often wordplay and humor.

Compatibility on the
Fire
 level manifests as a perception that the two people are sharing an enthusiasm and common purpose that is coming from a bigger source, as if both are part of something huge and cosmic. Both are caught up in a huge release of high-spirited energy, and both feel they are participating in a larger-than-life experience
and a powerful, impersonal energy source that is truly inspirational. Compatibility on the
Earth
 level is sometimes experienced as what a friend of mine (with Taurus Mars) has called an “ecstatic sense of security.” One’s senses come alive, and the perception of physical reality and the body changes noticeably. Both people can also have an intensified appreciation of nature and the environment, and there is often an awareness of being supremely focused in the present in a rare, relaxed, accepting frame of mind.

The importance of recognizing the reality of the energy level of compatibility cannot be overemphasized, because it can explain so much about the dynamics of close relationships and in fact can help achieve a clarifying perspective on—
and often improve—many
 areas of a specific relationship. One area where understanding the energy interchange is especially useful is in assessing compatibility on the sexual level. Perhaps this is so because sex involves such a direct contact with primal human energies and their accompanying emotions. It utterly bewilders me that most counselors and sex therapists have only a random batch of speculative theories to support their work, and unfortunately for them and their clients, most completely ignore the insights into sexual energy and its interchange with another person that can be found in astrology.

It became painfully apparent to me, through contact with hundreds of clients who had previously been to counselors and therapists who were not familiar with astrology, that so many sexual problems were merely a case of simple old-fashioned incompatibility. This includes many cases of sexual “dysfunction,” such as impotence, frigidity, premature ejaculation, and so on. To be sure, many cases of incompatibility can be worked with, adjusted to, and lived with. But this is not to say that the fundamental lack of harmony
on the level of sexual energy
 has been changed or “cured.” It is simply acknowledging the fact that very few couples are completely compatible on the sexual level, and in fact—from my experience—I estimate that the vast majority of
couples live with some degree of sexual/emotional incompatibility to which they have to adjust with some conscious effort.

The point is that sexual compatibility or incompatibility essentially emanates from the energy attunement of the individuals involved. Such a view is quite incomprehensible to the therapists and “researchers” who insist on viewing sex as primarily a mechanical function (although they do admit it has some emotional overtones). This depersonalization of sex, and the accompanying separation of sex from the cosmic dance of life—which should be seen more as a
religious
 experience than as a mechanistic experience—naturally leads to human beings who have sexual problems being treated in a mechanical way, and often with an overlay of extraneous intellectual analysis. The saddest part of this practice is that such treatment often tends in the long run to aggravate the original problem, for the person is encouraged to become even more of a
disembodied self
.

Perhaps the most tragic consequence of many superficial modern approaches to sexuality (whether in the mass media, academic psychology, or couples therapy theory) is that
it causes people to distrust their own unique sexual and emotional nature and responses
. Using astrology, on the other hand, to analyze individuals’ erotic and relationship orientations involves not only accurately describing their attunements but also recognizing the huge variety of energy combinations and modes of expression.
After all, why does it often take decades to find even one person with whom you are especially compatible, if not for the complexity of the issues and personality factors involved?
 (
Please re-read that sentence!
) If the simplistic answers put forth in so many pop-psychology books, courses, and TV shows were truly reliable or comprehensive, one must admit that the results in individual lives would be far better. When you are really attuned to someone on the energy level, there is no problem with the pace, rhythm, or intensity of your energy flow in relation to the other person. Everything flows together naturally. When this admittedly ideal
harmony is not present, however—as is usually the case—the birth charts provide diagrams that are much like X-rays of the individuals’ energy patterns and attunements. These are extremely helpful in understanding the conflicts and blockages being experienced, and also in discovering the areas where energy flow might be channeled more easily and agreeably.

One psychologist who has presented a realistic approach to many relationship and sexual problems, especially in the context of society’s pressures and double-standards today, is Herb Goldberg. In his excellent book
The Hazards of Being Male
, he describes the importance of recognizing what is essentially an issue of energy compatibility, rather than viewing many sexual problems as
mechanical
 malfunctions. For example, he points out that impotence is “almost always a pair-specific phenomenon” that is in effect making a statement about the man’s feelings toward the woman and their relationship at that time. His energy is not being mobilized within the mutual energy field that they are generating, whether that is a semipermanent or merely a transitory situation. He points out that it is more correct to say that “men are impotent with
some
 women under
some
 conditions and their nonresponsive reactions reflect important truths that they must learn to trust and understand” (HBM, p. 28). Likewise, many sexual problems experienced by women should not be considered only
her
 problem, but rather manifestations within the energy field and emotional reality of a specific relationship. In fact, Goldberg observes, in this era of over-intellectualization, trying to disguise, rationalize, or ignore the lack of a genuine, spontaneous sexual response may be abandoning an important way of monitoring the state of the relationship and each person’s feelings about it. And in addition, failing to deal with the real problem is setting up a strong likelihood for worse intimacy and relationship problems in the future.

Similarly, the work of Dr. Elisabeth Lloyd and many other researchers also challenges the mechanical view of sexual relationship and
dynamics that dominates so much of modern theory and therapy, and that has such unfortunate results in individual human lives. A professor of biology at Indiana University, Dr. Lloyd shows how society so completely misunderstands and distorts the nature and function of female orgasm, largely by misguidedly superimposing a one-style-fits-all rigid mechanical view of how all women supposedly ought to function sexually. “Getting the evolutionary story straight,” she says, “has potentially very large social and personal consequences for all women, and indirectly for men, as well.” I won’t go into the details of the research presented in her book
The Case of the Female Orgasm: Bias in the Science of Evolution;
 I merely want to emphasize that this widespread misunderstanding—and the resultant cultural beliefs, media images, and psychological expectations related to such exaggerations or distortions—has a powerful impact on identity, relations between the sexes, and self-understanding. If women have unrealistic expectations about what is normal or natural in relation to what they may experience in intercourse, they may well feel inadequate, inferior, or abnormal when they do not actually have such spontaneous ecstatic experiences. This can result in an inhibiting lack of confidence in the women, and also in disappointment, criticism, or pressure from their partners. False theories that are generally accepted about female orgasm, Dr. Lloyd states, are significant partly because “men’s expectations about women’s normal sexuality, about how women should perform, are built around these notions.”

Dr. Lloyd’s work, and that of other researchers, provides key insights into how people’s theoretical expectations can have severely debilitating effects on behavior and self-confidence. In other words, it is customary in our society today to unnecessarily complicate our lives through misinterpreting people’s behavior by unconsciously assuming that we are all alike, or by adding numerous layers of “scientific” theory, faddish assumptions, or psychological jargon to the ways that we perceive people. Instead of looking at life through rose-colored glasses,
we then wind up seeing life through thickly coated dark glasses that impede our ability to see reality with sharp clarity. Astrology, on the other hand, when properly understood and applied, enables one to
individualize
 the assessment of human personality, motivation, and behavior in a way unknown to any other method of psychological or scientific understanding.

Before going into the details of Venus and Mars in each sign, there are a few issues that I must draw your attention to with great emphasis. First, although I have stated often in the preceding chapters how amazingly complex and varied people are, it is easy to read over such words and say, “Well, of course!” and then go on to live out unconscious prejudices without realizing the implications of what I am trying to clarify. Therefore, I think it will be much more striking if I give a couple examples to show the relativity and subjectivity of all observations and descriptions of other people, especially in regard to the ultimately subjective sphere of intimate experience. From the notes I took when I led discussion groups about relationships, I find two rather humorous but also illuminating examples. Note that the groups were composed of mostly women, and they talked primarily about men. All knew some astrology, and I therefore could easily find out the astrological factors of each person in the group.

One woman (who had other fixed signs emphasized that conflicted with the fixed sign Leo) observed that Mars in Leo men were so “loud, pushy, egotistical, and obnoxious” that she always found them impossible to get along with. Another woman, who had the Moon harmoniously aligned with (and thus receptive to) Leo, immediately responded with her sharply contrasting viewpoint and comments: “Leo Mars men are so warm, affectionate, and generous!” She was always emotionally charmed by them.

A woman with Venus in Taurus expressed her exasperation about men with Mars in Aquarius, undoubtedly feeling that they were
not physical and demonstrative enough: “They are so frustrating and aloof!” But immediately, a Venus in Gemini woman (whose Air attunement was receptive and appreciative toward a Mars in the Air sign Aquarius) voiced enthusiastic praise for that kind of man’s “brave leadership and mediating abilities with all kinds of people,” which she found very stimulating. The woman with Venus in Gemini did not at all mind the Mars in Aquarius man’s lack of physical intensity, so long as his verbal and intellectual skills were energizing.

Rarely have I been so starkly reminded as I was in those groups of how we all view other human beings through our own particular lenses! One of the great values of learning astrology is that
we can become fluent in a language of human personality and behavior
 that not only is unbelievably accurate, but also contributes to mutual human understanding and tolerance.

Venus, Mars, and the Sun’s Energy

Another point that I cannot emphasize enough is that Venus and Mars should
never
 be considered in isolation from the rest of the chart, and particularly never analyzed separately from the Sun, the source of all energy in our solar system, which—scientifically and symbolically—provides the core vitality of every individual person. In analyzing someone’s personality through astrology, the Sun’s energy, one might speculate, is filtered through, reflected in, and distributed by the planets. To put this specifically, the Sun’s element and sign energy is implicitly enlivening and flowing through
all the other planetary placements
 in anyone’s birth chart. (Obviously the Moon reflects the solar light, and there is even some scientific evidence that Jupiter amplifies the solar radiation that it receives.) This seems to me a useful conceptualization of how energy seems to flow in the energy fields of individuals, as depicted in individual birth charts—those maps of
our solar system that are calculated for the moment of birth, which are the foundation of all astrological studies.

I arrived at this particular perception only after many years of observing people, and I was particularly helped by a detailed study of athletes and their birth charts that I did over a period of twenty years, focusing on a variety of sports but especially basketball, baseball, and some track and field. Watching the athletes in action allowed observation of the direct expression of their individual energy qualities and styles, without the intervention of words or social posturing to obscure the person’s true nature. Through this study, it became apparent that, for example, in an especially aggressive Aries Sun athlete, Mars (the “ruling planet” of Aries) in Gemini could easily manifest as a very intelligent basketball player with quick perceptions and great hand-eye coordination, who was also known for the usual Gemini Mars traits of being outspoken and often critical of his teammates for poor judgment on the court. In other words, the aggressive Aries Sun Fire energy was pouring through that player’s entire personality, making his Mars more forceful than Gemini Mars is usually known to be.

A few additional examples of the importance of the Sun’s element, sign, and vital power in determining the expression of Mars and Venus energies will make this concept come alive even more effectively. Because, as I mentioned, the Sun seems to radiate its energy
through
 the person’s Mars and Venus, one could simply state that there are twelve general types of expression for each Mars sign, depending on the Sun sign (for example, Mars in Aries with Aries Sun, Mars in Aries with Taurus Sun, and so on). There are also many kinds of Venus attunements for each Venus sign (for example, Venus in Pisces with Pisces Sun, Venus in Pisces with Aries Sun, and so on). The following examples will illuminate this blending of energies:

One of the best basketball players of recent years, Tim Duncan, is a Taurus Sun, and sure enough, he seems self-contained and
impassive most of the time. However, his Venus (the ruling planet of Taurus) is in aggressive, competitive Aries, which manifests as an intensely focused competitor whose self-control should not be mistaken for passivity. In his short career so far, he has already won three championships in the most competitive professional league in the world, the NBA.

Although Venus in Virgo is known for modesty, inhibition, and some erotic awkwardness, it is not lacking in sensuality and erotic eagerness when the Sun is, for example, in demonstrative Leo or highly sexual Scorpio. Likewise, rather cool, aloof Libra Venus will be far more intensely expressive if the Sun is in Scorpio or Leo.

On the other hand, a Libra Sun or Pisces Sun person with a Scorpio Mars, although undoubtedly being strongly attuned to sexual expression, may be somewhat less sexually energetic (and certainly more refined and sensitive) than a person with the Sun in the more lusty signs of Aries, Scorpio, or Capricorn who also has Mars in Scorpio.

An earthy Capricorn Sun individual who has Venus in aloof, mental Aquarius may be much more physically demonstrative of his or her affections than would a detached, intellectual Aquarius Sun person with Venus in Aquarius as well.

A Gemini Sun or Leo Sun individual will usually be far more sensitive to the
feelings
 of others if Venus is in ultrasensitive Cancer than if Venus is in the less emotionally sensitive sign of Gemini or Leo.

As the reader becomes acquainted with the basic energies and qualities of the planets in various signs, he or she will be able to visualize and understand such combinations more and more accurately over time. This procedure of
blending
 the various factors in an individual’s birth chart is a necessary skill required to analyze and interpret the
many factors in any individual chart. I also want to emphasize the
complexity
 of energy flow within each person, which reflects the psychological and erotic complexity of each individual and is also crucial in understanding the energy interactions between people. When two people meet, there is a wide range of energy interchange that happens immediately, consciously or subconsciously. Multiple signals are exchanged, instinctively or intentionally. And we often mistakenly generalize one strong energy signal to mean that that particular quality, just because we are so receptive to it, represents the totality of the person’s nature—a truly misleading self-deception! Astrology, far more precisely than any other method, can help clarify the complexity and quality of these interpersonal interactions on the fundamental energy level of living reality.

Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

9

Venus in the Fire Signs

Venus in any of the Fire signs tends to express idealism and high expectations associated with their love relationships and social relationships. They are rather impulsive, and they energetically express their enthusiasms. In that sense, they are “emotional”—but they are not particularly
feeling
 in the sense of demonstrating sensitivity to others. In fact, a simple, sensitive “give-and-take” on a personal level is often difficult for these people. Big, impersonal dramatic gestures are far more natural and comfortable for them. In fact, I would say that, for Venus in Fire,
respect
 is more important than detailed communication in relationships. They feel close to others when enthusiasms, distant goals, or dynamic activities are shared with them. Their own identity has to be strongly confirmed in relationships, and a partner in a close relationship has to fit their exciting and idealized vision of life and their idealized image of a lover. Because of this lack of realism, they can be easily fooled in love and other close relationships. They do not generally extend themselves to inquire about and discover the other person, as explained in
Chapter 8
, but rather expect the other to come and join them and reinforce their personal dream (and preferably polish their self-image). (The reader should review the section on Venus in the Fire signs in
Chapter 8
 for more details.)

Other keys to understanding Venus in the Fire signs are as follows:

Their inspirations and future aspirations about life and relationships are more
real
 to them than are traditions, social conventions, or logic.

Because they need to pour out their energies into the world, they are
demonstrative
 but also often tactless, impulsive, and insensitive. In fact they put out much more energy than they are willing to experience internally and intimately, thus often confusing the opposite sex.

Venus in Fire sign people are not particularly receptive, emotionally or sexually, although they are
very
 receptive to admiration and flattery. They equate romance with hyperenergetic activity and—if possible—some demonstration of personal worship.

Venus in Aries

Those with Venus in the impulsive, aggressive, impatient sign of Aries are driven to impress people, show others new things, or introduce them to new experiences. They need to feel they truly excel in their chosen field. They also need to feel that they are in charge, directing the action; however, in relationships, they are especially attracted to others who show a similar initiative—which often leads to conflicts. Such headstrong people admire others who are forceful and innovative, but can they get along? Venus in Aries people, especially if they like you, are very direct and impulsively spontaneous. Their greatest charm is the straightforwardness of their approach to social interaction, often laced with slightly daring humor. If things start going awry, especially as the initial excitement of a new activity or relationship diminishes, their mood can change quickly to being combatively oversensitive and easily offended. These folks are not known for subtlety, and they often like competitive sports, even violent ones like boxing. Fighting is a natural part of life for them, and they admire others who
will fight for a cause. They value such individuality and fierce independence. They themselves, however, want to be Number One. They hate to be kept waiting and are afraid of not only boredom, but also of being taken advantage of (since that signifies that someone has
got the better of them
). The key to the psychological nature of Aries is an
addiction to the

new
. Life is not worth living for them if there is not a new challenge, a new project, a new person, or a new level of achievement to conquer. And this also goes for their relationships, which is probably their biggest problem area—achieving a stable, satisfying, give-and-take partnership.

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 V
ENUS IN
 A
RIES

After decades of working with astrological psychology, I have distilled the following core psychological qualities for those with Venus in Aries in this way:

Expresses affection directly, impulsively, enthusiastically; especially enjoys the first stages of relationships.

Self-assertive and demanding qualities can make intimacy difficult to achieve.

Values individuality, initiative, and independence in self and others; gives of self energetically and responds to others’ enthusiasms.

Both men and women with Venus in Aries can be
fiercely
 proud of their attachments, loved ones, and relationship achievements. They can certainly be emotional, but rarely would any observer describe them as deeply
feeling
. These folks must believe that they are “winning” in any relationship; some view relationships as a field of competition—which they may fear or be challenged by—but at least where they want to come out on top. Those of both sexes tend to lose interest easily in a potential lover or partner when their imperfections become apparent. Such is the idealization of a “lover” for those with Aries
Venus that one gets the impression that they imagine a superwoman or superman as the only being suitable for their commitment. Neither sex is very romantically sentimental, receptive, or sympathetic; they are more direct and blunt. The similarities heard in interviews are striking: the Venus in Aries men, according to women, are not very giving and are extremely self-centered; and the Venus in Aries women, according to men, aren’t very receptive, patient, or feminine. (See the details and quotes at the end of this section for examples.)

Women with an Aries Venus face quite a challenge, for the planet of love, sharing, and harmony is in the sign of war, strife, and assertion of the Self (rather than appreciation of the Other, which is the keynote of the opposite sign, Libra); this is a difficult attunement, but then Aries people are supposed to love challenges! “These women have no patience with feminine games,” as one male interviewee said; “they are not passive, soft, yielding” (AI). They initiate action and take charge, but they also like a man who is aggressive and in fact will not truly respect one who is too gentle or patient! Venus in Aries in a woman is a built-in contradiction that expresses itself in many forms: they demand that the male be aggressive but often react to male aggression with explosive anger and indignation; they are straightforward in expressing feelings, yet irritable and demanding; apparently excitable and even insatiable sexually, they burn out quickly and have difficulty receiving and sharing in sex. In other words, they are actively desirous sexually in an impulsive and rather “masculine” way. Thus, they have trouble relaxing and opening up—physically and emotionally. One solution to their sexual difficulties is self-knowledge, coupled with cultivating more patience with oneself and with potential lovers, as well as learning how to express more tolerance and openness with their partners.

Men with Venus in Aries may be especially appreciated by women of the feminist mode who crave independence and who are comfortable with being extremely aggressive. At least, these men will be appreciated until they reveal that they actually do have expectations
and demands, and might even be the first signers of a declaration of the double-standard. Until then, if the woman can pretend to be a superior being with great sexual interest, no faults, and no needs whatsoever, you can continue to “party.” Venus in Aries men often like sports, whether being actively engaged and working energetically at staying in shape, or simply observing competition from the comfort of a couch. These guys really do love being cared for and catered to by a woman, but they often seem clueless as to how to express that appreciation in a way the woman understands. As one man wrote in a questionnaire about men with Aries Venus, “They have childlike fantasies and actions, easily attracted to superficial things like hairstyle. They have a simplicity of attitude toward love and women” (AQ).

Among the most insightful quotes, interview statements, and questionnaire replies that illuminate Venus in Aries are these:

Julia Parker further explains a central dilemma of an Aries Venus: “It is, however, important for them to be aware that they put their own needs and ideas about how the relationship should develop rather too forcibly, and they must try to counter this somewhat selfish attitude” (AHB).

One woman, who did not know astrology, remarked of two different men she knew who both had Venus in Aries: “Both are incredibly selfish beings emotionally” (AI).

One man who had been married to an Aries Sun woman who also had Venus in Aries remarked: “Masculine type sexuality and fast and furious sex. But her emotional needs were zero. Wanted a lot of sex but did not want to prolong it” (AI).

Another man wrote of Venus in Aries women: “Strong sex drive, sexually impulsive. Takes the initiative sexually (which is
very
 rare among women!). Has a big sexual ego and wants to be very sexually attractive. Very direct about sexual matters” (AQ).

Debbi Kempton Smith, in her entertaining and insightful book
Secrets from a Stargazer’s Notebook
, rounds out the picture of Venus in Aries: “The males and the females with Venus in Aries are male chauvinists. Imagine what this does to women with Venus here. They too believe that woman’s place is in the sack, being conquered, but they love the hunt.… These women are fiercely competitive.… But they do little to earn your admiration in the sack because they are so selfish. They will only strive to please you at the outset” (SSN).

“Affectionate relationships begin with enormous enthusiasm but may fizzle easily—Aries’s enthusiasm is not famous for its longevity” (WA).

A thirty-year-old woman in a questionnaire says of both sexes who have Venus in Aries: “They are very idealistic and romantic and seem to be more in love with the idea of love than they are interested in actually cultivating relationships beyond the initial excitement of newness. The men are especially prone to confusing lust with love, and both sexes tend to lose interest quickly unless the object of their affections continues to fulfill the ideal of ‘super-person.’ They also aren’t much into ‘preliminaries’ of courtship; they want to get right down to the business of satisfying the physical desires that attracted them in the first place; they’re very direct in this kind of expression” (AQ).

One female questionnaire respondent with Venus in Aries says: “I’m always looking for a certain excitement in my day-to-day existence. I like to be enthusiastic and positive.… I’m not over-assertive but I don’t let anyone walk all over me. Also, it’s important for me to be independent and self-sufficient, and I have gone to great lengths to maintain these things.… I am attracted to fiery men the most” (AQ).

Venus in Leo

Those with Venus in the proud, dramatic sign of Leo don’t do anything half way. When showing their affection for someone, they do not just buy them a simple rose, but rather a florist shop! When committed to a loved one, they can demonstrate an admirable loyalty. But some of these folks can go to such extremes of misplaced, immoderate, impractical loyalty that they wind up not only giving up far too much of their own common sense and objectivity, but also often inadvertently harming the very person or family to which they were so
blindly
 loyal. Although this placement is not particularly complicated psychologically (since they primarily thrive on praise and attention), they do embody various contradictions. Their emotional expression, while warmhearted, is also calculating and not at all spontaneous. As Grant Lewi pointed out, they want to make sure that they make the right effect, and they use their affections at will to get what they want. Lewi continued, “No one with Venus in Leo ever was talked into anything” (AFM). Some other sharply contrasting qualities in their personality are sincerity, generosity, and encouragement of others, combined with patronizingly demeaning others and showing jealousy of other people’s success. At best, they outgrow this childish self-centeredness as they go through life and achieve a true, big-hearted nobility. Flamboyant demonstrations of their success, and fondness for public expressions of wealth and superiority, often overwhelm the feeling of warmth that old friends and family once had for them. A huge, vulnerable pride is evident in their lives and relationships. The merest “slight” is taken as a major offense: try not going to their party or failing to do the properly worshipful gesture in a public setting. They are very proud of their closest relationship and, in general, have to feel intensely respected by all. They deeply want
admiration
 and recognition, even if they are unconscious of that desire. Their greatest gift is probably their ability
to vitalize others through their faith in them, and through conveying a warmth of encouragement toward a greater goal or ideal. This is where they are truly God’s gift—a channel for heartfelt inspiration.

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 V
ENUS IN
 L
EO

The core psychological qualities and tendencies I have distilled for those with Venus in Leo are:

Expresses affection warmly, dramatically, playfully, and enthusiastically.

Exchange of deeper feelings may be encumbered by the need to be the center of attention or to dominate others’ emotional life.

Gives of self with creative vitality and receives from others graciously and proudly; can be very generous and loyal.

Both men and women with Venus in Leo are rather in love with dramatic love. They crave grandeur and excitement, and often stage affectionate displays and romantic situations. They both have tremendous pride in their primary partnership and also in their own wonderful generous qualities. Although easily offended by real or imagined slights from others, they are often quite insensitive to other people’s feelings. After all, they are superior beings, and their ego and freedom of emotional expression are far more important than the mere petty concerns of the common people. Many Venus in Leo people are grandiose, attention-seeking, or snobs, although they seek to conceal it from others. A constant need for praise is perhaps their most tiresome feature, and they have a tremendous weakness for flattery, one of the ways that they can be emotionally manipulated. If these folks, however, outgrow and transcend the small ego that is pretending to be great and learn to really give of the sunny Leo love this placement ideally symbolizes, a cosmic beneficence can shine through.

Women with Leo Venus can be the true prima donnas of the romantic sphere, with a queen-complex in love. At worst they are so self-centered that they can’t relate at all on a simple, sincere human level and often use others for self-aggrandizement. And so they sometimes wind up lonely, peevish, and indignant. At best, they are true blue and make everyone feel happier and appreciative about life and love, although they may break the budget doing it. They want to do a lot for their beloved and make sure he enjoys every moment of time spent with her, and they don’t mind if lots of other people notice their magnificence and generosity. Their own sense of identity and value is sometimes so tied up with the current state of their primary relationship that they often go into crisis when things get rocky. Although liking overt physical demonstrations of affection, they are rather impersonal and not particularly receptive on a true feeling level. They stimulate enthusiastic displays of “love,” especially if really in need of affection, but this “loyal” queen can also drop you like a stone immediately if someone more exciting and glamorous comes along. When at their most arrogant and haughty, they can treat men like dogs, even after they go through the paces of showing the required devotion.

When a man has Venus in Leo he is invariably proud of his
values
 as well as his woman. His way of showing appreciation to friends or coworkers is to say, “I’m proud of you”; and you are supposed to take that as adequate payment for your work or loyalty. He thinks flattery means as much to everyone else as it does to him. In romance, he prefers someone he can show off but also someone who admires him endlessly and publicly. He likes dramatic flair and demonstrative women who make grand gestures and parade “fine” tastes. As one questionnaire stated, “Venus in Leo in men gives a
marked
 propensity to take over as the center of attention in social situations.” They can be good at sales work, at business management, and in large groups. Although flirtatious and sometimes boastful, they do not seem to cut
themselves off from successful relationships or marriage due to pride or infidelity alone; in contrast, the women with this position, much more commonly, may do so in an outburst of indignant anger.

Among the most perceptive insights I’ve found about Venus in Leo are the following quotations from writings, interviews, and questionnaires:

Mary Coleman captures some of the dominant traits of this personality factor: “Responses to love and affection are lavish, open-hearted and almost childishly trusting. The urge to spread sunshine through the adored one’s life is heartfelt and genuine. But later, some recipients may realize that in return lifelong homage and admiration are also decreed” (PIC).

A woman who had worked extensively with men and women who had this placement stated: “Flattery will get you everywhere with these people! They must be respected, and they need praise and compliments continually. They want to feel like a king or queen. They can be encouraging to others, but expect reciprocation” (AI).

The experiences of more than one man with Leo Venus women were synthesized rather bluntly in comments from another interviewee: “The message they give out is ‘I am a queen-like bitch and won’t give you the time of day.’ These women seem especially cruel and obsessed with their powers over men if they also have other planets in Leo or some Scorpio in their charts. They often treat men like shit while simultaneously flirting with them to get worship and have their asses kissed. The man must be obsequious and let himself be walked on for her to promise a future bestowal of even slight favors. But then she violates her promises” (AI).

A female questionnaire respondent has observed that folks with Venus in Leo have “a genuine liking for people and radiate friendliness so everyone can feel comfortable around them. This seems to
be somewhat more true for men than for women (with Venus in Leo), but for both sexes it can sometimes be that they put people off by coming on too strong. The women seem to get a little more stuck on the Leo vanity expression of their femininity; they sometimes try to impress others with their knowledge of ‘chic’ and ‘class.’ … I’ve seen cases of Venus in Leo where the person is ultra-control-oriented or manipulative of others in close relationships and whose needs for recognition and to always hold the upper hand can hold them back terribly in terms of carrying on a successful one-to-one relationship. They have tremendous amounts of pride, and are very sexual too” (AQ).

Venus in Sagittarius

Those with Venus in the freedom-loving, open-minded sign of Sagittarius are very fun-loving, idealistic people. A rather happy-go-lucky, philosophical attitude underpins lots of their reactions to life and also explains how buoyant and resilient they are when confronted by life’s vicissitudes. Others may call it shallowness or lack of emotional depth or evasion of true feelings. Take your pick, but it’s hard to stay mad at them when they disappoint. Although they are often fickle and hard to please, one should not take personally their chronic lack of satisfaction. They simply have a hard time dealing with routine, tedious reality, and facing the practical facts and limits of life. They don’t like living in the present. It is hard for them to settle down to one lifestyle or one relationship, for they always crave a brighter, distant horizon, something or somewhere that is invariably
beyond
 the immediate status quo. In their view there is always room for improvement, and optimistically thinking that a better reality exists just over the hill is virtually a philosophy of life. These folks love surprises, and you can expect anything from them at any time—just to put a little spirit into life’s dull moments. A sense of freedom and space is essential for them:
mental and intellectual space (learning, discussion, debate) and physical spaciousness (outdoor activities, sports, travel). These folks are rather contradictory in many frustrating and often irritating ways: the ideals of all kinds that they love to preach seem to apply mainly to others, whereas they rationalize their own failings; they judge others for moral shortfalls, whereas their own licentious or hurtful behavior is just viewed as part of life’s grand variety that must be broad-mindedly tolerated; they are brutally honest about revealing others’ hypocrisy or negligence, whereas they react indignantly to blunt truths about themselves that pierce their own inflated self-image. In short, many of Sagittarius Venus people’s faults and problems in relationships come from their habitual focus on a distant vision and their consequent inability to see what is right in front of them.

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 V
ENUS IN
 S
AGITTARIUS

After more than thirty-five years of working with astrological psychology, the core psychological qualities I find most accurate for Sagittarius are:

Expresses affection freely, enthusiastically, generously, and idealistically, motivated by an innate trust and faith in life and love’s blessings.

A restless urge for freedom, adventure, and always anticipating something better can interfere with establishing lasting close relationships.

Tolerant and broad-minded attitude toward love with a need for philosophical harmony; values honesty but may insensitively overlook others’ feelings.

Men and women with Venus in Sagittarius both want to be heard, or at least overtly noticed, in any social or interpersonal situation. And in a close relationship, the sharing of ideas and a sort of philosophical
harmony is necessary for it to last. But for it to last even one evening, there also has to be some appreciation of the other’s humor or a type of mutual entertainment, because Venus in Sagittarius people really cannot tolerate boredom and are naturally high-spirited. For these folks, love is an adventurous game. Exploring each other and the interaction’s potential is exhilarating, and one way of doing that is blunt, probing questions and daring, challenging, teasing statements. Honesty is of course valued above all by these people, but in their pursuit of that ideal, they can be extremely insensitive and dismissive of others’ feelings and needs. Casual remarks often do real damage and can reveal a surprisingly irresponsible, self-centered attitude toward love and sex.

Women with Venus in Sagittarius are tremendously enthusiastic and open-minded about love and sex initiatives. Their attitude and actions could sometimes even be called celebrational, full of laughter and enjoyment and playfulness. Fun, yes, but one can never expect flattery or praise from these women. You’ve got to earn your own plaudits and glow with self-confidence to intrigue these women. And make sure you retain their
respect
, for any obvious violation of the high ideals you may proclaim will be immediately noticed and used to lower you a notch in their cosmic book. This Venus placement in women is found in many who are particularly impulsive and generous with their affections; they can be quite demonstrative, but they are also rather impersonal in approaching relationships, in spite of their obvious charms. Being a lighthearted “pal” comes more naturally than being a truly passionate, intense lover; and their granting the other person huge amounts of freedom reveals that they also want to maintain a certain personal distance and independence, without restraint or heavy expectations.

Men with this Venus placement have many of the characteristics just described and also want someone who is idealistic and upstanding, in the sense that they can reliably count on that person’s decency and good intentions. They also are attracted to someone who is venturesome
and who has a broad outlook on life. Needless to say, honesty is a requirement, and they also need a partner who is upbeat, positive thinking, and preferably humorous. A dour or somber person who takes the details or setbacks of life too heavily will squeeze the life out of these men.

The following quotations from numerous interviews, questionnaire responses, and writings give even more insight into Venus in Sagittarius:

“Your heart is unafraid,” as Grant Lewi writes, “you follow your instincts to the last outpost of experience.… You see no reason why emotions aren’t made to be followed, and you are among the most impulsive of the children of Venus” (AFM).

Tiffany Holmes observes the following about Sagittarius Venus in her book,
Women’s Astrology:
 “An abundant head of hair, a twinkle in the eye, and a memorable laugh are what others find most attractive in the Venus in Sagittarius person. A sense of humor is of paramount importance to the person having Venus in Sagittarius, and a light-hearted banter as well as shared ethical principles play significant roles.… This sign placement for Venus may also discount the importance of material things if the current love interest cannot offer them” (WA).

One woman observed that “men with Venus in Sagittarius are ‘fun’; they enjoy being around people, talking and having a good time. They like women who are independent and not too possessive or demanding, but who like to follow their lead and be ‘pals.’ They’re generally open to just about anything, so they don’t get hung up on trends or set ways of thinking or acting. They’re very friendly and have a ‘bon vivant’ quality to them. Women with Venus in Sagittarius have charisma; people pay attention to them when they walk into a room” (AQ).

Another female questionnaire respondent touched on how central inspirational or religious aspirations are when she wrote that her Venus in Sagittarius “is the reason I need someone who will give me the freedom to be myself and express myself. Someone who understands my spiritual needs as well as my emotional and physical needs. And above all, someone who is open and honest with me” (AQ).

Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

10

Venus in the Earth Signs

When Venus is in any of the Earth signs, there is a noticeable patience, a careful, systematic approach to life and relationships, and an innate attunement to the rhythms of nature. Those with Venus in Earth take time to express their feelings, but once they have committed themselves, they tend to remain resolute, dutiful, and solid as the earth. Having a dependable life structure as a basis for emotional security is paramount with these folks, and they seek to arrive at that goal both through self-control and also through controlling everything and everyone else around them as much as possible. There is an essential conservatism in their approach to relationships, which may not be spontaneous nor always imaginatively stimulating; but the dependability and pleasure in shared responsibilities are often ample reward for them because they see this as steady progress toward building a joint lifestyle. (The reader should review the appropriate section in
Chapter 8
 for additional information about the qualities of Venus in this element.)

Other keys to understanding Venus in the Earth signs are as follows:

For those with Venus in Earth, physical matter is ultimately real to them, and fulfilling obvious practical and security needs is a major component of what motivates them in relationships. Early in the relationship, these people automatically try to categorize the potential that the new person has and inevitably make judgments about whether future investments of time will be worthwhile.

Tuning in to a rhythm of natural life always becomes apparent in relationships with those who have Venus in Earth, for encountering these people provides a dense experience of physical life that
slows things down
 and makes one live in the present.

If one is not particularly attuned to the Earth element, one should be aware that seeking advantage over or control of the other person comes naturally to those with Venus in Earth. Seeking to advance oneself through relationships and to “own” as much money, power, security, attention, or fame as possible comes naturally to many with this Venus element. To what extent it dominates depends on what other major factors the person has in the birth chart, as well as his or her personal ideals.

Venus in Taurus

Those with Venus in the steady, grounded, pleasure-oriented sign of Taurus are particularly focused on comfort and harmony. Indeed, Venus is the traditional “ruling planet” of this sign, and when Venus is located here there is a great need for harmony, comfort, pleasure, and beauty. Sometimes their taste is excellent—an appreciation of classic symmetry; and in other cases they have a propensity toward the gaudy and ultra-luxurious. Much depends on their background but also on the other factors in their charts. In any case, they are very concerned with appearance—both in dress and in how their home appears. Material security is a major focus of their attention and desires, sometimes to the point of being noticeably greedy or stingy. These folks can be infinitely patient, kind, and sustaining for others, giving them encouragement, affection, and food for body and spirit; at their best, they are pleasingly sociable and also quite artistic. At worst, they can be among the most possessive, oppressive, stubborn, grasping, indolent people on the earth that they are so attached to! They can become very mean
and irritable if
unsatisfied
 in a major way, because they want to
have
 their pleasure or object of desire.

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 V
ENUS IN
 T
AURUS

The core psychological qualities and tendencies for a Taurus Venus that I have distilled over the past thirty-five years are as follows:

Expresses affection physically, warmly, steadily, and possessively.

Intimate exchanges may be hampered by a reluctance to release feelings or lose control.

Values material comfort and beautiful physical objects; deeply appreciates physical sensations: sight, sound, smell, taste, and touch.

Men and women with Taurus Venus have a winning, considerate manner (at least in public and when in a good mood), and they often have an especially pleasing voice, either in conversation or song. As Tiffany Holmes wrote in
Woman’s Astrology
, “The sign gives a need for continuity and comfort in love relationships.… The person having Venus in Taurus simply settles into quiet routines involving at least one other person—[however] the other person may not
want
 to be assumed to be available every Friday night.” They like “love” but prefer regularity rather than surprise and are often rather lazy and not very imaginative or experimental. Possessiveness colors their affections strongly, and they hold on for a long while, taking a long time to get over relationships that don’t work out. Their
retentiveness
 about money and possessions extends to their reluctance to let go in expressing their real feelings. In other words, although gently affectionate and attentive to practical needs in most cases, these folks are rarely emotionally demonstrative.

Women with Venus in Taurus expect to be pampered and wooed. Any form of hurry in the relationship’s development or in seduction
turns them off. They expect a man to dress well, to cater to their desires, and to pay for everything. What’s he good for if not providing material comfort and security, after all? These women have a great need for physical contact and touch, which affects their moods greatly. Sex for them is not sentimental or mental, but extremely sensual and an end in itself. Usually quite passive in sexual exchanges, unless lots of Aries or Scorpio perhaps is also accented in the chart, they prefer prolonged sexual encounters that need not have much variety so long as the experience is deeply instinctual.

Venus in Taurus men have a quality of patience and interpersonal rapport that smoothes over even a very aggressive personality. The simple pleasures of life slow them down, and perhaps more than any other men, they love comforting routines that they can savor. They are usually attracted to an earthy, solid, reliable type of woman—someone whom they can depend on, for care and feeding in particular. They don’t mind if she has some intelligence and personality, but more basic qualities are higher priorities.

Among the most insightful quotes, interview statements, and questionnaire replies I have found that illuminate Venus in Taurus are these:

Debbi Kempton Smith clearly describes their attraction to often lavish appearances: “They see God in material things—nice furniture, pretty boxes, your flesh and bones.… The boys like their girls in dresses, and the girls want the boys dressed up real sharp.… Here greed is seen as a delicious virtue. They love The Good Life” (SSN).

Grant Lewi has a unique take on this Venus sign: “You understand love deeply; accept it with your whole being, are confident to express it wordlessly, value love’s silences rather than its speeches. You know both the ecstasy and despair of love; and you cling to a love once given and received with unbelievable tenacity, even in the face of
great obstacles.… Your emotional responses color your whole life; all sense reactions are intensified by this position” (AFM).

A female questionnaire respondent finds Venus in Taurus women to be “very expansive, possessing a combination of earthy sensuality and a sense of grace and beauty (aesthetics) which emanates depth and texture. They’re very comfortable with their physical selves, and in tune with their physical surroundings. They know how to make other people comfortable, and enjoy nurturing. Most tend to be artists of some sort: musicians, painters, dancers, etc.” (AQ).

Another questionnaire says of a woman with both Mars and Venus in Taurus: “She definitely has a problem with material goods. Once she has something, she cannot bear to part with it, including borrowed items and even birthday gifts she has bought for someone (she usually keeps them for herself). Environment cannot explain this, as she came from a very wealthy family” (AQ).

Venus in Taurus can be “very laid-back about relationships, inwardly very involved, but very reluctant to let their feelings show. It’s kind of like they are a little scared to have their feelings exposed. They pretend not to care and then put up a wall in a flash. Very security conscious, but stubbornly unable to allow the feeling’s expression. Once they become attached to a man they tend to stay with him through thick and thin” (AQ).

Venus in Virgo

Those with Venus in the analytical, precise, efficient sign of Virgo find in their own contradictions and complications plenty of material to feed their voracious love of analysis. The unfortunate part of this tendency is that it often leads to excessive criticism of both self and others and to a great deal of self-consciousness—hardly a recipe for spontaneous, relaxed living or loving. Although these folks want a
healthy, helpful, faithful relationship, they have an even greater love in life: work and duty. Another of their dualities is the tension between sensuality and puritanism. They never seem quite sure if things are morally, aesthetically, or sexually perfect enough, or whether they should acknowledge the fact that they really
are
 one of the Earth signs and thus should accept the sensual part of their nature. Often they needlessly create tension in themselves through denial or suppression of their physical needs. This comes in part from their innate modesty and natural tendency to focus on
imperfections
 rather than the whole picture in all aspects of life. As Julia Parker wrote in her excellent
The Astrologer’s Handbook
, “There is a tendency with this placement to underestimate the powers of attraction, and the inherent modesty of this sign will be expressed in a genuine shyness when it comes to developing relationships” (AHB). She points out that communication and friendship need to be part of a good relationship for these folks and that they “must consciously learn to relax into their relationships.” The ultimate assessment of Virgo Venus in my experience is that they need a relationship that incorporates
both mental and sensual communication
.

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 V
ENUS IN
 V
IRGO

The core psychological qualities I find most accurate for Venus in Virgo are expressed as follows:

Expresses affection matter-of-factly, modestly, helpfully, practically, and timidly.

Petty criticisms or a natural reserve can interfere with emotional give-and-take and the expression of passion in relationships.

A need to serve and be useful gives emotional satisfaction; finds pleasure in precise attention to details and analytical mental activity.

Both men and women with Venus in Virgo can be the most honest, dutiful, helpful partners in the world once they are fully committed to a relationship, which means that the partner has to have passed some very exacting tests. If the Virgo Venus individual has
accepted
 the other person (faults and all!) and has relaxed his or her sharply critical attitudes in the service of a meaningful love, this person can be a disciplined, reliable lover and partner without peer. They are especially helped to achieve this if they have another planet in one of the Water signs, particularly Pisces, which balances off the Virgo prissiness and coldness with Piscean tolerance and sympathy. These people are usually rather fastidious about appearance and also certain possessions, but they absolutely have to learn that a partner is not a possession. They are quite methodical and cautious in their approach to relationships, for Virgo is an essentially passive and quite introverted sign. They are slow to awaken to a relationship’s possibilities and prone to doubting their own attractiveness or value. They seem to have mastered self-criticism! They are, however, often unusually appreciative of affection from others when they do experience it since they do not generally have great confidence or broad experience in that area of life. But sometimes, due to their narrowness, they tend to overestimate the breadth of their romantic and sexual experience. They worry constantly about love affairs and seem constantly confused about how they really feel about someone, and they frequently create problems through excessive analysis.

Women with Venus in Virgo seem to never stop analyzing their relationships and sometimes keep a historical log or journal of their romantic experiences. One must play by the rules to kindle a relationship with these ladies. A mental rapport must be established before she can be interested in romance. They are physically sensitive, although rarely impulsively passionate. A methodical, clinical approach to love-making can cause too much focus on technique; and when everything proceeds from mental concepts there is not much
spontaneity and certainly not a relaxed inclination to lose oneself in the experience. In fact they often talk about sex more than doing anything about it. One factor I have frequently observed in Virgo Venus women is their constant references to their “promiscuous” past, which they seem to exaggerate. They never seem to mention a promiscuous present, perhaps trying to preserve the virginal female ego of Virgo, the sign of the virgin.

Venus in Virgo men generally want a proper woman—someone neat, intellectual, disciplined, and probably one who is career oriented. As one man’s questionnaire response stated, “Venus in Virgo men don’t like women who are identified with having sexual prowess. They like women to have a ‘clean look.’ ” Debbi Kempton Smith points out that these men want women to dress neatly or be as meticulous as models, or to be someone who is really in love with her work. She says another image that attracts these guys is “the well-scrubbed lady in the long skirt cooking health foods” (SSN). Similar to women with Venus in Virgo, these fellows seem to have little perspective on love and relationships. By defining love and sex too narrowly and perfectly, they inadvertently create a problem and a source of disappointment and/or guilt. Getting realistic and clear about their true desires, needs, and capacities for relationship is the only remedy.

The following quotes from writings, interviews, and questionnaire responses paint an even more detailed picture of how Virgo Venus is expressed:

What one man with Virgo Venus said in an interview applies to both sexes who have this placement: “Love feelings are awakened by my feeling helpful and able to give practical support to the other person’s needs, especially if they have suffered lately. I can then feel that I’m contributing something genuinely therapeutic” (AI).

Mary Coleman, author of
Picking Your Perfect Partner
, assesses the basic love nature of Venus in Virgo as follows: “Responses to love
and affection are cool, appraising and thoughtful. Practical considerations get mixed up with the hearts and flowers material to the point that everyone wilts before anyone surrenders. But later some recipients may decide a unanimous ‘Yes’ vote
was
 worth waiting for” (PIC).

One woman with Venus in Virgo said, “I could meet the most attractive man in the world, but if he only said a few words and couldn’t communicate, I wouldn’t touch him” (AI).

Many interviews established that guilt or pity often plays a role in motivating these folks to show affection to another. Other interviewees confirmed that they were slow to awaken to strong erotic feelings. (AI)

Grant Lewi further describes Venus in Virgo: “You relate emotional experience to a well-organized code of conduct.… Your belief in the proprieties is so strong that you just don’t have emotions that would involve their violation.… Small niceties are emotionally important to you, and if you’re not careful, you will substitute the sentimentalities of love for love itself.… Your sense of good taste is your emotional censor, and will keep you from many unconventionalities …” (AFM).

“Venus in Virgo men are emotionally practical. They tend to know what they need in terms of relationship and to find it and then stick to it. They’re usually somewhat shy and don’t reveal themselves too easily, and have a tendency to be ‘picky’; they’re hard to satisfy because they’re perfectionists, and seem to demand perfection in their women and in themselves” (AQ).

A female questionnaire respondent who was married to a man with Venus in Virgo very frankly states: “Men with Venus in Virgo tend toward prudishness. They treat love-making as ceremonial at the onset—almost like a religious thing. Everything must be perfect—no
backseat romance for these boys. They shun crudeness—even what I consider honesty—and have a fairy-tale view of sex, where we all have Barbie and Ken doll bodies. Another male friend with Venus in Virgo didn’t lose his virginity for a long time, even though he did everything but ‘the act.’ He was waiting for everything to be ‘just right.’ For years, even though he let houseguests use his bed, he would request that no one make love in it because he was saving not only himself but his
bed
 as well for the perfect act.… Ironically they do not seem concerned with serving their partner’s needs—the Virgo seems to come through (rather) as a need for order and perfection” (AQ).

Venus in Capricorn

Those with Venus in the eminently practical, matter-of-fact sign of Capricorn are very serious and careful about anything that affects their emotions, reputation, or material security. Many do have a good sense of humor, but it tends to be dry or expressed with a certain restraint. I have known a few people with this placement who never really laugh at all. There is a great deal of self-control and self-containment. The deeper emotions are rather hard to express, and they would rather show their feelings or appreciation for another person through action or duty than through verbalization. They have a capacity to strengthen others through their solid commitment and affection, and they instinctively want to do a great deal to support their partners and to make them successful. They are proud of their reliability and old-fashioned loyalty—even if they discreetly violate it now and then. Their somewhat formal manner serves as a mode of protection, which their rather insecure, defensive nature requires. This formality manifests also as a traditional, somewhat predictable approach to social interactions; as one Capricorn Venus woman said, “I’m very conscious of how my relationship looks to my friends” (AI). Many like to show
off their loved one in a surprisingly demonstrative way, almost as a status symbol. Capricorn is a very impersonal sign—quite detached; and having Venus here often implies a somewhat predictable, mechanical approach to all interpersonal activities. One woman with this placement even said in an interview, “I’m not really personally involved in my own life.” That approach must be the ultimate in detachment!
Authority
 and
control
 are two other keynotes of the sign Capricorn, and they are expressed here as a need to have some kind of control over other people in most types of relationship or to recognize issues of power in interpersonal dealings. They will use others and may find it difficult to meet others on an equal one-to-one level, unless their roles are clearly defined. The natural attunement to
authority
 also manifests in how easily the women (especially) with this position can fall for authority figures (coach, boss, etc.), and how both sexes have an innate ambition to achieve, gain power, and accumulate influence, respectability, or money. Both sexes often have a particular fixation on the father—a common obsession for people with any number of major factors in Capricorn. The desire for
control
 is also noticeable in how the women organize their households. They can, for example, move into a new house or apartment and the next day have it looking like they’ve lived there for years!

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 V
ENUS IN
 C
APRICORN

Many years’ experience observing those with Venus in Capricorn has led me to formulate this summary of their core psychological qualities:

Expresses affection cautiously, seriously, dutifully, and mechanically, with perseverance.

A need for self-control and emotional reserve may give an aloof, impersonal demeanor that can inhibit the development of close relationships.

Ambitious, conservative, and concerned with reputation; capable of loyalty and of facing the work and responsibilities of a committed relationship.

In both men and women, their natural caution leads them to pursue safety and to require some kind of commitment (at least lots of
time
) before they feel comfortable getting involved in a relationship. Otherwise, they may feel severely depressed if they have not met their own standards for propriety and self-respect. Although they are quite conscious of conventional behavior and social image and thus rarely are publicly demonstrative or daring, one should not doubt their capacity for deep earthy passion and sincere, physically expressed feelings. They simply feel that such intimate and deeply personal aspects of life are too meaningful, and make them too vulnerable, to express them casually. In fact, this is one astrological placement that delivers far more than they promise, both emotionally and sexually. But they do not want to be embarrassed or humiliated; they want a faithful, secure relationship within which to share the serious intimacies of life. Both the men and women are often attracted to someone older, socially superior, or in a position of authority. Venus in Capricorn is not above marrying for material security—or at least postponing or denying themselves a certain relationship because of the lack of a financial foundation. One limiting result of their conservatism is that the narrowness and rigidity of their “practical” views and their attachment to traditional images often prompt them to cut off potentially fulfilling relationships. This can be self-defeating and ultimately impractical, resulting in loneliness.

Women with Venus in Capricorn view all intimate relationships so seriously that they often worry over them far too much, adding a burden to them that can become oppressive. Once having made a commitment, these ladies tend to be honest, sincere, and loyal—sometimes to the point of self-abnegation or burdening themselves with an
unsatisfying relationship just because they remain solidly devoted out of principle. The depth of commitment these women naturally adopt also runs parallel to the depth of desires they have within themselves; they can be very demanding of affection, sometimes to the point where the other person may find it utterly depressing and eventually may react with a type of rejection. Women with Venus in this sign combine an authoritative, take-charge attitude with a preference for a relatively traditional approach to sex. This seeming contradiction is evident throughout all of the research. If acting too passively, they become insecure and uneasy with how emotionally vulnerable they may be; yet, even if sometimes rather mechanical, a summation of the research available seems to indicate that they get more complete sexual satisfaction from basic intercourse than perhaps any other Venus sign. They like being “taken” and having a deep experience at the archetypal level of nature’s polarized energies. And they may constitute a good number of the supposed 30 percent of women (according to one survey) who say they have had an orgasm through basic intercourse alone. But, in spite of their passion, they are also able to go through long periods of celibacy.

Venus in Capricorn men, in addition to sharing the serious attitudes just described, are often attracted to a woman who is quite formal, aloof, and conventional. They often find an older woman and/or one successful in her own right quite intriguing and worthy of special respect and admiration. Not sentimental at all, they very rarely reveal their feelings, even to themselves! Maintaining control, or at least the appearance of it, in love and sex relationships is imperative for these men. The woman also must exemplify self-control for her to continue to intrigue him, and in addition, he must respect the woman and her values to feel much love for her.

Among the most perceptive insights into Venus in Capricorn are these from a variety of sources:

Debbi Kempton Smith intersperses her usual quips with sharp insights in her comments on Venus in Capricorn: “Don’t ever show affection in public, and don’t keep your hands off them in private. Venus in Capricorn folks … are fascinated with bone structure. Your body is the way they like to study architecture. [They] are attracted to moody loners, and you can never be too rich or too skinny.… Weirdly, they are more often exploited themselves because they feel inadequate about their own worth. So they may try to ‘buy’ you in some way.… Both sexes believe that the best partners won’t be theirs unless they ‘earn’ the partner’s devotion. This pound-wise, penny-foolish attitude on their part wrecks many budding romances.… Venus in Capricorn people need two things only: a kick in the pants for being too worrisome and mercenary, and lots and lots of kisses along the spine. These weird, frosty people are lusty beyond belief” (SSN).

A woman with Venus in Capricorn who also had Moon in Taurus said: “I’m concerned about material security.… The deciding factor when I married my ex-husband was all the land he would inherit. I couldn’t stand being married to him” (AQ).

A thirty-year-old female with Venus in Capricorn reports: “In order for me to feel emotionally attuned to a situation or to another person, I need a sense of structure. By being able to order my environment, especially in the midst of a chaotic episode, I gain a sense of security that allows me to express what I’m feeling.… I’m relatively cautious about opening up to other people. I tend to be very distant and detached with strangers; not talkative and involved, even superficially. Then as I get to know people better I become a lot more spontaneous, but still guarded and easily defensive. My capacity to express my love for someone doesn’t seem to really open up until I feel some commitment from them; I have to trust that people are serious in their intentions toward me and are going to stick
around before I really let loose. My feelings for others also seem to develop gradually and expand inwardly instead of outwardly in open displays. As far as the aesthetic qualities of Venus in Capricorn go, I am attracted to and value things that are beautiful, but have a purpose or function beyond being simply decorative. I like things to be practical but creative, and I’m real big on
texture:
 things that affect me emotionally that I can touch” (AQ).
