HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 M
ARS IN
 S
CORPIO

A summary of core psychological qualities for Mars in Scorpio that I have formulated over the last few decades are:

Asserts self intensely, magnetically, passionately, and powerfully.

Physical energy and initiative are prompted by strong desires, compulsions, and challenges; capable of great endurance.

Sex urge is motivated by the need to share deep emotional closeness and to experience profound intensity; secretiveness and the need for self-protection and total control can hinder decisiveness and freedom of expression.

Both men and women with Scorpio Mars are always quick to react to anything, although—given their love of self-control—they hate to show it; but they do explode at times when particularly on edge or when anger has been building up for some time. These folks have an incredible sense of self-protection, which usually has the accuracy of
a sixth sense, although sometimes it leads them to be quite suspicious and paranoid. A psychic sensitivity to any plan that will affect them is common among these people; it is as if they put out psychic feelers into the environment or into others’ subconscious minds, giving them the ability to determine solutions to problems, solve mysteries, see the fault in any strategy, and even find lost objects! Naturally, their acute sensitivity, chronic suspicion, and occasional explosiveness make close relationships a real test of their self-control—especially over their emotions. If slighted, their instinct is to act vengeful, as if they could remedy their hurt or sense of injustice by
attacking
. They take sex especially seriously, and they are often rather conflicted about it. They have an early awareness of sex and feel it powerfully within themselves, but often they hate its hold over them and the fact that it is one thing they cannot control. Hence, some of these folks try to suppress the instinct, and the accompanying emotions, to an extreme degree. Yet, the fact that they try to deny it or make it a taboo amplifies their intrigue about sexual experience. Sex opens doors for them to the kind of deep communication that they crave, and it is one thing in life that finally lets them get out of control in a releasing, relaxing way. Most people with this placement have a strong attraction to the emotional intensity of sex and the experience of being overwhelmed by such powerful fundamental life energies, and some regard a sexual relationship as an opportunity to explore a further dimension of feeling, which sometimes leads to personal transformation.

Women with Mars in Scorpio are intensely amorous sexually and need regular demonstration that they are desired. Some of them have the personal magnetism that the men with this placement so often exude. They are always strong willed and determined, with a definite strategy that they follow in all things, although they rarely reveal it. This will and desire to control lead to a conflict in some cases because these women want a strong man as a partner, but many also have an urge to dominate, not just share, in the relationship. They are quite
possessive and jealous (sometimes in an angry, resentful way), and they want to feel closely bonded to the partner. As one man wrote about Scorpio Mars women in a questionnaire, “A one-man woman who doesn’t like to change partners; very sensual with an intense energy” (AQ). These women are powerhouses who can be extremely capable in any field, from healing, to athletics, to business. They are tremendously resourceful in everything, including running a household. Their sheer power does intimidate some men, but this is just as well since they really want someone who is self-sufficiently strong and confident. They are attracted to men who are intense and passionate, and who demonstrate that they care even through a bit of jealousy. Some of these women actually don’t want too much independence but rather prefer to know that they are needed a great deal, which makes them feel desirable and appreciated.

Men with Scorpio Mars usually exude a certain charisma, a magnetic aura of power and sexuality. The best example I have seen personally was the late senator and presidential candidate Robert Kennedy, whose Mars and Sun were both in Scorpio and whose energy field and aura were remarkable—even at the distance of about ten to fifteen yards at which I was standing. Because these people radiate such powerful energy, they elicit strong—and often extreme—responses from others. They tend either to repel people quickly or to attract them immediately. Generally forceful, decisive, and somewhat impatient, they can be difficult to deal with at times; but in an emergency or extremely demanding situation, they may be the best people to have on your side. With their capacities for thoroughness and problem solving, combined with their drive and intuition, many are successful in business and investing; but they are also particularly suited to detective work, scientific research, and a broad array of healing arts. The Scorpio Mars man has been termed “a self-satisfied soul” by one writer, in reference to the fact that they resist anyone who tries to change them. Because these guys are very stubborn in their ways—and
usually more so as they get older—only a very clever, sympathetic, and patient partner will succeed in making them see and then adopt other viewpoints or behavior patterns. They may grow and change on their own, especially if they pursue some self-transforming path of personal development (as they often do), but they instinctively resist manipulation by others. One man’s questionnaire response particularly emphasized that men with this placement manifest “powerful energy reserves” (including sexual energy) and that they have the “energy to bounce back” from adversities.

The following quotes from interviews, questionnaire replies, and other sources fill out the Scorpio Mars portrait:

A questionnaire reply by a woman commented very precisely (and accurately, in my view) on some characteristics that one finds in people of both sexes with this placement: “I have known a lot of these people. They are some of the most intense individuals around. Absolute perfectionists where work is concerned, extremely passionate about everything with strong likes and dislikes. Violent and destructive when angry, and slow to forgive (sometimes never). Over-indulgence in all sensory experiences” (AQ).

In an interview, a mostly Air and Fire woman in her mid-thirties gave this assessment of Scorpio Mars men after having known a couple and having lived with one for more than a decade. The keyword that kept recurring to her throughout the interview was
intensity: “Very heavy!
 They glom on to you and
hold
 and don’t allow much space for the woman to project her own self or assertiveness. Dominating, and greedy and demanding for sex—which for them is connected with conquest, control, pain, depth, and desperation. These men usually seem full of jealousy and conflict—very insecure” (AI).

A questionnaire from a woman about Scorpio Mars men also used the word
conquest
, but in another context: “Their intense gaze can
be felt for yards upon the part of the body being stared at—a large energy field. Intense in pursuit, jealous, shrewd; surprising withdrawals while planning attack, great at conquest in business” (AQ). Another woman who had Venus and Mars in Scorpio memorably characterized her ideal lover as follows: “I want someone to get in there and totally take me apart” (AI).

Tiffany Holmes refers specifically to the extremism of Mars in Scorpio: “The obsessiveness of Scorpio can magnify outrages and sexual fixations, granting much more importance to the objects of these passions than they may deserve. Expressions of anger can be sarcastic in the extreme.… It often seems that the choices are to struggle or to swear off sex altogether” (WA).

Here’s a remarkably acute and perceptive assessment of Scorpio Mars by Grant Lewi: “It can be the best or the worst of all possible things to find in the chart. In its best form the energies flow into channels that make for deep personal security, through business and love. Magnetism is powerful; the vital forces serve the ego forcefully to draw others and hold them, and to extract from the world the maximum of material and spiritual benefits. In its worst form, the same security urges turn to fear that the ends of the ego will not be achieved, and the native seeks escape in a variety of undesirable forms.… Your constant aim must be self control—to direct your energies and your habits away from yourself into objective action; to draw other people to you, and to hold them, with love rather than fear; to avoid the negative destructive instincts of jealousy, fear, suspicion; to achieve faith in yourself, your world and the people who inhabit it with you. Your energies are an almost tangible force emanating from you in a sort of aura; you make yourself felt wherever you are; and can genuinely build up or tear down another person by what you think about him. This is because your instincts are so powerful that you show in movement, action and tone what
you inwardly are believing and you are nearly as effective in silence as in speech” (AFM).

Mars in Pisces

Those with Mars in the fluid, sympathetic, multi-directional sign of Pisces are among the most sympathetic and intuitive of all the Mars signs, but they have habitual trouble defining what they themselves want to achieve. Their aims are invariably affected by their ideals and by their hard-to-define sense of mission in life, and these subjective influences are often quite unconscious. In addition, when you find the planet of self-assertion in the most sensitive, compassionate sign of the zodiac, you have individuals who moderate their expression of self with an underlying kindness and sympathetic awareness of how others will feel about anything they do. These folks generally devote themselves toward their work with dedication, showing little need for recognition. Their unobtrusive, self-abnegating
modus operandi
 is a rarity in the world today, although it may hold them back professionally if they too effectively “hide their light under a basket.” Another crucial factor in understanding any planet in Pisces is the yearning to escape from mundane, oppressive “reality.” And so with Mars in this sign, we observe a strong drive to escape, which can manifest in many ways. It can be channeled into love, romance, and sex—or into fantasies associated with these realms of experience. Alternately, it can be expressed through artistic endeavors, creating forms that demonstrate a strong sensitivity and reveal the yearnings of their imagination. Or it can be focused on spiritual aspirations following any number of different paths. So much depends on what other factors are strongest in the birth chart that it is especially difficult to generalize about this Mars placement. People with Mars in Pisces can be inspirational or “dissipational,” devoted to selfless service and idealistic causes or seeking to lose themselves through escapist activities or even mind-altering
substances. Some are focused on a high ideal, and others are totally scattered and diffuse their energies in unproductive ways. This is an extremely sensitive placement for usually forceful Mars, and these folks are often quite emotional (although they may hide it well—especially the men). They frequently have healing powers and natural charitable instincts. Although these people are reputed to have low physical energy, as evidenced in a couple of quotes at the end of this section, it is more accurate to say that their energy fluctuates. Much also depends on how the Mars placement is integrated with the rest of the individual’s birth chart. For example, two of the most graceful and intuitive athletes of recent decades have Mars in Pisces: Björn Borg, who dominated tennis for a number of years (including five Wimbledon championships), and Bernard King, a spectacular All-Star pro basketball player who was a dominant scorer for many years.

HE
 E
SSENCE OF
 M
ARS IN
 P
ISCES

Although virtually everything about Pisces is difficult to verbalize, the most comprehensive expression of Pisces Mars’ core psychological qualities can be condensed in these phrases:

Asserts self idealistically, empathetically, agreeably, with underlying kindness.

Initiative and willpower are colored by sensitivity and compassion for others; pursues one’s desires subtly, motivated by inspiration, intuition, or a guiding vision.

Physical energy and sexual drive are always affected by dreams, moods, and emotions; self-assertion and decisiveness are encumbered by substantial personal and emotional vulnerability.

Men and women with Mars in Pisces share an especially cautious, sometimes socially timid
modus operandi
. Not being all that sure of themselves or of their attractiveness or even of what they really want,
they approach new initiatives, including relationships, subtly, intuitively, and tentatively while they “test the waters” and sense the reaction of the other person. Their natural inclination is to strive toward their goals by indirect means, not exposing themselves unnecessarily to emotional injury. When considering a potential partner, they have to make certain that the other person is sufficiently responsive and respectful of their own sensitive nature before they are comfortable opening up about their affectionate feelings. Mars in Pisces is a sensuous (but not terribly sexual) placement, and the idealistic focus is more on dreamy romance than on intense physical passion. Those of both sexes with Pisces Mars tend to be extremely romantic and often involved in fantasy, sometimes to the point of living in a fantasy world or expressing an artistic or literary talent that springs from their imaginative activities. Acutely conscious from an early age of their own vulnerability and hypersensitive emotional reactions, these people are thus aware of others’ emotional needs and also are the least assertive of all the Mars signs. They tend to disguise the expression of their desires or to say them so quietly or to imply them so indirectly that no one notices. Later, they can become angry and resentful that no one is paying attention to what they want or need.

Women with Mars in Pisces are by nature not particularly assertive about what they want in any area of life, including in relationships and sex. But they very much want to
please
 and they prefer not to have to
say
 anything to give the other a clue as to how they feel or what they want. They prefer to have their partner intuit what they want or need. However, because of the unclear messages they send nonverbally, they often get pulled into things that they may have wanted to avoid. Basically far more emotional than physical, these women need to feel close to a partner and to feel appreciated, and they try exceptionally hard to show their devotion and caring to the partner. But because they have quite low confidence in their sexual attractiveness, they often display that timidity through an awkwardness in sexual expression, in spite of
their fervent attempts to please. These women are especially attracted to men who exhibit some dominant Pisces characteristic: spiritually oriented, artistically inspired, musical, spaced-out, lost and confused, or dreamy, visionary, and idealistic. Oddly enough, they are attracted to contradictory qualities in men: to those who are dependent and needy, but also to those who are protective, compassionate, self-sacrificing, and strong enough to woo her romantically. Although this attraction to contradictory qualities often leads to disappointment, when it works, it really generates romantic bliss.

Men with a Pisces Mars have a strong imagination, as well as a psychic and artistic sensitivity that is rare in males. At best, they have a definite softness and delicacy that they can express at the same time as they are being energetic and assertive. Women in a discussion group I led used the following words for Mars in Pisces men: “sweet, flexible, caring, touchy, affectionate.” These guys definitely like romance, mystery, and often semi-secret flirtations or fantasies; they like mysterious partners who intrigue them by stimulating their imagination. They like flirting and the company of women in general; yet, although seemingly intrigued by sexual attractions, they are often very passive and not particularly overwhelmed by or demonstrative of intense sexual desire.

Some further insights into Mars in Pisces are evident in the following book, questionnaire, and interview quotes:

A woman who has Mars in Pisces wrote the following in her questionnaire: “I hesitate quite a bit before acting.… the quality of my self-expression varies enormously. Sometimes I come on self-confidently and decisively, and sometimes meekly. This is an area of myself that I’ve been working on for quite a while now. There has been some improvement, but energy ebbs and flows a lot” (AQ).

Another woman wrote in a questionnaire: “Mars in Pisces women are idealistic to the point that they have a difficult time dealing with
mundane activities; they have to look at what they do as having a cosmic connection with a greater whole; they tend to get lost in the universal and have a hard time centering themselves in reality. They tend to like men who will take care of them and of the practical aspects of day-to-day existence” (AQ).

Much of the last interview excerpt can also be applied to many men with Mars in Pisces. They can often be rather unfocused in the here and now, and ungrounded in the physical world. As one woman stated in an interview about a Pisces Mars man she had had a relationship with, “It was like he wasn’t even there—no physical presence! It was hard to feel that he was really with me personally” (AI).

Grant Lewi points out an especially important characteristic of many people who have this Mars placement, one that it is useful to know how to adjust to: “Energy can waste itself in brooding, introspection, and fears, if you aren’t constantly on guard against these. You should select for yourself activities that don’t tax the physical body too much, and learn to follow the ebb and flow of your strength. You can in this way … go a long way, for when you are not working, you are storing up ideas and vitality in the conscious and subconscious that express later. But when you try to keep going all the time, you lose energy, magnetism, and ideas. You shouldn’t try for too much physical expression of love, for your energies don’t work here any more constantly than in other physical matters. When you’re tired, you aren’t worth much and tend to think badly of yourself. Master yourself by allowing plenty of time for rest and by being willing to go at a slower pace” (AFM).

Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

17

The “Aspects”: The Energy Interactions Between Planets

And think not you direct the course of love; for love, if it finds you worthy, directs your course.

—Kahlil Gibran

If one does not know to which port one is sailing, no wind is favorable.

—Seneca, First-Century Roman Writer and Philosopher

In analyzing any relationship, in addition to comparing the planets’ general energy attunements according to the element of the signs they are in, one can also assess the energy interaction of any two planets in a chart comparison much more specifically. That is to say, one can evaluate in depth, and with remarkable precision, the interaction between specific components of two people’s energy fields by comparing birth charts at a further level of detail that includes a study of the most exact “aspects”—the specific angular relationships between two planets recognized for centuries in traditional astrology. In fact, by looking at the angle that any two planets form in the zodiac, one can generally gauge the intensity of energy interaction in a reliable and striking way. These angular relationships and measurements, usually called
aspects
 or sometimes
interchart aspects
 when doing a comparison, are crucial in completing the process of tying together all the
factors in any individual birth chart, as well as in any comprehensive comparison between charts.

Obviously, introducing yet another factor into the mix of energies, symbols, and signs does add a new level of complexity (and possibly some confusion!) to the long list of factors we have already discussed: planets, elements, signs, the Ascendant, and so on. However, if you have made it this far in this rather wide-ranging book, you must have either a serious interest in astrological psychology or at least feel a rather compelling curiosity about the subject. And no doubt it is already evident how complex a science astrology is and how much time and effort it will take to become truly proficient at blending all the factors together into a coherent, useful “picture” of energy dynamics and interactions. In addition, until now I have focused primarily on the Moon, Venus, and Mars, with only brief references to the other personal planets and to the outer planets. In some of the examples in this chapter, however, I want to encourage you to broaden your familiarity with the other planets and to begin to experiment with all the planets and their mutual interactions. In addition to some specific phrases in Guideline B later in this chapter, I also advise the reader to study
Appendix A
 to get a feel for how all the planetary energies and principles may be expressed.

In this chapter, I will begin to explore the quite complex subject of aspects, particularly in regard to how they expand our understanding of energy interactions in a
chart comparison
. Using the basic guidelines explained below will enable even a novice in this subject to take the first steps toward doing a chart comparison. However, this is merely a quick and incomplete introduction to this subject. A more detailed and comprehensive treatment of the subject of aspects is far beyond the scope of this book. For a more thorough explanation of aspects, especially as used to understand individual birth charts, the reader should consult
Chapter 8
 of my
Chart Interpretation Handbook
. And for a more detailed, step-by-step guide to analyzing not only the
aspects but also the other facets of comprehensive chart comparisons, the reader should see my
Relationships & Life Cycles
.

However, before discussing the aspects’ definitions and meanings, I would like to dispel some of the misleading superstitions often associated with certain aspects. Because astrology is generally not taught in terms of a modern awareness of energy fields and flows, and because of the unquestioning hold over people that foolish fortune-telling delusions have, the majority of interpretations of aspects that the reader can find in books, in computer programs, and on websites are simplistic and unrealistic. Moreover, they are usually cast in a severely limited, black-and-white (good/bad, compatible/incompatible, success/failure) conceptual framework that has little practical value or reliability and often has negative psychological results in people’s lives. In addition, the words used in so many old-fashioned astrology books (and often in many books and Internet sites even today) are deterministic, crude, and oblivious of any complexity or subtlety in human experience or in close relationships—not to mention psychologically and emotionally destructive. As the noted author and health activist Anthony Robbins wrote, “The words we attach to our experience become our experience.” This should be obvious today to anyone purporting to give psychological advice, but somehow many people prefer childish, know-it-all assertions over honest evaluations of the known facts at hand. Hence, those pursuing astrological studies and/or seeking astrological advice should be alert to information sources or individuals that employ rigid, judgmental, or fatalistic language. One should immediately reject such sources on the basis that, even if one believes that some things in life are predetermined, the likelihood that the originators of such opinions are wise or spiritually evolved enough to know what is determined for
you
 is exceedingly slim.

It is for these reasons that I chose the two quotations at the beginning of this chapter as overall guidelines, particularly for their relevance to understanding relationships. For both quotations point to
the fact that there are far more overarching forces in love and life than can be found in any single astrological detail. As the great mystical poet Kahlil Gibran (author of
The Prophet
) advises, love itself will direct one’s course; and in that context one should view all astrological aspects between two people’s planets as part of a learning experience within the mystery of discovering love. The wisdom of Seneca’s quotation advises us that one needs to know
where one is headed
 before one can intelligently adapt to circumstances, and this applies to all life activities. But when applied to the emotionally charged subject of close relationships, this quote says to me that no type of compatibility or incompatibility, no type of easy or challenging relationship, will be worth much analysis if the two people do not have a common goal, ideal, or sense of direction. In other words, what kind of relationship do the two people want to experience or build? And within this broad view, a specific aspect between planets should be seen from the perspective of the overall pattern of energy interaction and within the context of the entire life plan.

Figure 17.1

This diagram of the zodiac, with the first sign Aries positioned where the Ascendant is always located, is called the natural chart. This version also includes the ruling planet(s) associated with each sign, as well as indicates the degrees along the circle of the zodiac starting with the first degree of Aries, where the Sun is located at the spring equinox. Count the number of degrees between any two points in the zodiac in order to determine the exact angle between them. Then use the following section of this chapter to decide if that angle represents one of the major aspects
.

The Major Aspects

Figures 17.2
 through
17.6
 depict the angles that are traditionally regarded as the “major aspects”—that is, the most significant and widely used interrelationships between two planets, or between a planet and the Ascendant. Note that all except the conjunction are multiples of 30 degrees, reflecting a pattern parallel to the 30-degree sections of the zodiac that constitute the astrological signs. (There are also many “minor” aspects that some astrologers use, but, among these minor aspects, I personally focus only on the 30- and 150-degree angles, called the
semisextile
 and
quincunx
 aspects, respectively. However, because these angles are relatively minor in the intensity of their energetic expression in relationships as compared to the other aspects described below, I do not discuss them in this book.)

Figure 17.2

Conjunction (0 degrees)

The conjunction occurs when two planets, or a planet and the Ascendant (or another major angle of the birth chart such as the Midheaven), fall within a few degrees of each other in the zodiac. I normally use a maximum distance of 6 degrees between them, although if the Sun, Moon, or Ascendant is involved, I will stretch the “orb” allowed to 7 degrees. However, in all cases when interpreting aspects, the more exact (or “closer”) the angle is, the more powerful the aspect and the more pronounced its manifestation in the person’s life. (For example, if in a chart comparison one person’s Saturn is 12 degrees Cancer, the conjunction will be most powerful if the other person’s planet is 11, 12, or 13 degrees Cancer. Still powerful, but a bit less so, will be the interaction if the second person’s planet is, say, 9–10 degrees or 14–15 degrees Cancer, and so on.) The conjunction is the most powerful aspect; it is a celestial signal of the most intense merger and interaction

of the two energies involved. Usually a dynamic focus of the relationship that is immediately noticed by both people involved, how the conjunction is experienced depends entirely on the planets and signs involved, and also on how they interact with other factors in the people’s charts
.

Examples:
 John has Venus in 15 Scorpio, and Martha has Neptune between 9 Scorpio and 21 Scorpio. Mary has Mars in 9 Sagittarius, and Jim has the Moon between 3 Sagittarius and 15 Sagittarius.

Figure 17.3

Opposition (180 degrees)

This aspect occurs when two factors are opposite in the zodiac, usually in opposite signs. Almost always quite stimulating—and sometimes overstimulating—the opposition is the second most powerful type of aspect and it often simultaneously combines attraction and conflict. Opposites may attract, but they also often have radically different approaches to things. In the zodiac, the opposite signs have a great deal in common and are quite similar in many ways; yet—while often being complementary—they are also sometimes irritated or repelled by one another. A very important energy interaction in analyzing all kinds of relationships, and an aspect that is quite complex for beginners to understand, some of the oppositions do indeed represent opposed tendencies that are too rigid or conflicting to be reconcilable. But other opposition aspects (especially those involving the Ascendant, the Moon, or Venus) usually indicate compatible qualities that produce a pleasing sense of equal give-and-take that feels like wholeness, completion, or a balanced rapport has been experienced.

Examples:
 Danika has 22 Cancer Ascendant, and Bill has Venus between 15 Capricorn and 29 Capricorn (a 7-degree orb is allowed because the Ascendant is involved). Daniel has
the Sun in 23 Leo, and Betty has Jupiter between 17 Aquarius and 29 Aquarius.

Figure 17.4

Square (90 degrees)

Although the square and trine aspects are equally significant in chart comparisons and next in strength after the conjunction and opposition, I list the square first because most people tend to be sharply aware of the more obvious conflicts, challenges, or stress-points indicated by square aspects, whereas they often take for granted the ease of energy flow and immediate mutual understanding symbolized by the trine. Usually involving planets that are not in harmonious elements (Water/Air, Earth/Fire, Fire/Water, Air/Earth), the square aspects often require the two people to make a significant effort to adjust to each other’s divergent energies and approaches in those areas of life indicated by the planets involved. Some of these aspects will manifest merely as a difference in attitudes that simply has to be accepted, as irritation, or as a sort of “static” in the interchange of energy. But some squares are much more difficult to adjust to and may be experienced as obstructive, violently conflicting, or oppressively inhibiting. Sometimes, in the areas of life that the planets symbolize, the people are really deeply at cross-purposes, although they may not be consciously aware of it. In short, some square aspects reveal profoundly irreconcilable differences, whereas others show challenges that can enable the people involved to learn about completely different points of view
.

Examples:
 Tristan has Mars at 7 degrees Capricorn, and Silvia has Mars between 1 Libra and 13 Libra. Terry has the Moon in 18 Pisces, and Sam has Uranus between 11 and 25 Gemini (a 7-degree orb is allowed because the Moon is involved).

Figure 17.5

Trine (120 degrees)

There is usually an easy energy flow, a constructive combining of talents, and a natural mutual understanding in the areas indicated by the planets involved in this interchange. These energies flow together harmoniously, for they usually involve signs of the same element. Hence, there is often a spontaneous, enjoyable rapport between the two people. The trine aspects, if incorporating at least one person’s Ascendant, Sun, Moon, or other “personal planets,” can go a long way to ameliorate conflicts shown elsewhere in the chart comparison. (This is particularly true if a comparison reveals many trine aspects between the people’s planets.) These aspects often symbolize an effortless combining of energies toward any common goal. Mutual understanding is easily achieved in the areas symbolized by those planets, and many of these aspects are not only energizing but also mutually encouraging for both people. They often sense a pleasant, strengthening harmony in their mutual energy field, and hence joint enterprises are unusually easy, spontaneously cooperative, and enjoyable
.

Examples:
 Herbert has Mercury in 20 Gemini, and Kim has Jupiter between 14 Libra and 26 Libra. Helen has Mars in 7 Taurus, and Ken has Uranus between 1 and 13 degrees Virgo.

Figure 17.6

Sextile (60 degrees)

Less immediately obvious in an energetic way than the previously discussed aspects, the energy exchange denoted by this angle is nevertheless a strong compatibility component in any relationship. The sextile often forms what I might call a background energy exchange that is gently encouraging and supportive, mainly through new learning, openness to new people and ideas, and an ongoing,
constructive give-and-take. Most sextiles involve planets in harmonious elements (Earth and Water, or Fire and Air) and may be considered pleasantly stimulating and constructive. They symbolize a relatively easy blending of the energies represented by the planets involved
.

Examples:
 Nate has Venus in 9 Pisces, and Ann has Saturn between 3 Taurus and 15 Taurus. Nancy has the Ascendant in 8 Aries, and Adam has Jupiter between 1 and 15 degrees Aquarius (a 7-degree orb is allowed because the Ascendant is involved).

Other Aspects:
 For purposes of chart comparisons, in the vast majority of cases, the major aspects alone—in addition to the other factors explained in this book and in the other books I recommend—will yield far more data than you can ever incorporate into your assessment of a given relationship. Hence, the analysis of further minor aspects will contribute little toward a truly rewarding and incisive evaluation. This is because we are focusing here on the
dominant energy dynamics between two people
, and the
major
 aspects symbolize, measure, and illuminate the primary, most intense energy interactions.

Guidelines for Distinguishing and Understanding the Most Important Aspects in a Chart Comparison

Guideline A: Focus on the closest aspects
. The most exact aspects reveal the most dynamic and intense exchange of energy between people. These are key areas where the two people’s energy fields interact powerfully, showing whether their specific attunements merge together well or conflict—or a combination of both. Such close aspects deserve the most attention in interpreting a chart comparison. (See #3 in the next section, “Other Key Factors in Interpreting Aspects in
Chart Comparisons,” for more details and the rationale underlying this guideline.)

Guideline B: Each aspect must be evaluated according to the nature of the planets (and signs) involved
. In other words, the particular type of major aspect is secondary to the specific energies that are interacting. For example, Jupiter conjunct the Sun, and Jupiter square the Sun, will both manifest as exuberantly encouraging lots of optimistic self-expression, although the square will admittedly often be accompanied by certain excessive or extreme behavior. Another example would be if one person’s Moon closely aspects another’s Saturn. In this case, whether Moon is square Saturn or trine Saturn or conjunct Saturn, there would be
some
 strong similarities in the people’s experience of such a relationship. However, the square or opposition might be felt as more oppressive or unsettling, whereas the trine or sextile would usually be felt as constructively stabilizing, even if somewhat restrictive.

The reader should refer to
Appendix A
 for a detailed list of how the planetary energies are expressed, and keep in mind that it is a
constant, dynamic interaction of life energies
 that is represented by these astrological factors in a relationship and thus in a chart comparison. But just for a quick preview of this dimension of astrological science, the following phrases are a concise guide to how each planet “affects” the other planet in a close aspect in a chart comparison.

Sun
 illuminates, vitalizes, encourages, seeks identity with

Moon
 responds to, emotionally resonates with, receives, seeks security in

Mercury
 activates perceptions, new concepts, communication, connections and exchange of ideas

Venus
 harmonizes, stimulates affections and sense of pleasure, energizes social/aesthetic/romantic responses

Mars
 energizes, impatiently initiates, urges decisions, aggressively pushes toward action

Jupiter
 expands, urges to improve or be optimistic and confident

Saturn
 delays, restricts, concentrates, forces realistic assessment and sense of responsibility

Uranus
 speeds up rhythm of life, electrifies perceptions, revolutionizes attitudes, disrupts established patterns of thought and action

Neptune
 inspires or undermines, idealizes, romanticizes, always sensitizes

Pluto
 thoroughly transforms, challenges to completely eliminate the old

Guideline C: Aspects with the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant are always important
. This was explained to some extent in
Chapter 7
 on the Moon and in the Ascendant section of
Chapter 6
, but please also study the various ways these energies can be expressed that I define in
Appendix A
. Considerable factual evidence indicates that harmonious Moon aspects in a chart comparison are especially important for long-term domestic harmony. For example, the pioneering psychologist Dr. C. G. Jung did a statistical study of the chart comparisons of almost five hundred marriage partners, which he published in his famous work,
Synchronicity: An Acausal Connecting Principle
. The final results showed at least one person’s Moon involved in three of the four interchart aspects occurring most often in this batch of marriage charts. Those chart comparison aspects were: Moon conjunct Moon, Moon conjunct Sun, and Moon opposite Sun. The fourth most common interchart aspect in Jung’s study was Mars conjunct Mars, an apt symbol for mutual sexual understanding and physical energy
attunement, as well as clearly portraying unified goals and a harmonious
modus operandi
 in everyday life.

Guideline D: Close aspects with the other “personal planets”—Mercury, Venus, and Mars—should always be evaluated
. If an aspect, or an aspect pattern that involves two or more planets, does not include any of the “personal planets” (Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, and Mars) or the Ascendant, it may not be important for that particular relationship. There are exceptions to this guideline, but it is generally true.

Guideline E: Any planet opposite the Ascendant will be conjunct the Descendant, a particularly important relationship factor
. (The Descendant is simply the point in the zodiac exactly opposite the Ascendant. It thus is always in the sign opposite the Ascendant’s sign, and—although far less important than the Ascendant in assessing
individuals’
 energy attunement and psychology—it comes into play strongly in all chart comparisons.) In other words, a conjunction of
any
 planet on either side of the Descendant point will always have a notable energy impact on the overall vibration and quality of that relationship.

Guideline F: Ultimately, a clear perception of the whole comparison and its major themes is the goal
. This type of comprehensive perception comes only with time and considerable experience, and it is as much a rare natural ability and an art as it is a science. However, once this art/science is mastered, it then can allow one to assess the entire interactive pattern of the two people’s energy fields.

HART
 C
OMPARISONS

1.
“Double Whammies”: Intense Interpersonal Energy Interactions:
 An extremely important factor in chart comparisons, one that many years ago I termed the “double whammy,” occurs when one discovers
a repetition of a similar energy interaction between the two people (or two charts). The clearest and most powerful example of this “double whammy” is when one finds that there are two close aspects between the same two planets in the comparison. An example would be: Jane’s Venus is in a square aspect to Jim’s Uranus; and Jim’s Venus is conjunct Jane’s Uranus. Another example would be: Henry’s Mars is trine Henrietta’s Moon; and Henrietta’s Mars is opposite Henry’s Moon. There can be many variations on this theme, but by using only quite close aspects (within 6 degrees of exactitude), the beginner doing a comparison of charts can quickly focus on a central energy dynamic of any relationship. (It is important to be aware that any two such close aspects, whether the aspects are trine, conjunction, or any other, should draw one’s attention to the issues symbolized. Much depends on the planets involved, but—in general—if at least one of the aspects is a trine or sextile, one should not jump to any negative conclusion. In addition, many of the opposition aspects are
complementary
 and thus compatible and stimulating in a relationship/chart comparison.)

2.
The Preponderance of the Same Message or Theme Symbolized:
 Arriving at an assessment of the dominant messages (or themes) in a comparison will take some time and a systematic listing of the charts’ many factors and interchanges. At first, this can be a rather demanding study. But, in addition to the double whammy occurrences mentioned in the previous paragraph, one should look for a preponderance of challenging or harmonious aspects (revealing a tone of friction or easy flow in the people’s energies), or for a substantial number of indications that symbolize a similar message or tell the same story. This is far too complicated a subject to explore in detail in this book, but—after some detailed study in astrology and comparisons—one should not hesitate to acknowledge one’s own spontaneous intuitive insights that
encompass many factors
 in the charts at hand that produce a coherent “picture.”

Similarities of all kinds should be noted when one is analyzing compatibility, and
two of the most fundamental areas that often show natural rapport and mutual understanding
 are: some emphasis on the same element(s) in each chart, and planets in the same signs of the zodiac that are also energized in the other person’s chart.

3.
The Importance of Close Aspects:
 This fact is important not only for beginners starting to use astrology, but also for people more experienced in astrological traditions and methods. And it is important not only in chart comparisons, but
even more so for accurately assessing the dynamics of individual charts
. In order to have
reliable
 information based on the measurements between planets, if the range of degrees allowed for an aspect (what is called the “orb” in traditional astrology or the “arc” of the circle when measuring this range of possible placements) is not tightly restricted, the measurements quickly dissolve into meaninglessness because of the laws of probability. Going into the details of the mathematics of this issue is far beyond the scope of this book, but please note the following:

If one restricts the allowable orb to 6 degrees of exactitude for a given aspect, that results in a 12-degree arc of the circle of the zodiac being within that allowed angle, or a 1 in 30 (3.3 percent) chance of this aspect being relevant.

If one expands the orb to 7.5 degrees of exactitude allowed, that results in a 15-degree arc of the zodiac’s circle, or a 1 in 24 (4.1 percent) chance of that aspect being relevant.

This all seems fine when considering one specific aspect (for example, a conjunction or square), until one takes into consideration that there are many major aspects. If one adds together all the probabilities of a major aspect between any two planets, one quickly discovers this fact: the greater the orb allowed in measuring aspects, the less precise
the information obtained and the more rapidly the energy interchange symbolized dissolves into meaninglessness.

In short, astrology is not just a symbolic art that people can endlessly use for their projections and intuitions, without clear definitions or reliable laws. The mathematical, astronomical precision of astrology is what makes it so reliable, if one keeps to reasonable guidelines. Neglecting that dimension of astrology, as so many do, leads not only to inaccurate judgments and interpretations, but also to discrediting a profoundly promising and productive science.

4.
Religious Beliefs, Spiritual Orientations, and Ideals:
 One thing rarely seen in astrology books about compatibility is the acknowledgment that religious/spiritual beliefs and orientations (whether conscious or assumed) are crucial in most long-term close relationships. Included in this assessment would also be the people’s general tolerance for others, their social and racial prejudices, their general values and overall ideals, and—in short—the total outlook on life. The strength and placement of Jupiter in each individual’s chart is especially pertinent to assessing such an overall outlook on life, but—in reality—the attitudes represented and the “tone” of each birth chart have to be looked at since so many factors are involved. These issues may seem minor when people are young, perhaps freshly deprived of deeper personal values by the intellectual game-playing of the educational system (especially at the college level), and without much real-life experience; but these issues will resurface eventually if the people begin to search for the meaning of life. For example, as a pioneer in reexamining ancient “esoteric” traditions, Manly P. Hall, wrote, “A mystic married to a materialistic, matter-of-fact, or atheistic partner is at a singular disadvantage. If we have faith in God and in the reality of universal principles, we will eventually clash with a mate who admits no power superior to himself” (INC, p. 7).

5.
Attitudes Toward Money, Saving, and Risk-Taking:
 Another factor rarely acknowledged in the majority of astrology publications about relationships is how important the individuals’ attitudes toward money and expenditures are—thus showing what they really
value
 as well as revealing keys to their preferred style of living. Determining this is important for not just close living-together relationships, but also for many types of business relationships, and even for interactions between relatives and roommates. Understanding these attitudes and related emotionally charged conflicts is clarified in part by looking at the placements of Venus; but there are also numerous other factors outside the scope of this book, such as the second and eighth houses, and whether Jupiter (faith in the future) or Saturn (caution about future prospects) or the signs they rule are dominant. In short, if one person is instinctively conservative, cautious, or perhaps invariably cheap, and the other is generally extravagant, careless, and irresponsible (even though he may be seen by others as “generous”), significant problems most likely loom in the future of that relationship. Issues such as how much faith in the future, practical common sense, self-confidence, etc., the individuals have all come into play here—often influenced not only by individual personality but also by one’s social background, family history, and other factors.

As Manly P. Hall wrote in his insightful work
Incompatibility
,

In most cases, one of the marriage partners is more financially conscious than the other; that is, one is inclined to save more or to spend more.… As Benjamin Franklin pointed out, there is nothing more demoralizing than debt. In a strange way, however, there is nothing more impoverishing than a life built around a dollar.… Worry over money is a real, though often concealed, cause of incompatibility. It not only disturbs living, but contributes to poor health and often ends in serious emotional or even physical sickness. (INC, pp. 10–11)

In summary, a detailed explanation of all possible aspects in chart comparisons is beyond the scope of this book, or perhaps of any book. In any case, it is far better to learn the basic principles explained in this book, and then to experiment with and explore the range of their meanings. If you find the results are illuminating and worth deeper exploration, you can then study the meanings and methods explained in some of my other books, such as
Relationships & Life Cycles
 and
Chart Interpretation Handbook
. If doors continue to open leading to a broader understanding of oneself and one’s relationships, the next step could be to obtain the books by Lois Sargent (for interpretations of specific aspects in comparisons) and Ronald Davison—all listed in the bibliography in
Appendix F
.

This chapter provides only brief guidelines, directed to those who know little astrology. The ability to blend these energies in an accurate way will develop over time, along with the amount of study and experience one acquires. It should also be emphasized that a deep understanding will develop much more quickly from participating in one-on-one personal
dialogues
 with a variety of people than from mere book-learning, doing one-sided speculative interpretations that appear to others simply as an entertaining guessing-game, or doing “readings” for people one has never seen and from whom one will never receive any feedback.

Person-to-Person Astrology: Energy Factors in Love, Sex and Compatibility

18

Using Astrology and Chart Comparisons Wisely and Realistically

The greatest happiness of life is the conviction that we are loved for ourselves, or rather loved in spite of ourselves.

—Victor Hugo

Many people, when they realize the quality of insight and accuracy of psychological information that astrology can contribute to their lives, get quite excited and often so completely carried away with its possibilities that they begin to attribute to astrology even more precision and scope of application than it can realistically provide. The fact that it is an awe-inspiring field of study and system for human understanding can lead people to adopt it as a semireligious system for addressing all the major issues of life, rather than simply as a remarkable science of energy and a reliable tool for psychological insight. Overestimating astrology’s precision and predictive capacity is just as misleading as the common prejudice of
underestimating
 its accuracy and practical value. In truth, although good research and clearer explanations of essential principles are always needed (as is the case in any science), the wise, realistic, and accurate use of astrology depends more on the level of consciousness of the practitioners of this complex art/science than on further research into planetary correlations with human behavior and personality.

In fact, I like to emphasize that the proper use of astrological information and techniques is to further one’s depth of understanding in order to
live more consciously
. Astrology should not be used to evade personal responsibility or to foster illusions of perfect solutions or ultimate bliss. Rather, astrology provides us with an extremely useful and reliable tool for understanding human nature as well as for tuning in on the essence of specific interpersonal dynamics. It allows us to gain perspective, objectivity, and insight; and ideally, anyone using it should retain a sense of proportion in his or her assessments of the meanings it reveals. Although it can often have a healing and transformational impact on individuals when used intelligently, astrology is not a therapy per se. We still need to take the next steps—to act on the insights, to do the work to improve our lives and relationships—but with a broader awareness than before, due to the deeper understanding the application of astrological principles gives us.

Likewise in the field of relationships, when we consider our own individual birth chart or compare our chart with another person’s, the primary goal should be to gain objectivity—not to run away from difficulties but rather to deal with them with full awareness and honesty. The level of awareness of the two people in any relationship is always the most crucial factor, and the way they choose to deal with the inevitable conflicting tendencies or personality patterns is only predictable to the extent that the individuals function unconsciously. A knowledge of astrological principles reveals the laws of energy and polarity active in all of life—yin/yang, positive/negative, male/female, Sun/Moon, Venus/Mars, and so on. And a keener awareness of the implications of the polarities and the paradoxes of life allow one to step back and see the whole picture and the whole person. For example, no human being will be completely compatible or acceptable to any one of us on all levels of personality, conduct, and energy exchange. With every attractive quality that charms us about someone, we also encounter—at least eventually—the accompanying “other side of the coin,” which
appears more difficult, unpleasant, bewildering, or incomprehensible. As Manly Hall wrote,

The kindly man may be too silent; the hardworking husband may lack ambition; the happy and optimistic wife may appear impractical; the devoted and sincere woman may not be glamorous. We must learn to appreciate those qualities which have greatest worth and accept the rest, for the simple reason that others must accept us on the same basis. (INC)

Hall’s observations are closely related to the tendency of human beings to
idealize
 potential partners, as I discussed in
Chapter 3
, making it difficult to build a genuine relationship with a complex but inevitably flawed partner. And the importance of this practical fact is why I include Victor Hugo’s quotation at the beginning of this chapter. After all, we are all in the same boat—imperfect beings with limited capacity to love, and yet often desperately wanting to be loved and accepted as we are.

The importance of acknowledging the
complexity
 of individuals’ personalities, and thus the consequent complexity of all relationships between them, leads me to another important bit of advice. I strongly recommend that the reader
not
 use a computerized chart interpretation service to evaluate a relationship. The complexity of comparing and combining the many factors in two charts immeasurably magnifies the difficulty of designing a reliable interpretation program. It is fine if one wants to have the two
individual
 charts calculated by any accurate service (see
Appendix B
), but trying to get a real appreciation of the complexities of any relationship is undermined by the simplistic, black-and-white sort of rigid analyses and pat advice that one usually finds in such products. The impression one gets of any relationship from such a computer-generated assessment is often deterministic, unrealistic, and usually wrong. In addition, you will short-circuit the discovery process of going through the details of a particular comparison,
which allows you to learn not only about yourselves but also about astrology in general and how the life energies it diagrams flow and interact. In addition, the reader should be aware that the vast majority of websites that purport to provide such “readings,” relationship analyses, and other services are created by people who have little or no knowledge of real astrology and are focusing simply on profiting from your trust.

A C
OMMENT ON THE
 R
ESEARCH FOR
 T
HIS
 B
OOK

As Abraham Lincoln wrote to Horace Greeley in 1862, “I shall try to correct errors when shown to be errors, and I shall adopt new views so fast as they shall appear to be true views.” Any written work attempting to present astrology intelligently, and with some awareness of its complexity, has to keep focused on essential principles. Otherwise one can get lost in endless detail or wind up with 1,200 pages of material that ultimately strikes the reader as verbal mush. I have always tried to present astrology in a way that respects the intelligence of the reader and assumes that he or she will be able to apply the guidelines and principles I have presented in relation to their own lives. In this day and age, any statement about relationships and sexuality is likely to be controversial and touch off emotional reactions for some people. Because I view astrology as at least a potential science, I have tried to focus on essential energetic and relationship principles that most people can identify with. I assume that a wide variety of people will be able to understand and apply these core energetic and cosmic principles in their own lives.

Over the years of research for this book, I have had no control over who would participate in the questionnaires and little influence over interviews (let alone what specific opinions, views, and observations would be expressed). As some readers have no doubt noticed, there seems to be a huge and varied amount of information on some
Venus, Mars, or Moon placements, whereas a smaller amount and fewer quotes appear for other planetary positions. Some signs are just simpler to explain and describe than others, and I have in general tried to eliminate most quotes that merely duplicated others. (For example, I could easily have listed perhaps two dozen interview and questionnaire excerpts that all said essentially that Mars in Virgo was extraordinarily hardworking and focused on details, but I have usually deleted such endless redundant quotations.) In addition, I have spent years searching for those rare, strikingly original or insightful examples that express in a concise way each placement’s most essential characteristics and qualities.

Obviously, people attuned to a particularly communicative or verbal approach to all the dimensions of relationship are especially motivated to volunteer information for the kinds of research I have done, whereas more retentive, secretive, or nonverbal types of personalities will not participate nearly so often. I did try to compensate for this, not only by quoting reliable authors’ views, but also by asking quite a number of people for interviews who had not volunteered. Most were quite cooperative, and some were surprisingly and illuminatingly honest, although with a few it was like “pulling teeth.” Nevertheless, I think the final product is a fair overview of the entire range and multiplicity of energy attunements that astrological symbolism encompasses. Although I do not maintain an Internet site or blog, I will certainly consider readers’ proposed amendments, additions to, and clarifications of this material, especially those that are original and discriminatingly perceptive, should there be future editions of this book—which of course I cannot guarantee, nor can I promise any reply to such communications. (Any such contributions should be sent to S. Arroyo, c/o CRCS, P.O. Box 1460, Sebastopol, CA 95473—USA or e-mailed to:
astroconf@sbcglobal.net
.)

Approaching a Chart Comparison

The tendency for people today is to want immediate results in many areas of life, without their investing time and effort in sufficient study, research, or preparation. Hence, people easily get lost in details, statistics, and mind games, while losing sight of the principles, sense of purpose, and awareness of the
whole
 that provide meaning and a real perspective. This modern trend is as true in astrology as it is in medicine, environmental science, agriculture, and other fields.

Especially in evaluating relationships and compatibility, it is essential that one not make any judgment too hastily. Each relationship is a unique situation involving unique people who have values, assumptions, and goals that will not be apparent unless you specifically inquire. The question of “What are they looking for in this relationship?” is crucial. Moreover, one must be aware that the central issues, desires, and goals that individuals and couples focus on tend to change with age.

A very insightful book by the talented astrologer Marc Robertson,
Sex, Mind and Habit Compatibility
, points out these key factors to be aware of when evaluating relationships:

The questions “What do you expect from it?” and “What do you want from it?” have to be considered when analyzing any relationship, as well as “Are you seeking excitement or ease?” And he observes that chart comparisons can be valuable in assessing all sorts of relationships, including parent-child interactions, people involved in business projects, and so on. Obviously, you will not emphasize the same factors when considering a housemate or an academic thesis advisor as when you are investing money with someone or drawn toward deep intimacy with another.

He also writes, “The person who may be most irresistible to you may also be the one who is most impossible to live with on a comfortable,
easy basis” (SMH). There can be many astrological reasons for this phenomenon, and the specifics of each chart comparison have to be evaluated to illuminate the energy dynamics in play.

In addition, Robertson cautions, “Every relationship that’s going to be meaningful is not necessarily going to be pleasant” (SMH). This observation is similar to a few of the quotes I cited earlier in this book: deep relationships and love go their own way, often ignoring our strategies and expectations. For example, two people whose attunements to the four elements are not very compatible may still learn a great deal from their relationship, even though it may not be easy—so long as both have sufficient energy harmony with each other to communicate on some levels, supported by a good amount of caring and motivation. Over time, the two people can then actually learn to accept and appreciate each other’s attitudes and energies in an authentic way, without always feeling the need to try to change or oppose the other’s tendencies.

F
URTHER
 G
UIDELINES FOR
 I
NTERPRETING
 C
HART
 C
OMPARISONS
: A C
ONCISE
, U
SER
-F
RIENDLY
 C
HECKLIST

One of the crucial assumptions employed when comparing two birth charts should be that enabling people to know themselves and each other better will always be helpful in some ways, even if the truth is sometimes difficult to face. Assessing the real energy
experience
 of each person is one particularly useful approach for facilitating discussion of their mutual interaction, through which they can arrive at a deeper knowledge that transcends abstract concepts. That quest for knowledge of self and other should underlie all person-to-person astrological analysis and dialogue.

In addition, acknowledging each individual’s psychological bias (rooted in their energy attunement) is essential for doing a realistic and illuminating chart comparison. For example,

Those with a lot of emphasis in their charts on Venus, Libra, or the seventh house look to others for energy stimulation and support much more than most people. Energy compatibility is thus far more important to them than to most other folks since they are so dependent on others for feedback and fulfillment. Relationships of all kinds are crucial to their happiness in life and can energize them or deplete them according to how harmoniously they are going. Furthermore, their key relationship is of paramount importance in determining their mood, energy level, and sense of well-being.

Those especially attuned to a need for communication—both intellectually (Air) and emotionally (Water)—tend to be more fussy and hard to please in relationships. They have more demands, in other words, that mere appearance or style or behavioral ritual will not satisfy.

People with a lot of attunement to the Earth element usually take
limitation
 for granted in daily life. Hence, their attitude toward relationships can be: “Well, this works OK and this is nice. That’s good enough.” More idealistic or romantic people, on the other hand, often want so much to have a total experience of compatibility or merger that they create images in their minds that may turn out to be self-deceptions, ultimately leading to disappointment.

People strongly attuned to the Fire element can have a tendency toward grandiose images and huge, idealistic dreams in relationships, but—fortunately for them—they can often be relatively content moving along together dynamically, keeping busy on endless projects, and convincing themselves and each other that they are happy. (Fire signs tend to be positive thinkers!)

(See
Chapter 15
, “The Elements in Chart Comparison,” in my book
Astrology, Psychology and the Four Elements
 for a more extensive treatment of this topic.)

Although we cannot explore all the dimensions of advanced astrology and chart comparisons in this introductory book, a few factors should be noted briefly to alert the reader to various important energy interactions—as a sort of checklist—as he or she experiments with comparing birth charts:

As mentioned earlier in this volume, harmonious attunements and stimulating interactions (such as an opposition aspect) between the individuals’ Suns and Moons are always important and desirable; and they are often found in marriage partners, as C. G. Jung’s statistical studies showed. A number of aspects involving one person’s Sun and another person’s planets can reinforce one’s vitality and sense of identity, as well as encourage one’s confidence. Many kinds of harmonious aspects involving the Moon, as discussed in
Chapter 7
, can aid in living together easily and in achieving a state of domestic tranquility that sustains good health.

As discussed extensively in previous chapters, interactions between Mars and Venus are important for sexual and emotional harmony and stimulation, but they are by no means the only indicators of erotic energy exchange. One person’s Moon harmoniously attuned to the other’s Venus or Mars can also indicate physical and emotional comfort and acceptance. Neptune aspects of many kinds add a romantic aura to the relationship and a sense of oneness, fantasy, and even transcendence and magical merging. Uranus aspects with the other’s Venus or Mars can add a jolt of excitement and adventure, and Pluto’s aspects can bring into play a powerful impersonal sexual attraction, often very lustful and possessive and sometimes even ruthless.

Aspects between one person’s Ascendant and the other person’s Sun, Moon, or planets are not emphasized nearly enough in most books on this subject. Assuming one has accurate birth charts based on precise birth times, one should always check to see if one’s Ascendant
aligns by a fairly close aspect—
or at least by
 sign—with planets in the other’s chart. These are usually very important links and can signify especially meaningful and often long-term relationships. The majority of the conjunctions, some oppositions, and many harmonious aspects involving one of the Ascendants are important indicators of particularly intense close relationships, and often of compatibility.

Close interactions between Mercury in one chart and Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, Venus, Mars, Moon, Uranus, or the Ascendant in the other can stimulate especially interesting verbal communication and mutual understanding of each other’s way of thinking and each person’s attitudes.

A
close
 aspect between Jupiter in one chart and any number of planets or the Ascendant in the other’s chart should never be underestimated, for such an aspect (that is, a close angular and energetic interaction between the planets) has the power to change the tone of the entire relationship by smoothing over many small difficulties. Be especially alert to the importance of the following aspects in a comparison, whether the relationship is “romantic” or business-related: Jupiter conjunct or opposite the Ascendant; Jupiter in any number of close aspects with the Sun or Moon; Jupiter harmonizing with the other person’s Saturn; or Jupiter being harmoniously attuned to the other person’s Venus, Mars, or Mercury.

Where Do I Go from Here?

There are a myriad of additional factors one can use and analyze when doing a comprehensive chart comparison, and the books marked *** in
Appendix F
 are especially recommended. Ronald Davison’s books are classics, and I particularly encourage the reader to study Lois H. Sargent’s
How to Handle Your Human Relations
 for a remarkably accurate,
detailed assessment of the various aspects between any two planets in individuals’ charts (sometimes also called “interchart aspects”). Although it is a very small booklet and not easy to find, it is well worth the effort. Among my own books,
Relationships & Life Cycles
 has the most material on chart comparisons, including a step-by-step system for
thoroughly
 comparing charts and also an entire chapter on the interpretation of the “houses” of the birth chart in comparisons.
Astrology, Psychology and the Four Elements
 has a great deal of material on understanding energy flow between people; and
Astrology, Karma & Transformation
 includes a chapter that discusses relationships and chart comparisons from a spiritual and karmic point of view.

The study of many works like those just mentioned is required if one wants to attain a high level of proficiency in doing reliable, perceptive chart comparisons. But even serious, systematic study of methods and techniques is just a foundation. One then needs experience and some humility to remain open to the truth of each situation. The rules or laws of astrology work with and refer to archetypal and energy levels of life. Hence, although the rules of interpretation and guidelines for assessing compatibility in any particular case will be obviously borne out in most relationships, one should not be simplistic or doctrinaire. The amalgam of innumerable astrological factors and interactions in any comparison leads to a striking picture of bewildering complexity—especially for beginners. But this merely reflects the complexity of human beings and their relationships. One should not be discouraged. This very complexity is the reason why one will occasionally find two people whose long-term relationship is very successful, although it seems to break many of the rules about compatibility. In these cases, one has to look at the whole picture and at each individual’s unique needs and attunements. These people, for example, might actually thrive on periodic conflict or require considerable psychic distance from their partner.

In any case, the humility I mentioned above should keep one open
to the reality of each situation and each comparison and help one to avoid posing as a “know-it-all,” which historically has earned many astrology practitioners the label of charlatan or fortune-teller. Astrology implies a view of life that is radically different from that of materialistic science, which sees the universe as random and meaningless. Students or practitioners of astrology assume, or should assume, that the cosmos knows far more than we humans do with our limited intellects. Hence, we are always discovering more, and we can always be humble and grateful when we do indeed learn more about the mysterious, awe-inspiring patterns of the universe.
